# Decisions

Every choice that isn't dictated by `docs/SPEC-M1.md`, with its reason. Newest stage at the bottom of each section.

## How each design pillar is enforced in code

### 1. AI is part of the engine

- **One registry, in the core.** `evani_core::params::ParamRegistry`, `curve`, `expr`, `formulas`, `slots`, `tiers` and the budget calculator (`effects::plan_budget`) all live in `evani-core`, which every game ships. Shipped games read baked values from the same registry the live API edits, so there is nothing to translate at bake time. `evani-live` is only a door to that registry and is never in a shipped build.
- **Registering is the only way to have a tunable.** `PluginContext` is the single registration surface for kits, looks and modules. It stamps a dotted owner on every param and formula (`kit.example`, `look.basic`, `fx`, `render`, ...), so the schema always says where a value came from.
- **Tests require each part to register its tunables.** Every module, kit and look in the workspace registers its tunables, and a test checks it:
  - render: `render.exposure`, `render.clear_color`
  - fx: `fx.<n>.enabled`, `fx.<n>.value_weight` and each effect's own params
  - physics: `physics.gravity`, `physics.substeps`
  - net: `net.send_rate_hz`, `net.interp_delay_ms`
  - xr: `xr.render_scale`, `xr.reference_space`
  - live: `live.revert.*`
  - core: `core.audio.*`, `core.overlay.interval_ms`
  - kit: `kit.example.*` and the formula `kit.example.boost_scale`
  - look: `look.basic.*` and the tonemap slot
- **Effect weights are params.** The budget's degradation order reads `fx.<n>.value_weight`, so an AI can retune it live.
- **Uniform-feeding params are marked.** Params that feed uniforms are flagged `gpu`, and `revision()` / `changed_since` give dirty tracking for upload on the next frame.
- **Every param has a bake target.** `ParamDef::bake` (`BakeTarget`) records where `bake.export` writes each value: look, kit, tier, policy, or none for `live.*`.
- **The sandbox panel uses the live API's own levers.** It writes the same things the live API writes (the active tier and `fx.<n>.enabled`), directly, so a person and an AI drive identical state.

### 2. WebGPU-first, no legacy

- **wgpu is pinned to WebGPU only.** It is `=30.0.1` with `default-features = false` and features `webgpu`, `wgsl` and `std`: no `webgl`, no GLES, no Vulkan, Metal or DX backends.
- **No GPU backend outside the browser.** wgpu is a dependency of `evani-render` only on `cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")`, so native builds (tests, tools) link no GPU backend at all.
- **The adapter must present to the canvas.** `request_adapter` gets `compatible_surface`; TIMESTAMP_QUERY is requested only if the adapter has it (otherwise the overlay says `gpu n/a (timestamp-query unsupported)`).
- **The message is the only fallback.** `evani_render::webgpu_available()` checks `navigator.gpu`. If it is missing, `RenderModule` shows the full-screen message ("This game needs WebGPU") through `evani_core::web::show_message`. No other code path exists.
- **A non-secure page gets its own message.** Browsers hide `navigator.gpu` on plain http (not localhost), so `evani_render::no_webgpu_reason(secure, has_gpu, has_adapter)` picks "This page needs a secure address" instead of the "needs WebGPU" text. `isSecureContext` is read through the existing js-sys; no new dependency or web-sys feature.
- **The WebGPU bindings need one cfg.** `.cargo/config.toml` passes `--cfg=web_sys_unstable_apis` for wasm32, because that cfg gates web-sys's `Gpu*` bindings.

### 3. Modularity you can prove

- **Optional modules are optional dependencies.** Each one is an optional dependency of the game crate behind a feature (`fx`, `bloom`, `vignette`, `physics-simple`, `physics-rigid`, `net`, `xr`, `live`). A module that isn't listed is not compiled.
- **Each effect is its own feature.** Every effect is a Cargo feature of `evani-fx`, and its WGSL is `include_str!`ed only inside `#[cfg(feature = ...)]`. A build without bloom has no bloom shader bytes.
- **xtask always starts from nothing.** `cargo xtask build <app>` maps `evani.toml` to features and always passes `--no-default-features`, so the build contains exactly what the manifest lists. It rejects features the crate lacks, and kits or looks the crate doesn't depend on.
- **Size per module is measured, not estimated.** xtask rebuilds the app once with each listed module off and once with everything optional off ("base"). It prints raw and gzip deltas and writes `target/evani-sizes/<app>-<variant>.json`. Diffs don't sum exactly, because LTO and wasm-opt share code between modules; we chose measurement over a symbol-size scan because it is exactly what a player downloads.
- **Markers show what a build contains.** Every module has a stable id `evani.module.<name>`, and every WGSL file starts with `// evani.shader.<name>`. xtask collects the declared names from the sources and looks each one up in the wasm.
  - It looks names up exactly rather than scanning for word boundaries, because the wasm data section packs strings back to back with no separators.
  - If one name is a prefix of a longer declared name, a hit inside the longer name doesn't count.
  - `cargo xtask sizes` prints the bloom, fx and live columns from these markers.
- **Size is part of every build's output.** Each report includes the percentage of Unity's 8 MB web starting point.

### 4. Tiers, budgets, VR and determinism

- **Tier numbers are data, not code.** Tiers are `assets/tiers/{low,medium,high,ultra,vr}.json`, `include_str!`ed into `evani_core::tiers::Tiers::builtin()`. Rust code holds no tier-specific numbers.
- **The budget calculator lives in the core.** `effects::plan_budget` (`EffectWant`, `EffectState`, `BudgetItem`) is pure and unit-tested. `FxModule` runs it on every tier change and every relevant param change, toggles graph passes, writes `BudgetStatus` and `EffectsStatus`, and adds an overlay note when the budget switches something off.
- **VR comfort.** `EffectInfo::vr_comfort_off` forces an effect off on stereo tiers. `effect.toggle` refuses to switch it on there, with `reason: vr_comfort`.
- **Determinism:**
  - a fixed 60 Hz step with an accumulator and a max-steps clamp (`time::FixedStep`);
  - seeded `Pcg32`, checked against the reference vectors;
  - a sparse-set ECS that iterates in dense insertion order and never iterates a hash map;
  - a schedule ordered by `(stage, order, insertion)`.
- **Same seed, same result.** The example kit's test runs two apps with the same seed and asserts identical state.

## Layout (from the spec's target layout)

- **One crate each.** Kits live in `kits/evani-kit-<genre>` and looks in `looks/evani-look-<style>`, each its own crate. The M1 example kit and look are real crates, used by apps/sandbox, so `bake.export` has somewhere to write.
- **Stubs that still count.** `evani-physics` (features `simple`, `rigid`) and `evani-net` are stubs that compile, register params and report in the overlay. `evani-xr` only does feature detection (`navigator.xr`, `XRGPUBinding`, immersive-vr support).
- **Dev-only tools.** `evani-live` and `tools/evani-live-bridge` are dev only (the bridge was built on branch `m1-bridge` and merged into `m1-foundation`). The tools entry is `tools/xtask`, run as `cargo xtask`.
- **No Look Lab.** apps/sandbox has only a minimal panel (tier select, effect checkboxes, budget line) next to the overlay. The Evani.dev tab is the Look Lab.
- **`evani-` package prefix.** Every package name starts with `evani-`, so the commands are `-p evani-sandbox` and `-p evani-xtask` (the brief said `-p sandbox`).
- **XR is a separate, on-demand download (design; M1 ships only the stub).** In M1 `evani-xr` is a build-time module that only feature-detects (`navigator.xr`, `isSessionSupported("immersive-vr")`, `XRGPUBinding`) and shows the result in the overlay; a game without `xr = true` gets none of it. Later stages build it as a second wasm so flat-screen players never download VR code:
  1. **Two artefacts per game.** `cargo xtask build` builds the game's main wasm with only a tiny `xr-detect` part (the feature detection plus the Enter VR button, a few hundred bytes) and a second wasm, `<game>-xr.wasm`, from an `evani-xr` cdylib target holding the stereo view, the XR session loop, controller input and the VR comfort rules. The size report lists it on its own line, never added to the main download.
  2. **Shared state through a narrow ABI, not shared Rust types.** wasm32 has no stable Rust dynamic linking, so the XR module does not link against the game's crates. The main wasm exports a small, versioned surface (`evani_xr_abi_version`, read the camera rig and the `RenderWorld` draw lists, submit per-eye view and projection) and the XR wasm imports it. Both use the one `GPUDevice` (WebGPU objects are plain JS handles passed across), so there is no second device and no resource copy.
  3. **Loaded on the button press.** Pressing Enter VR runs `import()` of the XR module's JS glue and `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming` of its wasm (content-hashed name, so the browser caches it after the first time), then `navigator.xr.requestSession("immersive-vr")` with a WebGPU XR layer. The renderer switches to the stereo path and the VR tier (comfort-off effects forced off, `assets/tiers/vr.json` limits); leaving VR returns to the saved flat tier. A failed download or session shows a message and leaves the flat game running.
  4. **Proof, as for the other modules.** The size report shows the main wasm unchanged with and without `xr` except for the detect part, and the marker scan finds `evani.module.xr` only in `<game>-xr.wasm`.

## Crates (all exact pins in `[workspace.dependencies]`)

| Crate | Where | Why |
|---|---|---|
| wgpu =30.0.1 (webgpu, wgsl, std) | evani-render, wasm only | WebGPU through the maintained Rust API; no other backend compiled |
| wasm-bindgen =0.2.129, wasm-bindgen-futures =0.4.79 | wasm only | JS interop and async adapter request; at or above what wgpu 30.0.1 needs, so Cargo unifies them |
| web-sys / js-sys =0.3.106 | wasm only | DOM, input, Gamepad API, Web Audio, localStorage, fetch, performance |
| console_error_panic_hook =0.1.7 | evani-core, wasm only | readable panics in the console (the overlay shows them too) |
| serde =1.0.229, serde_json =1.0.154 | evani-core and up | tier files, bindings, params and values JSON, live envelope |
| glam =0.34.1 | workspace pin only, no crate uses it yet | maths types for the render stage |
| bytemuck =1.25.2 | workspace pin only, no crate uses it yet | uniform and vertex casting in later stages |
| naga =30.0.1 (wgsl-in) | dev-dependency of evani-fx and evani-look-basic only | native tests validate every WGSL file and slot body; never in a game build |
| toml =1.1.8 | evani-xtask only | reads evani.toml and the app's Cargo.toml (as `toml::Table`, no serde derive needed) |
| flate2 =1.1.10 | evani-xtask only | gzip sizes in the report |

The live bridge (`tools/evani-live-bridge`) is a native dev tool that never ships in a game. Its
crates are pinned in its own Cargo.toml, not in `[workspace.dependencies]`, because nothing else
uses them:

| Crate | Why |
|---|---|
| tokio =1.53.3 | async runtime for the server, child processes (Trunk) and stdio (MCP) |
| axum =0.8.9 (http1, ws, no defaults) | HTTP routes and WebSocket upgrades on one localhost port, with typed Host/Origin header access |
| tokio-tungstenite =0.30.0 | the WebSocket client the `mcp` mode uses to reach `/ctl` (and the real-socket tests) |
| futures-util =0.3.34 (sink) | splitting WebSockets into reader and writer halves |
| rmcp =3.5.1 (server, transport-io) | the official Rust MCP SDK, for the stdio MCP server; handler written by hand, no macros |
| getrandom =0.4.3 | 32 bytes from the OS RNG for the dev token |
| evani-live (path, native) | the bake planner (`evani_live::bake::plan`), so the bridge writes exactly what the page's session bundle describes, with the same rules the engine reads back |

The page side of the link (`crates/evani-live/src/ws.rs`) needs no new crate, only extra web-sys
features (WebSocket, MessageEvent, CloseEvent, Document, Element, Location, console).

## Build pipeline

- **Trunk 0.21.14** (`cargo install trunk --locked`) builds the apps. It fetches wasm-bindgen and binaryen (wasm-opt, version_123) into its own cache, so we don't vendor them.
- **wasm-opt runs through Trunk**, via `data-wasm-opt="z"` on the app's `<link data-trunk rel="rust">`. xtask doesn't call wasm-opt itself.
- **wasm-opt needs explicit feature flags.** rustc 1.98 enables bulk memory, nontrapping float-to-int, sign-ext, mutable globals, reference types and multivalue by default for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`. Trunk's wasm-opt rejects such a module unless told, so index.html passes the matching `--enable-*` flags in `data-wasm-opt-params`. We enable only the features rustc already emits; nothing newer.
- **Release profile:** `opt-level = "z"`, `lto = true`, `codegen-units = 1`, `panic = "abort"`, `strip = true`.
- **xtask caps build parallelism.** It runs Trunk with `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=6` unless the caller set it, so this shared PC stays under its 70% CPU rule.
- **`--ship` drops live.** `cargo xtask build <app> --ship` turns `live` off whatever evani.toml says. A shipped game never contains the dev-only API.
- **The app crate's `default` features mirror its evani.toml**, so a plain `trunk serve` matches the manifest.

## Core design

- **Plugin traits.**
  - `KitPlugin::build(&self, &mut PluginContext)`.
  - `LookPlugin::build(&self, &mut LookContext)`, with `points()` and `styles()`. `LookContext` derefs to `PluginContext` and adds palette, material, shader_slot, post_effect, post_param and generator.
  - `EngineModule`: `id`, `label`, `build`, `start`, `frame`, `visibility_changed`, `status`.
- **Render types are abstract in the core.** Core holds only `MaterialDesc`, `MeshData`, `GeneratedAsset` and the slot sources, so it has no GPU dependency.
- **Shared resources live in the core.** `EffectsStatus` and `BuildInfo` are core resources, so the live API and the overlay can read them without depending on evani-fx or the app.
- **Looks can veto but not force.** A look can switch an effect off but can't force one on. The tier's `effects_on` decides what starts on, then the budget calculator decides what fits. With no look, effects aren't filtered by style class.
- **Composite passes never read their own target.** The bloom composite uses additive blending and the vignette multiplicative blending.
- **`bloom_weight` takes the mip index.** The slot's signature is `(mip: u32) -> f32`.
- **Baked values apply at start.** Baked params, formulas and look `post_param` overrides are queued during build and applied in `App::start`, after every module has registered. Unknown names become overlay notes, not errors, because the module may be compiled out. (Order since 2026-10-14: look overrides first, then baked files, so baked values win; see below.)
- **Events arrive one tick later.** Events are double-buffered, so a system sees an event the tick after it was sent. This keeps order independent of system order.
- **Asset content hashes use FNV-1a 64.** It is small, dependency-free and stable. It is used for cache keys, not for security.
- **Rigid wins.** If both physics features end up on (feature unification), `rigid` wins (`evani_physics::BACKEND`).
- **Tier fields can be null.** Where the spec gives no number (fps floors, refresh rates, resolutions), the tier field is `null`.
- **Owners are dotted, like param names** (`kit.example`, `look.basic`, `fx.bloom`), following docs/LIVE_API.md.
- **The live API answers at the frame boundary.** `window.evani.live.send()` returns a Promise that is answered at the next frame boundary. Planned commands answer `unknown_command` with `planned: true`.
- **The panel is an `EngineModule`.** DOM events only queue commands, which are applied at the frame boundary. The DOM is updated only when the param revision, the tier or the budget changed, so the panel adds no per-frame DOM writes.
- **Lints:**
  - `unwrap_used` and `expect_used` are denied workspace-wide outside tests (`clippy.toml` allows them in tests);
  - `unsafe_code` is denied;
  - browser errors go to the overlay (`ErrorLog`).
- **Renderer layout.**
  - Pipelines depend only on the output format, never on the size, so a tier switch or resize rebuilds targets and bind groups only.
  - Two bind-group layouts: A (texture, filtering sampler, dynamic uniform) for passes that sample, B (dynamic uniform only) for the forward frame and for effects with `input: false` (vignette).
  - Per-frame uniforms: a 3 x 64 KB ring, 256-byte aligned, one `write_buffer` per frame. Uniform bytes are copied from `f32` slices with `bytemuck::cast_slice` (the instance streams are described in the 2026-10-14 sprite-packing entry below).
  - Post effects use one render pass per draw (few, full-screen). Timestamps are written per render pass, summed per graph pass and read back through 3 buffers.
  - The canvas itself is at render resolution, so full-size targets match it and `present` is a copy plus sRGB encode.
- **Every pipeline build is a job.** Error scope, module, pipeline; then the scope and `get_compilation_info` are awaited in a spawned task that posts to an inbox. A pipeline is installed only after it compiled cleanly. If a look's slot body fails at start, the pass is rebuilt with the engine default bodies and the failure is logged. A slot rebuild swaps all affected pipelines together, or none.
- **Rejected slot bodies are notes, not errors.** LIVE_API says a rejected patch must not reach `console.error`; the old pipeline keeps drawing.
- **Device loss rebuilds everything.** The lost callback only sets a flag (it must be `Send`); the next frame drops the GPU state and requests a new adapter and device. An epoch number ignores callbacks from the old device. Slot bodies, params and the tier live in the world, so nothing is lost.
- **Adaptive quality settles after a (re)start.** It ignores frame times for 1.5 s after a device start or recovery, because pipeline compiles then are not the scene's real cost (without this, a recovery shed bloom).
- **Looks set render values through `post_param`.** `render.saturation`, `render.ambient` and `render.clear_color` belong to the renderer (they feed its uniforms); a look overrides their defaults instead of registering copies.
- **Module frame order is render, then fx.** A live effect toggle shows one frame later.

## Bench

- **Browser GPU bench plus a folded-in native run.** `apps/bench` is a library and a binary. The browser build is the real bench (`cargo xtask bench`). The old native CPU-only bench is folded in: `cargo run -p evani-bench` runs the same content and state machine headless, where the render module only counts draws, frame ms is the engine's CPU ms and GPU ms is n/a. One code path, so the native run cannot drift from the browser one. The old "sim under a quarter of the frame budget" rule is gone; the native run uses the same checks as the browser (frame ms being CPU time there).
- **Runner: a small Node script over raw CDP.** `tools/bench-runner/run.mjs` (Node 24 built-in WebSocket, no npm packages) starts Chrome and speaks the DevTools protocol; `cargo xtask bench` builds, serves and calls it, then prints the table from the JSON with the same `format_table` the native run uses (xtask depends on the bench library for the shared types). Chrome comes from `--chrome`, `EVANI_CHROME`, the installed Chrome or Edge, then Playwright's headless shell. Installed Chrome (`--headless=new`) is preferred: it reaches the real GPU through D3D12 and exposes timestamp queries. Flags: `--enable-unsafe-webgpu --enable-webgpu-developer-features --disable-frame-rate-limit --disable-gpu-vsync` plus no background throttling, so frame time is the cost of the frame, not the 60 Hz display. `requestPointerLock`, `requestFullscreen` and `keyboard.lock` are stubbed before the page loads. The runner only kills the Chrome it started.
- **One fresh page per tier.** A new page means a new GPU device, no state carried between tiers, and a viewport per tier: the tier's `target_resolution` (Ultra 2560 x 1440) or 1920 x 1080 at devicePixelRatio 1. Opening the page by hand without `?tier=` runs every tier in the one window.
- **Adaptive quality and warm-up are off.** The bench sets `aq.enabled = false`, forces the tier with `Tiers::set_active`, and skips the first-run warm-up by saving the first tier as the chosen one (`render.tier`) before the renderer starts; the previous value is put back at the end.
- **Run length.** Per phase 120 settle frames (drawn, not measured) then 300 measured; two phases per tier, so 600 measured frames and about 840 in all. `?warmup=` / `?frames=` (or `--warmup` / `--frames`) change it. The native run uses 10 + 60.
- **Deterministic content and camera.** All content comes from integer hashes; the camera is `CameraPath::pose(frame / 60 s)` with the frame counted by the bench, not read from the clock, so every run draws the same frames whatever the real frame time.
- **Stress sizing.** 3D phase: equal numbers of cubes and spheres plus a ground plane, filling 90% of `max_triangles` (sphere 720, cube 12, plane 2 triangles). 2D phase: 90% of `max_sprites`, submitted in layer order (the renderer's layer sort is then a no-op), over a backdrop of 5% of the triangle limit. Draw calls cannot be stressed through content: the renderer batches one draw per mesh kind and one for all sprites, so they stay at about 7 to 17 (mostly effect passes); the check is still made against the limit.
- **GPU time.** `RenderStats.gpu_ms`, the sum of the per-pass timestamp-query times the renderer already reads back (one to two frames late). The table shows the mean per phase (worse phase). It is `n/a` when the adapter lacks `timestamp-query`. It is information; only the real frame time is judged. Chrome quantises timestamps, so tiny values (under 0.1 ms) are rough.
- **What is judged.** Frame time: avg <= 1000 / `fps_target` plus 10% (the tolerance the warm-up uses for "holds its target"), and p99 <= 1000 / `fps_floor` where the JSON gives a floor. Counts: draw calls, triangles (3D phase) and sprites (2D phase) <= the limits, budget points used <= the tier's points. Two guards so a run cannot pass by doing nothing: the renderer must really have drawn at least 80% of the planned content, and no render errors may appear. The frame-time columns show the worse of the two phases.
- **VR is a preset only.** It runs mono at the VR preset (72 fps target, 750k triangles, ...) and the report says `(mono, preset only)`. There is no stereo path in M1.
- **A FAIL is a finding, the tool still worked.** `cargo xtask bench` exits non-zero when any tier fails (so a script or CI notices a regression) and prints which check failed with the numbers. A weak machine failing a high tier is expected and is not a bug in the bench; a run error (no Chrome, no WebGPU, a timeout) is reported separately as `ERROR` and also exits non-zero. Compare runs on the same machine only.
- **The old skeleton is replaced.** The example kit's spinners are no longer part of the bench; it measures the renderer, not a kit.
- **First real result (RTX 4060 Ti, Chrome 155): Ultra failed its frame target, then passed after the bulk-packing fix.** Before: the 2D phase with 450k sprites averaged 22.5 ms between frames (p99 31.2 ms) while the GPU needed 4.1 ms; the bench's own submit is about 2.8 ms (`submit_ms`), so about 17 ms was the renderer packing sprites one float at a time (`to_le_bytes`, built with `opt-level = "z"`). After the sprite and instance packing change (see the 2026-10-14 entry at the end of this file): Ultra avg 10.0 ms, p99 12.4 ms, GPU 2.4 ms, PASS; the other four tiers also got faster. Full table: low 0.65 to 0.51 ms, medium 1.45 to 0.93, high 4.88 to 2.22, ultra 22.48 to 10.00, vr 0.93 to 0.65 (average ms, worse phase; one run each, same machine).
- **`--shots` is best-effort.** It saves a picture mid-phase for a human check; a busy 1440p page can fail to answer a screenshot, in which case the runner logs it and carries on without the picture. Plain runs never take screenshots.


## Live render adaptation (stage 5a, 2026-10-14)

- **2026-10-14 — Transactions and history.** Every mutating command (needs a 1–500 char note) is planned first (validated, clamped, nothing applied), then applied and recorded as one transaction holding its exact old/new values; a `batch` is one transaction, and any failing part applies nothing. History is linear in the page: undo/redo step whole transactions, add no entries, are never watched and clear the current watch; a new transaction after an undo truncates the redo tail; 500 are kept. A tier change's effect-switch resets (made by the effects module on the next frame) are folded into the same transaction, so undo is exact. Snapshot restore is a diff applied as one transaction.
- **2026-10-14 — Deferred replies, telemetry drained per frame.** A mutating reply waits `opts.telemetry_ms` (default param `live.telemetry_ms`, 1000) so it can carry the next second of frame timing and what the budget switched. Due replies are drained once per frame and nothing blocks the frame; `telemetry_ms: 0` replies at once. Samples sit in a preallocated 1024-frame ring with no per-frame allocation. The page caps telemetry subscriptions at 16 per session; the 4-per-connection cap is the bridge's job (it already ends a connection's subscriptions on close).
- **2026-10-14 — WGSL `slot.set` goes through `SlotRebuilds`.** The page does a structural check, queues a rebuild request and parks the command; the renderer compiles in an error scope and completes it. If no renderer takes the request within 3 frames, the page completes it itself and reports `compile.checked: "structural"`, so headless and renderer-less builds still work. After 8 s without a result the change fails with `timeout`. On any failure the previous body stays (`kept: "previous"`), with messages in slot-body coordinates.
- **2026-10-14 — Auto-revert rules and baseline guard.** Each change is watched for `live.revert.window_s` (10 s); if the 0.5 s frame-time average (all samples, including ones before the change) stays more than `live.revert.over_pct` (25 %) over the target for `live.revert.sustain_s` (3 s), or `DeviceStatus.lost_count` rises, the inverse of the change is applied as a new `auto_revert` transaction (so undoing it brings the change back) and the original is marked `auto_reverted`. A change made while the 1 s baseline was already over the limit is not armed, so a slow machine doesn't undo innocent changes. One watch at a time; a newer change supersedes it.
- **2026-10-14 — Bake goes page → bridge → disk.** The page can't write files, so it returns a bundle of changed, touched values with their targets and note lines. The bridge plans the files with `evani_live::bake::plan`, a pure native planner (caller reads and writes) shared by the tests and the bridge, then writes them. Writes are confined to `looks/<crate>/`, `kits/<crate>/` and `assets/tiers/`, with safe file names and a canonicalized-parent check against the engine root. Bake is not a transaction. Output is deterministic (sorted keys, shortest f32s) so diffs stay small, and CHANGES.md only gets a section when another file changed.
- **2026-10-14 — Shipped builds load baked files at build time.** Each look and kit crate uses `build = "../../tools/bake-index.rs"`, which embeds its baked files; the crate does `include!(concat!(env!("OUT_DIR"), "/baked.rs"))` and `ctx.baked_files(BAKED)`. A shipped build reproduces the tuned look with no live code and no runtime file access.
- **2026-10-14 — Capture, bench and compare interface in `evani-core/src/capture.rs` as plain data.** `DeviceStatus`, `CaptureQueue`, `CaptureView`, `CaptureResult` and `BenchPath` are data only; the renderer fills them, and only evani-live inserts the queue and bench path, so a build without live never queues a capture. `capture.frame`, `bench.run` and `compare.ab` stay `planned: true` until evani-live builds them on top. (Built in stage 6, below; the bench path moved to the game.)
- **2026-10-14 — `App::new` registers `aq.*` and `DeviceStatus`.** The adaptive-quality policy params (`AqParams`) and the device-loss counter exist in every game, so `policy.get/set`, the auto-revert target and device-loss detection don't depend on which modules are compiled in.
- **2026-10-14 — Look overrides apply before baked params.** A look's `post_param` sets a default for another module's param; a baked value is a later, deliberate tuning of the same param. Applying overrides first means baked values win.
- **2026-10-14 — Sprite and mesh-instance packing is one bulk copy.** The renderer packed every sprite and instance float by float (`to_le_bytes` into a byte vector, `opt-level = "z"`): about 50 ns per sprite, so 450k sprites cost about 17 ms a frame and Ultra failed its target. Now `InstanceRaw` and `SpriteRaw` (`#[repr(C)]`, `bytemuck::Pod`, 64 bytes each, the same 16-float layout the shaders already read, so no shader change) are written straight into reused `Vec`s and uploaded with one `write_buffer` per stream (buffers still grow to the next power of two). bytemuck was already in the lockfile through wgpu; `derive(Pod)` needs no `unsafe` in our code, so `unsafe_code = "deny"` stands. Mesh instances are grouped by kind with a two-pass counting sort (before, the list was scanned once per mesh kind); order inside a kind and the unknown-kind drop are unchanged. The sprite uv rectangles come from a 16-entry table built once per frame instead of a division per sprite. `sort_sprites` checks layer order in one O(n) pass and only then falls back to a stable `sort_by_key` (the old insertion sort was O(n squared) for a badly ordered list; the sort allocates a scratch buffer only in that rare case). Unit tests compare the packed bytes with the old float-by-float layout.
- **2026-10-14 — Where the remaining Ultra time goes (measured, not guessed).** A temporary `performance.now` probe (removed) on the 450k-sprite phase gave per frame: pack about 5.0 ms, `write_buffer` of the 28.8 MB about 1.85 ms, graph and submit about 0.15 ms; the bench's own content submit is 2.75 ms. That adds up to the 10 ms between frames; the GPU (2.4 ms) is not the limit. Pack is still the biggest piece because the size-optimised wasm does not unroll or vectorise the 64-byte copies.
- **2026-10-14 — `opt-level = 3` for `evani-render` tried and not kept.** A `[profile.release.package.evani-render] opt-level = 3` override cut Ultra from 10.0 to 8.5 ms (pack about 5 ms to about 3.4 ms) but grew the shipped bundle by 12.8 KB gzip in the full build (303.6 to 316.4 KB, +4.2%) and by 9.9 KB in the no-live build (221.2 to 231.1 KB). Ultra already passes with 45% headroom on this machine, and the weaker tiers are nowhere near their limits, so the size cost is not worth it. Revisit if a slower GPU/CPU machine fails Ultra on CPU time. Gzip sizes (`cargo xtask sizes`): full 302.3 to 303.6 KB, no-live 219.5 to 221.2 KB with the packing change (+1.3 and +1.7 KB; wasm raw 669.0 to 672.7 KB).

## Bridge follow-ups (2026-10-14)

- **2026-10-14 — `session.mirror` goes to every `/ctl` client, always on.** Broadcast like `auto_revert`, not tied to a telemetry subscription; the tab sees each committed change within a frame. The bridge still stores the latest copy for page reconnects.
- **2026-10-14 — Launcher shim forwards `--game`.** Other behaviour unchanged.
- **2026-10-14 — `NO_COLOR` is removed from every trunk child** (bridge game build, xtask sizes and bench builds): trunk reads it as a boolean and rejects "1", which Evani.App sets. `tools/live-check/capture.mjs` keeps its own removal, harmlessly.

## Live capture, bench and A/B (stage 6, 2026-10-14)

- **2026-10-14 — The renderer reads back raw RGBA; evani-live downscales and encodes.** `CaptureResult.rgba` is the full-size RGBA8 (sRGB) LDR target, copied into a mappable buffer after the graph walk (rows padded to 256 bytes), mapped asynchronously after submit and completed on a later frame, so a capture never stalls a frame. Downscaling (area average) and PNG encoding happen in evani-live, which only dev builds contain, so no image code reaches a shipped game. The readback is behind the render feature `capture`, the LDR target's `COPY_SRC` usage too.
- **2026-10-14 — Our own small PNG encoder in evani-live, no new dependency.** `img.rs` has a per-row filter (smallest sum of absolute values), a single fixed-Huffman deflate block with LZ77 hash chains (32K window), Adler-32 and a const-built CRC32 table, plus base64 and the image diff. About 550 lines with its tests, round-trip tested against `flate2` (already in the workspace, used only as a dev-dependency). A crate (`png`, `image`) would have added more code and dependencies for something only the dev bridge sees; sandbox screenshots at 480 x 270 come out at 145 to 186 KB (fixed Huffman codes trade some size for a tiny encoder), fine for MCP image content; a dynamic-Huffman block is the upgrade if size ever matters.
- **2026-10-14 — The sim clock hold lives in the core behind a `capture` feature.** `CaptureQueue.sim_time_override` is copied into `Time.held` by `App::frame_by`; while it is set no fixed ticks run and `render_seconds()` returns the held time, so the sim pauses and resumes where it was. The feature is `evani-core/capture`, turned on only through evani-live and the sandbox's `live` feature, so a build without live has no hold code at all. Bench and compare keep the hold for the whole run and set t = k/60 on frame k, so both sides of a compare see exactly the same frozen scene and camera.
- **2026-10-14 — Bench rules copied into evani-live, not shared with apps/bench.** `bench.run` judges with the same rules as `apps/bench` (avg within the frame target + 10 %, p99 under the fps floor, draw calls, triangles, sprites and points within the tier's limits; nearest-rank percentiles; failures as `{limit, max, seen}`). They are a dozen lines; sharing them would make evani-live (and so every live build) depend on the bench app, or need a new shared crate. The copy is cheaper than either.
- **2026-10-14 — Compare B (or A) can be a set of changes, not only a snapshot.** A side is `"current"`, a snapshot, or `{values}` / `{cmds}` planned exactly like `param.set` / `batch`; the side's target state is `with_changes(S0, changes)`, a pure function. Every run starts from S0 (captured first) and ends with S0 re-applied, so a compare leaves no trace unless `keep: true` (with a note), which ends on the real B and records it as one `compare.ab` transaction. "Try bloom off and show me" no longer needs a snapshot dance.
- **2026-10-14 — ABBA by default.** Runs go A, B, B, A (or A, B with `order: "AB"`) and each side's samples are pooled, so warm-up, shader-cache and thermal drift fall on both sides equally. Each side is captured once, on its first visit, before measuring. A 0.5 s warm-up (held at t = 0) precedes every run.
- **2026-10-14 — What is busy during a run.** While a bench or compare runs, every mutating command, undo/redo, `snapshot.save`, `bake.export` and the three run commands answer `busy` with `until_ms` (estimated remaining ms) and the run id: they would change the state under the run or read its temporary state. Reads still answer. A `capture.frame` only takes a few frames, so it just pauses the inbox. Auto-revert's watch is skipped while a run is active, so a heavy B can't be "reverted" mid-measure.
- **2026-10-14 — The game provides the bench path.** The sandbox inserts `BenchPath(scene::camera_path())` under its `live` feature; without one, live flies a default orbit. Live never inserts it, so a game's own path is never overwritten.
- **2026-10-14 — Live runs measure at the display's frame cap.** Unlike `cargo xtask bench`, the live page runs with vsync, so `frame_ms` sits near the refresh interval and `gpu_ms` (timestamp queries) carries the signal. First real result (sandbox, Ultra, RTX 4060 Ti, headless Chrome, 2 s AB): bloom off saved 0.31 ms of GPU time (1.38 to 1.07 ms; the three bloom passes 0.52 ms) and changed 64 % of pixels (mean abs 19.9, PSNR 18.2 dB).
- **2026-10-14 — Size check: shipped games don't grow.** `cargo xtask sizes` after stage 6: the no-live sandbox wasm is 471,996 bytes (221.2 KB gzip in all), 360 bytes smaller than the same build just before stage 6 (911cc8e, 472,356 bytes; the stage 6 brief's 467,917 predates the sprite-packing merge). The live builds grew from 303.6 to 329.2 KB gzip (the run state machine, image tools and PNG encoder); they are dev-only.

## Kit review card (2026-10-14)

- **2026-10-14 — `cargo xtask review <app-or-kit>` makes the hand-over card.** It writes `.vibedeck/review/<app>/card.md` (a plain top section the worker pastes into its "Ready for review" report, then the details), `card.json` and three captures. Everything that decides what the card says (bench lines and verdicts, size delta and the previous card, build-time on/off, reference prompt, try list, done-when parsing and the checklist) is a pure function in `tools/xtask/src/review.rs` with tests; `cmd_review` only gathers inputs. It runs from xtask so it reuses the Trunk build, size measuring and marker scan of `cargo xtask build` and the bench of `cargo xtask bench` rather than copying them.
- **2026-10-14 — Captures go through the live bridge, not the bench runner.** `capture.frame` with `camera: "path"` and a held `sim_time` gives deterministic frames of the game's own `BenchPath` (0, 20 and 40 s: three views a third of the sandbox's 60 s orbit apart, 640 px wide), whereas the bench runner only knows the `apps/bench` stress scene. `tools/live-check/capture.mjs` already starts or reuses this project's bridge and a headless Chrome with pointer and keyboard locks stubbed, so it gained a review mode (`--game`, `--times`, `--width`) instead of a second script.
- **2026-10-14 — The bench is reused when it is newer than the engine.** `target/evani-bench/results.json` is reused if it is newer than every file under `crates/`, `apps/bench/`, `assets/tiers/` and `Cargo.lock`; otherwise (or with `--rebench`) the bench runs. A failing tier is a finding on the card, not an error; only a run that saved nothing makes the card incomplete. The bench measures the engine's stress scene per tier, as done-when 3 asks (`apps/bench`), not the kit's demo; the card says so.
- **2026-10-14 — Which card the size is compared with.** `.vibedeck/review/history.json` keeps the gzipped size of every card made. "Previous" is the last card for the same kit when there is one (what this round of work changed, the question a re-review asks); else the nearest earlier kit in ROADMAP order that has a card (a new kit is compared with the kit built before it, so the growth from kit to kit shows); else the most recent card of any kit (kits outside the roadmap, such as the sandbox's example kit). The card names which rule it used. The history is local, like the card.
- **2026-10-14 — Build-time on/off check on the card.** Every `evani.module.*` in the shipped wasm must be render, a module evani.toml turns on, or one the app, kit or look declare themselves; every `evani.shader.*` must belong to a listed effect (`bloom` covers `bloom_*`), to the always-on render module, or to the app, kit or look. Anything else fails done-when 1. Listed modules or effects missing from the wasm are shown as a warning (broken feature wiring), not a failure.
- **2026-10-14 — The checklist is parsed from docs/KIT_PROCESS.md.** The numbered items under "Done when" (with "(... planned ...)" notes dropped) become the checklist, so editing the process edits the card. Items are matched to automatic checks by wording: gzipped size (build and on/off), `apps/bench` (bench), SPEC.md plus README (docs present and the ROADMAP row at `review`). The rest are marked for QQ to check by hand. A test fails if the process loses one of those three hooks.
- **2026-10-14 — `[review] try` in evani.toml.** An optional list of short lines for the card's "what to try". It is never a Cargo feature; the parser rejects any other field under `[review]`. A "What to try" section in the kit README is the fallback.

## M1 acceptance (2026-10-14)

- **2026-10-14 — Adaptive quality ignores capture and held-clock frames.** Repeated `capture.frame` calls stall frames on purpose (held sim clock, pixel readback), and adaptive quality reacted by shedding bloom and then vignette, so captures showed a degraded picture while `fx.*.enabled` still said on. Frames with a pending capture or a held clock, and the 1 s after (`CAPTURE_SETTLE_MS`), are ignored, the same way as the settle after a device start. Behind the render feature `capture`, so shipped games are unchanged.
- **2026-10-14 — Bloom tuned and baked.** The look's Bloom (threshold 1.1, knee 0.5, intensity 0.6, 5 levels) hazed the whole image. Tuned live by comparing captures at a fixed sim time and baked into `looks/evani-look-basic/params.json`: threshold 1.5, knee 0.2, intensity 0.3, 4 levels, so only the glow material blooms and the glow stays near its source. The shipped (no-live) build renders pixel-identical to the tuned live session (docs/M1-ACCEPTANCE.md, DONE 9).
- **2026-10-14 — Acceptance driver `tools/live-check/accept.mjs`.** Drives the bridge over MCP on stdio and /ctl like the AI and the tab, and compares builds with a stepped clock (performance.now and requestAnimationFrame replaced, exactly 1/60 s per frame) so two different builds render the same sim tick. Node only, no npm packages, like the other live checks.

## Kit 001 kart-racer core additions (2026-10-14)

Three small, additive changes the kart racer needed; every existing app and test builds unchanged apart from one new struct field (below).

- **2026-10-14 — Split-screen is `RenderWorld.views`.** A kit pushes `View { camera, rect }` (normalised `[x, y, w, h]` from the top-left) each frame; empty means today's single full-canvas view through `RenderWorld.camera`. The forward pass writes one frame uniform per view (its own camera and aspect) and, inside the one render pass, sets the viewport and draws every instance run once per view; the instance stream is packed once. Sprites, tonemap and effects stay full-canvas (a kit draws per-half HUD sprites itself; full-screen effects that care, such as speed lines, get the split through their params). Views clear with the draws each frame, and a live capture drops them so `capture.frame` keeps shooting one full frame from its pose. `count_draws` multiplies instance draws and triangles by the view count, so native stats match the GPU path.
- **2026-10-14 — Effects can read the scene depth (`EffectShader.depth`).** Two more bind group layouts: AD (layout A plus `texture_depth_2d` at binding 3) and BD (the uniform plus depth at binding 1); a depth shader reads it with `textureLoad`. The depth target is now created with `TEXTURE_BINDING`, the renderer builds an AD group per colour target and one BD group when targets are (re)made, and the forward pass stores depth only when an enabled effect pass lists `depth` among its graph inputs (otherwise it stays `Discard`, as before). `EffectShader` gained the public field `depth`, so struct literals must set it; bloom and vignette say `depth: false`. That is the one source-level change for effect authors, accepted because effects live in this workspace.
- **2026-10-14 — Per-pad bindings `padN:button:B` and `padN:axis:A[:+|-]`.** N is 1 to `MAX_PADS` (stored 0-based in `Binding::PadNButton` / `PadNAxis`) and listens to that gamepad only, so two local players can each own a pad; `pad:` still means any pad. Parse, `Display` and evaluation share one pad loop. Rebinding capture still records a pressed pad button as `pad:button:B`.

## Kit 003 match-3 core additions (2026-10-14)

One small, additive change the match-3 kit needed; every existing app and test builds unchanged.

- **2026-10-14 — `Input::primary_pointer()` keeps the last primary pointer's position.** It returns a `PrimaryPointer` (last position, the position of the last press, kind, down and the latched pressed/released edges). `Input::pointers()` forgets a touch as soon as it lifts (`PointerGone` follows every touch `pointerup`), so a quick tap or synthetic swipe whose down, move, up and gone all arrive in one frame left a press edge with no position. The new fields are written in `Input::handle` only for primary pointer events; nothing else reads them, so behaviour elsewhere is unchanged. A unit test covers a one-frame tap.
- **2026-10-14 — `juice` and `particles` are params-only effects for now.** Both follow `shake`: no render pass, a budget slot (0 and 2 points, as in the catalog), their tunables (`fx.juice.pop_scale`, `fx.juice.float_rise`, `fx.particles.max`, `fx.particles.density`, `fx.particles.gravity`) and the on/off state in `EffectsStatus`; the game animates its own sprites while they are on. The catalog's compute-shader particle system is a core request (CORE_REQUESTS.md). Both are in every tier's `effects_on`; they only exist in games that list them.
- **2026-10-14 — Bloom works with 2D looks.** `bloom`'s `EffectInfo.styles` said 3D and pseudo-3D only, so a 2D look (cartoon2d) had it switched off by style, while `catalog/effects.json` lists bloom for `2d` too. Sprites are drawn into the HDR target before the effect passes, so they bloom like meshes; the styles are now `ALL`. 3D games (kart racer, sandbox) are unchanged.

## Sandbox review fixes (2026-10-14)

Chris's review of the sandbox, notes 1 to 6.

- **2026-10-14 — Adaptive quality and auto-revert judge work time, not the frame interval.** With vsync the frame interval sits at 16.7 ms on a 60 Hz display however light the frame is, and it also stretches when the browser or another tab is busy, so judging it cut bloom and scale at Medium and High on a machine with plenty of headroom. Both now judge work time: CPU ms in the frame callback plus GPU ms, smoothed. GPU time comes from timestamp queries; without them the renderer estimates it from submit to `onSubmittedWorkDone` (shown with `~` in the overlay), which over-reads a little but never misses real load. Under load adaptive quality pauses effects by lowest value per point first, then steps the render scale down 0.05 at a time to a floor of 80 % of the tier's own scale (`MIN_SCALE_FRACTION`; `aq.render_scale_floor` cannot go below it, `aq.render_scale_step` is 0.05 to 0.1). Below the floor the right answer is a lower tier, not a softer picture.
- **2026-10-14 — Telemetry samples carry `work_ms` and `held`.** `work_ms` is the measure above; `held` marks frames stalled on purpose (capture, held clock, bench or A/B run, settle time after a device start). Held frames stay in `frame_ms` (they did take that long) but are skipped by every work-time average, auto-revert and adaptive quality, so a capture can neither revert a change nor lower quality. `held` stays internal (no extra output field); `work_ms` is added to change results and subscriptions. The `auto_revert` event says `detail.measure: "work_ms"`; `reason` keeps the name `frame_time` so existing clients still match it.
- **2026-10-14 — `tier.set vr` outside an XR session fails with `needs_xr_session`.** The VR preset is stereo and only makes sense inside an immersive WebXR session. On a flat screen it is now refused with `data.selectable` (the tiers that can be picked). Tier lists (the panel, `did_you_mean`) use `Tiers::selectable()`, and the panel shows Enter VR only when the browser has WebXR with WebGPU.
- **2026-10-14 — 4x MSAA from Medium up, as tier data.** Tier presets have `msaa` (1 or 4, checked on load; missing means 1): Low 1; Medium, High, Ultra and VR 4. Only the forward pass draws multisampled and resolves into `hdr`; effects and tonemap run at one sample, so the cost is the forward pass's. The multisampled depth is resolved (a small depth-resolve pipeline) only while an enabled effect pass reads depth; otherwise it is discarded as before. MSAA turns on once the 4x pipelines are built, so a tier switch never stalls. The overlay's render line shows `msaa 4x` while it is on.
- **2026-10-14 — `render.pixel_art` is canvas CSS, not a shader.** The browser upscales the canvas (render scale, DPR cap) to its CSS size. `render.pixel_art` sets `image-rendering: pixelated` on the canvas so that upscale is nearest-neighbour; off is `auto`. It costs nothing on the GPU, needs no extra pass or target, and is the same switch the platformer branch used. Inside the frame everything is still filtered normally; a pixel-art kit draws its sprites at whole-pixel sizes.
- **2026-10-14 — The protocol rule.** The `protocol` value (`evani-live/1`) is the contract version the Evani.dev tab checks. It is bumped to `evani-live/2` only for changes that aren't backwards compatible (a removed or renamed command or field, a changed meaning); additive changes (new commands, fields, error codes such as `work_ms` and `needs_xr_session`) keep `/1`, and clients ignore fields they don't know.
- **2026-10-14 — `cargo xtask size-report` measures what players download.** It builds each game's ship build (release, `live` off) and reports every shipped file raw, gzip and brotli. Brotli comes from the `brotli` crate (9.0.0, quality 11, window 22, what a static host serves pre-compressed), not the `brotli` command: the command exists in Git Bash but not in plain PowerShell or on other machines, and the crate was already in the local cargo cache. It is a dev-tool dependency only (xtask), never in a game. The report proves `wasm-opt` ran: Trunk writes wasm-bindgen's output to `target/wasm-bindgen/release/<bin>_bg.wasm` before running `wasm-opt -Oz` into dist, so the dist wasm must be smaller than that file, which must be newer than the build's start; otherwise the report fails. Over 1.5 MB raw (or with `--modules`) it rebuilds once per module to list the biggest contributors.
- **2026-10-14 — Adaptive quality judges a percentile, skips disturbed frames, and scales step costs to today's work.** Chris saw bloom stay "on, paused" with work at 0.96 of 16.7 ms after a load ended. Cause: each step's saving was measured during the load, when the GPU was shared with it (bloom read about 13.5 ms), and restore added that whole amount back (`work + cost <= restore line`), so bloom never fitted again. Now: (1) a step's cost is scaled by `min(1, work now / work when it was taken)` (`StepCost::cost_at`); (2) a step that stays blocked is tried anyway after 4 × `aq.upgrade_frames`, and if it has to be paused again the next wait doubles (at most 4 doublings), so a wrong estimate cannot hold an effect off for ever and a real overload cannot make it flap; (3) the controller judges the window's `aq.percentile` (default 80) instead of the average, and work must be over the line for `aq.degrade_frames` frames and a whole window, so one-off hitches never cut quality; (4) frames during a pipeline compile (in flight or finishing), a tier switch or a live change, and for `aq.grace_ms` (default 1000) after one, are not judged (`QualityState::disturb`, shown as "quality settling"). Recovery after a load: about 21 s in the unit test (`everything_comes_back_after_a_load_spike`, which fails on the old code with bloom stuck), documented as at most 25 s at 60 Hz. `held` (captures, held clock) and live auto-revert are unchanged; settling is a separate flag.
- **2026-10-14 — Dev builds label their download with the published size; log lines carry a time.** A dev build's download (live module, debug-ish assets) is far bigger than what players get, so the overlay says `download 5.60 MB (dev build; published ~228.7 KB gzip)`. The published size comes from `target/evani-sizes/size-report.json` (`cargo xtask size-report`), which the bridge passes at build time as `EVANI_PUBLISHED_GZ` (part of the build stamp, so a new report rebuilds); without a report it says "size not measured yet". Overlay log lines start with `m:ss.s` since the page started, so a reader can line them up with a recording.

## Kit 001 polish (2026-10-14)

Chris's review of the kart racer asked for grounded shadows, readable toon light with ink on the karts, a dressed track, real-looking placeholder models and scripted review captures. The core changes are small and additive; every existing app and test builds unchanged.

- **2026-10-14 — Built-in `MeshId::BEVEL_CUBE` and `MeshId::CYLINDER`.** Placeholder models built from raw cubes and spheres read as primitives, and wheels as 768-triangle spheres pushed Low past its 100k-triangle limit. `BEVEL_CUBE` (id 3) is the unit cube with every edge chamfered by `mesh::BEVEL` (0.12), flat shaded: 6 faces, 12 edge strips and 8 corner triangles, 44 triangles; under non-uniform scale the chamfer stretches with the box, which is fine for props. `CYLINDER` (id 4) is radius 0.5 along Y, 16 sides with smooth side normals and flat caps, 64 triangles. `BUILTIN_MESH_COUNT` is 5; the renderer uploads every built-in at start (about 4 KB more vertex data). New ids are appended, so existing `MeshId` values keep their meaning. The mesh test checks both are closed (every edge shared by two opposite triangles) and wound outward.
- **2026-10-14 — Outlines ink creases, not only silhouettes.** The outline pass found depth jumps only, so a kart in front of the road (a small depth step) and every edge inside a model stayed un-inked. It now also rebuilds view-space points from the depth around each pixel and inks convex creases where the surface bends sharply, so karts, drivers, bevels and props get ink on their inner edges. Still one full-canvas pass over the scene depth (no normal buffer, no per-mesh hull pass); the thresholds are `fx.outline.*` params and the toon look bakes its strength and thickness.
- **2026-10-14 — A `shadows` effect: one directional shadow map from Medium up, blob shadows always available.** The effect (catalog `shadows`, cost 2) is the budgeted switch and the tunables (`fx.shadows.distance`, `strength`, `bias`, `normal_offset`); the drawing is in the renderer behind its `shadows` feature: a depth-only pass from the light over a box that follows the camera, then a PCF lookup in the forward pass. Map sizes come from the tier (`shadow_map`, `shadow_pcf`): Medium and VR 1024 with one 2x2 hardware tap, High 2048 and Ultra 4096 with 3x3 taps; Low has none. Whenever there is no map (Low, the effect off, or a game without it) the forward pass darkens the ground under `RenderWorld.blobs` (`draw_blob(ground, radius)`, at most 32 a frame, `blob_strength` per look) instead, so nothing floats on any tier. Games without the effect build no shadow code (feature-gated shaders and pipelines).
- **2026-10-14 — Scripted capture moments (`CaptureMoments`, `capture.frame {moment}`).** Review captures of a racer along a camera path showed an empty track. A kit now registers named moments (`add(name, label, setup)`), each a function that stages the game state from the world (the kart racer starts a one-player race on its autopilot, reseeds the RNG and steps a fixed dt until a drift, an item hit or the finish line holds, then sets `Race.cam_snap` so its chase camera and FOV kick jump straight to the shot). Live runs the setup, holds the clock there and lets the game's own camera frame it; `session.status` lists the moments. The resource is core data, but only builds with live insert or fill it, so a shipped game has none of this code. `[review] moments` in evani.toml (`name@tier`) picks the card's shots and the tier of each; `capture.mjs --moments` turns adaptive quality off for the shots and puts the tier and adaptive quality back afterwards.
- **2026-10-14 — The toon look's light reads from the side.** A higher, softer light left the karts evenly lit. The sun now comes from the side and a little ahead (`render.light.direction` [0.62, 0.85, 0.28]) and the ramp bands are harder (edges at N.L 0.02 and 0.45, softened over +-0.025; shadow 0.25, mid 0.6, lit 1.0), so every model splits into a lit, a mid and a shadow side and shadows fall clearly to one side of the road. Only the kart racer uses the toon look.

## Kit 001 second review: look, start and moments (2026-10-14)

- **2026-10-14 — New built-in meshes `LOW_SPHERE`, `CONE`, `DOME`.** Cheap shapes for lights, markers and hills, with tests. Additive.
- **2026-10-14 — New core `rim` slot (`RIM` in `BUILTIN_SLOTS`, used by `forward.wgsl`).** Looks can add a rim light on drivers; the toon look sets `RIM_BODY`. Additive; looks that do not set it are unchanged.
- **2026-10-14 — The toon fog slot fades the far distance.** So no camera sees the end of the world (title screen review point 4). Together with a 2000 m far ground and a closed hill ring.
- **2026-10-14 — Start lights, grass slopes, start boost.** Lights count down 3-2-1 then green; the shoulder edges slope to the meadow to remove the ink line; throttle in the last 0.5 s before GO gives a 0.8 s boost (`start_boost_window_s`, `start_boost_s`, local players only so AI races stay deterministic). Rival AIs never aim for free item boxes, a possible gameplay tweak.
- **2026-10-14 — The stats overlay is hidden while captures are held.** The game hides it while a capture is pending or the sim time is overridden and restores the player's setting after; the saved setting is untouched.
- **2026-10-14 — HUD follows hud-layout.md.** Deliberate differences: the minimap stays bottom-right and the drift text bottom-left (the layout contradicts itself on drift text, and moving the minimap needs the CSS-to-canvas scale, planned with touch buttons).
- **2026-10-14 — Props are ECS entities; nine capture moments.** Scenery props become `kart_racer.prop` entities; moments `countdown`, `countdown_go`, `boost`, `overtake`, `final_lap`, `podium` added.
- **2026-10-14 — Measurements.** Final 5-round bench at 1920x1080 (mean total GPU ms): Low 1.24, Medium 3.24, High 3.22, Ultra 3.38; Low is cheapest. Published size 827.9 KB raw, 322.5 KB gzip, 267.1 KB brotli (was 300.7 KB gzip before the candy world, props and moments). GPU timings swing with the GPU clock state, so medians are quoted in the report.

## Kit 001 kart racer in the ECS (2026-10-14)

Chris's review point "entities 0": the kit kept karts and items in plain vectors inside its `Race` resource, outside the engine's ECS.

- **2026-10-14 — Kart racer gameplay objects are ECS entities: karts are spawned per race, items in flight live in fixed pools.** Karts (`Kart`, `AiState`, `Controls`) and item boxes (`ItemBox`) are entities, and the fixed tick is seven named systems in `Stage::Update` with explicit order (`kart_racer.flow` 0, `control` 10, `karts` 20, `boxes` 30, `bumps` 40, `items` 50, `standings` 60). `Race` keeps race-wide state and the entity lists in grid order (`grid`, `boxes`, `pools`); systems walk those lists, never a storage's dense order, so runs stay deterministic, and kart numbers in events and results stay grid indices. Items in flight (Goo, Bomb, Seeker, Blast) are a fixed pool per kind (16, 8, 8, 8) spawned once, each with `InFlight { on, seq }`; a full pool reuses its oldest item. Why a pool and not spawn and despawn: capture moments only get `&World` (`CaptureMoments` setup), and in this ECS spawning and despawning need `&mut World` (deferred `Commands` are applied by the schedule, not inside a moment), so a moment must be able to fire and retire items without a structural change. Karts are still respawned on every new race (`race::reset_mut`); a moment resets in place (`race::reset` on `&World`) and refuses, with an error, a field size that differs from the current one. A one-player race has 60 entities (8 karts, 12 boxes, 40 pooled items). No core change was needed beyond the additive ECS helpers already recorded (`get2_mut`, `entities_with`, `sorted_entities`, `count`, `despawn_all`); a `&mut World` moment setup would let a moment spawn items for real, which is noted, not requested.

## Versions, published size and the tier warm-up (2026-10-14)

Chris: "Make sure this has version numbers of things being used from Evani.dev." Plus two overlay lines that read wrong in kart-racer review captures. All additive; every existing kit, look, app and test builds unchanged.

- **2026-10-14 — The overlay's first line and `session.status.versions` list what the game was built from.** `evani 0.1.0 @<commit> · core 0.1.0 · render 0.1.0 · fx 0.1.0 [bloom outline speedlines shake shadows] · physics 0.1.0 · live 0.1.0 (evani-live/1) · kit kart-racer 0.1.0 · look toon 0.1.0`. Each version is the `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` of the part's own crate, reported through new default methods: `EngineModule::version` / `version_features` / `protocol`, `KitPlugin::version`, `LookPlugin::version` (all `None` or empty by default). The defaults return `None` rather than a version because a default body compiles inside evani-core and would report core's version for every kit; so each Evani.Dev crate implements `version()` as `Some(env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"))` (render, fx, physics, net, xr, live, both kits, both looks), and `None` marks a game-local module (the sandbox panel, the kart racer's UI), which is left out. The app records each part in `BuildInfo.parts` when it is added, so only what is compiled in can appear: a shipped build has no live part and no protocol. fx lists the effects it was built with (`FxModule` entries). The engine commit is baked by evani-core's `build.rs` (`git rev-parse --short=7 HEAD`, `unknown` without git, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT` overrides it); git itself is asked for the git dir, the common dir and the branch ref, so a linked worktree (whose `.git` is a file) and packed refs rerun the build when HEAD moves. `session.status` gains `versions { engine, commit, protocol, parts: [{kind, name, version, features?, protocol?}], line }` (docs/LIVE_API.md 6.26). A macro (`App::with_kit(kit!(..))`) was the other option; default methods keep every call site unchanged.
- **2026-10-14 — `cargo xtask size-report <app>` merges into the report.** The kart racer's dev overlay said "published size not measured yet" although `target/evani-sizes/size-report.json` already had a kart-racer entry: that dev build (10:27) was made by a bridge started before `EVANI_PUBLISHED_GZ` existed (10:11), so its stamp has no `published_gz` line; the current bridge's stamp includes the size, so its next `serve` rebuilds with it. Separately, measuring one app rewrote the whole file and dropped the others, so `size-report kart-racer` would have cost the sandbox its label. The report now replaces the measured apps' entries in place and keeps the rest (unit test), and a bridge test checks each served game finds its own entry and that a new report changes the build stamp.
- **2026-10-14 — The tier warm-up judges work, and the tier line names the warm-up's pick when another tier is active.** Review captures showed "tier warm-up picked ultra (ultra 16.7 ms)" in the log next to "tier medium". Two things: (1) the mismatch is the capture script (`capture.mjs --moments` runs `tier.set` per moment: drift@high, item_hit@low, finish@medium), and the warm-up's log line simply stays among the last four; the tier line now says `tier medium (set after the warm-up picked ultra)` while the active tier differs from the pick (`QualityState::warmup_pick`). (2) "16.7 ms" was the frame interval, i.e. the 60 Hz refresh, not what Ultra costs: the warm-up still judged the vsync-bound interval that adaptive quality stopped using in the sandbox review. It can't over-pick that way, but a busy browser stretching the interval made it walk down to Low on a GPU with headroom. It now judges CPU + GPU work (`TierWarmup::update_work`; the interval still paces the measurement, and stands in until a GPU time exists), and the log says `(work: ultra 3.2 ms)`. Unit test `warmup_judges_work_not_the_frame_interval`: 3 ms of work at a 33 ms interval keeps the highest tier (the old code went to Low); 40 ms of work at a 16.7 ms interval still ends on Low.

## Gate 0 questions (2026-10-14)

Chris's three questions at the Stage 0 gate: slow recovery after a load, "entities 0" in the kart racer, and Low costing more GPU time than High.

- **2026-10-14 — Fast recovery when work is well under the target.** Recovery after a load took about 22 s (documented as at most 25 s) because every step came back one per upgrade window, each needing a full window of clean frames. When work sits far below the target there is no reason to wait: once work stays under `aq.fast_pct` (default 50) % of the target for `aq.fast_ms` (default 1000 ms), every paused step whose measured cost (scaled to today's work, as before) still fits under the restore line comes back, one per frame. Steps that do not fit, or work between 50 % and the restore line, still use the slow path, so the hysteresis, the 80th-percentile window, disturbance grace and the growing waits for blocked steps are unchanged. Against flapping: if a fast recovery has to be cut again within 10 s (`FAST_HOLD_MS`), the next fast recovery waits twice as long (`fast_backoff`, at most 4 doublings). Unit test `fast_recovery_within_5_s_when_work_is_under_half_the_target`: back in 1.4 to 1.6 s at 30, 60 and 144 Hz; `everything_comes_back_after_a_load_spike` now asserts 5 s instead of 25 s.
- **2026-10-14 — The ECS gains pairs, ordered lists, counts and a reset (additive).** "entities 0" in the kart racer: the kit keeps karts and props in its own `Race` resource. The kit could have used the ECS (`kit.component`, `kit.system`, `World::query`; the example kit spawns and queries `Spinner`s), and nothing in the engine reads entities (the renderer draws from the per-frame draw list), so nothing pushed it there. But a race needed four things the ECS lacked: two entities' components at once (kart-against-kart bumps), a list of entities to loop over while changing the world, an explicit gameplay order (dense iteration is deterministic but a despawn swap-removes), and a one-call reset. Added: `World::get2_mut::<T>(a, b)`, `entities_with::<T>()`, `sorted_entities::<T, K>(key)`, `count::<T>()`, `despawn_all::<T>()` and `SparseSet::get2_mut`; nothing existing changed. The README's "How a kit puts gameplay objects in the ECS" is the rule for kits: every race or level object is an entity with a marker component; per-race settings and scores may stay in a resource. Moving the kart racer is Stage 2 work.
- **2026-10-14 — GPU cost per tier: the bench is the yardstick, and it checks the order.** Chris saw Low cost more GPU than High in the kart racer (2.53 vs 1.72 ms). Mean of 3 bench runs (uncapped, RTX 4060 Ti): Low 0.18, Medium 0.62, High 1.02, Ultra 3.12 ms, so the order holds on the engine. Two causes made Low look expensive in the game. (1) Clock state: a vsync-capped, light frame leaves the card at 210 to 330 MHz instead of about 2.7 GHz, and every pass then reads up to 20 times slower; on the live sandbox at High the forward pass read 1.75 ms with the card idling and 0.08 ms with an extra load keeping it clocked up (`gate0-probe.json`). That is also why the forward pass read 1.76 ms where an earlier run read 0.32 ms at 1280 x 720 with MSAA 4x. (2) Blob shadows: Low has no shadow map, so every lit fragment looped over up to 32 discs; the kart racer pushed 2 per kart plus items. `blob_vis` now returns at once for faces that do not face up and when there are no blobs, and skips a disc outside the fragment's height band or radius before any other maths; the picture is the same (each skipped case contributed exactly 0). Bench Low forward 0.18 -> 0.12 ms with 32 blobs. Kits should still push only the blobs they need (one per kart).
- **2026-10-14 — The cost order check judges medians.** `tier_order_failures` fails a run when a tier's median GPU ms per frame (median of the runs with `--runs N`) is more than 10 % and more than 0.05 ms under the tier below. The median, not the mean, because clock swings and a few slow frames move a mean several times over; the bench also runs uncapped and settles after the last pipeline compile, which keeps the clock up while it measures. The 10 % / 0.05 ms tolerance is about twice the run-to-run noise and the timestamp resolution, far under the real step between tiers.
- **2026-10-14 — The bench settles after pipelines, not only after a frame count.** Low runs at well under 1 ms a frame, so its 120 settle frames ended before the outline and vignette pipelines finished compiling and Low was measured without those passes. Settle now counts frames after the last pipeline compile or swap (`QualityState::disturbances`), at most 10 x `warmup` frames in all.
- **2026-10-14 — A step cut again soon after a restore is "confirmed" and is not probed again while the work is unchanged.** Chris asked whether the no-oscillation tests cover a fast recovery that brings several steps back at once. A multi-step restore under steady moderate load was already stable (`multi_step_fast_restore_then_steady_moderate_load_never_flaps`: all 7 steps back on consecutive frames 1.3 to 1.6 s after the load, then nothing changes for 43 s at 63 % and 75 % of the target, at 30, 60 and 144 Hz). But a restore that only partly fits flapped: the probe brought bloom back at about 24 s (30 Hz) and it was cut again (`a_fast_restore_that_only_partly_fits_cuts_once_then_settles` failed). Now a step cut within 10 s of any restore records its cost as measured then (`StepCost.confirmed`, `restored_at_ms`) and is not probed while the judged work stays at 80 % (`CURRENT_FRACTION`) or more of the work it was cut at; it comes back when the scene gets lighter, or with the doubled wait once the work changes.
- **2026-10-14 — Tier timings are measured on a stress scene that keeps the GPU clocked up.** Pass timings swung 2 to 5 times between captures at the same settings because a light frame lets the card drop to 210 to 375 MHz. `?stress=1` / `cargo xtask bench --stress` draws the same content on every tier (360k triangles of objects with blob shadows plus 500 full-canvas translucent fill layers, `STRESS_FILL_LAYERS`, `--fill N`), so only the tier settings differ. It keeps Chrome's 60 Hz cap: uncapped, the flood of frames starved the timestamp readbacks (5 readings in 600 frames). Each tier measures until it has 600 GPU readings (sampled only when `RenderStats.gpu_samples` moves), per pass the median per reading, then the median of 5 run medians with the min-max spread; nvidia-smi logs the clock every 500 ms. Measured on the RTX 4060 Ti: busy samples at 2760 to 2790 MHz on every tier (Low runs at about 60 % use), per-pass run medians within about 0.01 ms of each other on Low to High (on Ultra one run read shadows and forward about 0.2 ms higher) and totals within 3 % (`.vibedeck/screenshots/gate0/stress-table.txt`). The fill layers dominate the totals, so the table also gives the total without them.
- **2026-10-14 — The review card's tier cost order uses the bench's rule.** `checks::tier_cost_order` calls `evani_bench::order_failures` (each tier at most 10 % or 0.05 ms, whichever is larger, under the one below) on the latest stress `summary.json` when it is newer than the engine sources, else on the review's own bench path medians, and the card names the source. The old rule (each step more than 0.05 ms dearer, on one run's means) failed flat steps such as Medium and High, which share resolution and MSAA (0.79 vs 0.87 ms on the stress scene).
- **2026-10-14 — The overlay shows GPU timings as a 2 s median; telemetry stays raw.** `GpuWindow` (overlay.rs) keeps the last 1024 GPU readings in a fixed ring with 16 pass columns (NaN where a pass did not run; a column with no reading in the window is reused for a new pass name), one sample per new reading whatever the frame rate. The medians are worked out only when the overlay text is (4 times a second), into reused buffers. The total line reads `gpu 1.60 ms (median)` and the passes line ends `(2 s median)`, worded so the live-check parser of the passes line does not read it as a pass. `telemetry.sample`, `session.status` and the bench keep their raw per-frame numbers and averages, so LIVE_API.md is unchanged.

## Automatic process checks (2026-10-14)

Chris asked for five checks to run on every hand-over (KIT_PROCESS items 11 to 15).

- **2026-10-14 — Five checks, one registry.** Each check is one pure function in `tools/xtask/src/checks.rs`; `review.rs` maps each Done-when item to it through `AutoCheck` (a wording hook in the item's text) and `process_info`. Later checks append after item 15 and never renumber.
- **2026-10-14 — The world edge is found with a black and a white clear colour, plus the camera pose.** Sky and fog both follow `render.clear_color`, so the horizon cannot be found from one picture. The same view is rendered twice; pixels that follow the clear colour are background, and the camera pose gives each pixel's angle under the horizon. Background more than a tolerance under the horizon is the edge of the world. It assumes the ground is the plane y = 0 and up is +Y.
- **2026-10-14 — Two additive live changes support it.** `capture.frame` replies with `camera: {eye, target, up, fov_y_deg}` (`CaptureResult.camera`, filled by the renderer from the frame's `RenderWorld.camera`) and `session.status` reports `entities`. No new protocol version: both are new optional fields.
- **2026-10-14 — The overlay is hidden by the capture script, not by an engine flag.** A style rule on `#evani-overlay` and `#evani-panel` for the run, then a DOM probe per capture verifies it and the kit's `[review] hud` selectors. The capture PNGs come from the colour target and never hold DOM, so the HUD is verified in `page-<name>.png` screenshots and the probe.
- **2026-10-14 — The hand-over compares engine commits, not branch heads.** The engine is `crates/` and `assets/tiers/` minus each crate's `Cargo.toml` (every kit edits those). The main line is `main`, else `m1-foundation` (`--main <ref>`).

## Kit process upgrade: target pack, two build passes, critic, playbooks (2026-10-14)

Chris asked for a stricter kit process (STAGES.md Stage 2: the target pack, two build passes, scripted captures, automated checks, an independent critic and the lessons loop). KIT_PROCESS now runs target pack, spec, greybox pass with the playtest bot, art pass, captures and video, automated checks, critic rounds, hand-over, lessons; Done-when items 16 to 28.

- **2026-10-14 — The world-edge check has a hard-edge rule.** The kart title screen showed the end of its ground while the check passed: the title orbit was among the scripted cameras, but the height-scaled reach (250 m) forgave up to 16 degrees of background under the horizon for its 72 m high camera. Background more than 1.5 degrees under the horizon with solid world right below it is now a hard edge at any camera height (over 2 % of the width fails), and the reach before fog may hide the ground is 600 m. A regression test keeps the kart title and orbit renders failing.
- **2026-10-14 — Kit cooperation is a convention, never per-kit code in xtask.** The checks read the target pack in `kits/<id>/target/`, the `[review]` keys (`video`, `internal_resolution` added), the kit tests `playtest_win*` / `playtest_lose*`, a `review_dump` test that writes `$EVANI_REVIEW_DUMP/review.json` (colours and object grounding) and the geocheck tools' `.vibedeck/geocheck/<app>/report.json`. Missing input reads "not run", which is listed on the card and never counts as a pass.
- **2026-10-14 — The card has five states: PASS, FAIL, FLAG, not run, n/a.** FLAG is for a difference a person must judge (visual regression over 10 % of the pixels against the accepted captures); it does not fail the hand-over. The 2D-only checks read n/a when the catalog look is not `style_class` "2d". `cargo xtask review --accept` keeps the showcase as the next baseline after Chris approves.
- **2026-10-14 — The critic is an agent on a packet, judged again by xtask.** `cargo xtask critic <kit>` writes the packet (every picture and video frame, the target pack, the genre playbook, the quality bar, rubric R1 to R11 and the reply format); the orchestrator starts `.claude/agents/kit-critic.md` (Opus, read-only apart from its two report files) with fresh context. Pass: every applicable item 2 or more of 3, mean 2.3 or more, no must-fix items. xtask recomputes the verdict from the scores and the stricter one counts; it refuses a fourth round and says to escalate to Chris.
- **2026-10-14 — Genre knowledge lives in playbooks.** `docs/playbooks/<genre>.md` holds what a genre must get right, each item with its reason; the target pack copies the items that apply and the critic scores them (R8). Human fix rounds per kit are tracked in the ROADMAP Notes, goal 0 to 1.

## Kit 001 pilot of the upgraded process (2026-10-14)

Chris asked for the kart racer to run the full new process; this is the pilot, up to critic round 1.

- **2026-10-14 — The review dump measures the drawn scene, not the layout data.** `review.rs` builds the same statics the game draws (road slabs, shoulders, bank slopes, meadow patches, meadow), ray-casts straight down from every vertex of each object's drawn meshes and takes the smallest gap; karts on the grid, item boxes over a whole 4 s spin, every prop but the mountains, hills and clouds. It found floss bushes floating 13 to 20 cm, 17 trees and tufts buried up to 1.5 m in the road's bank and item box corners dipping 8 cm into the road; all fixed in the kit.
- **2026-10-14 — Item boxes float on purpose and are tagged.** They hover and spin like the genre's boxes; the dump's `intentional` reason skips the gap rule but still needs a contact shadow. The lift went from 0.45 to 0.7 m so the lowest corner of a spinning box clears the road by 0.17 m. Grandstands are tagged too (their footing sits in the bank by design).
- **2026-10-14 — Every kart gets a Low blob.** `BLOB_KARTS` = `MAX_KARTS` (12) with a cap of 16; the item boxes left the blob list for a dark road disc each, so Low still stays within the blob budget. Signs, canes, flags, fence posts and props stand on discs too, and each grandstand on a dark strip.
- **2026-10-14 — A shadow disc is one flat 12-sided cone 5 cm up.** Two stacked discs fought each other, and a disc on the ground fought it far off; one piece held `DISC_LIFT` up has nothing to fight.
- **2026-10-14 — Trees stay on the flat meadow.** A tree or tuft whose footprint reaches the road's bank moves straight out past the bank's foot (`off_bank`); one that would land in a gumdrop mountain or on another stretch of bank is dropped. The full layout loses at most 12 trees; Low keeps every third by seed.
- **2026-10-14 — The meadow sits under the lowest road.** Geocheck found grass fighting the meadow for depth where the road is low, and 1 cm grass patches fighting it past 70 m. The cause of the first was the bank slope's last metre, which met the meadow at a grazing 3.5 degrees (next entry), not the bank top: on this track the lowest road is at 0 m, so the meadow stays at -0.3. The meadow is now `min(-0.3, lowest road - 0.13)` (`Track::meadow_y`), the patches stand 6 cm proud and keep off the bank, the mountains and the trees' feet, and painted lines stand 5.5 cm proud: by geocheck's depth rule (gap = 2.4e-6 x distance squared) each pair stays apart past its 150 m view distance.
- **2026-10-14 — Each bank slope ends on a 6 cm step.** `draw::BANK_FOOT_STEP`: the slope stops 6 cm over the meadow and its slab runs on down into the ground, so no part of a shallow slope lies within depth precision of the meadow; `scenery::bank_reach` keeps trees clear of the shortened slope the same way.
