- **2026-10-14 — A far hill row closes the horizon for the title orbit.** The title and bench orbit flies at about 65 m, above the two hill rows, so sky showed up to 4.5 degrees under the horizon right above the hills (the review's world-edge check allows 1.5). A third row, 22 hazy hills 96 to 130 m tall at 640 m (inside the 1000 m far plane), reaches within 1 degree of the orbit's horizon in every direction it looks (`hills_reach_the_title_orbits_horizon`). Low leaves out the middle row instead (`HILL_ROW_THIN_SKIPS`), so its statics stay under 70k triangles; the near and far rows still close its horizon.
- **2026-10-14 — The playtest bot drives with the pad only.** `playtest_win_podium_finish` races three times with a start boost, the AI line plus a keen player's drifting and items, and finishes first each time; `playtest_lose_off_the_podium` drives on part throttle with no drifts or items and finishes 8th. A 400 s frame cap fails a softlock. Finding: a keen player wins every race on the default AI, so the AI's pace is a tuning question for the critic and Chris, not a bug.
- **2026-10-14 — Seam overlaps wait for the spline mesh.** The road, shoulder, kerb and rail runs are 4 m boxes; geocheck lists their seam overlaps and road slabs sunk into the bank on slopes. `kits/evani-kit-kart-racer/geocheck.toml` tags only the intended cases (goo puddle and boost flame moving through the ground, half-buried gumdrops); the seams stay listed until the runs become one `evani_render::spline::sweep` mesh.
- **2026-10-14 — The track is continuous swept meshes (supersedes the entry above).** `trackmesh.rs` sweeps the road, the grass shoulder with its bank, the kerbs, the rails and the painted lines along the track's own centreline into five meshes (road, grass, red, white, paint) that `draw_system` registers in `MeshLibrary`; the kit turns on `evani-render`'s `meshes` feature for it. The kit sweeps itself rather than calling `spline::sweep` because the cross-section changes from ring to ring (the bank runs out further where the road is high, and on the inside of a hairpin everything stays within 0.9 of the bend's radius so no ring folds). Red and white alternate in 4 m pieces; the paint, the start strip and the grid brackets are decals. Gentle stretches leave out every other ring when nothing moves more than 5 cm (`trackmesh::SAG`), which keeps Low's statics at 68k triangles (limit 70k); all meshes share the same rings, so their seams meet. Roadside props that would stand past the narrowed shoulder on the inside of a hairpin are left out. Road and grass are one colour each now (no per-segment shade).
- **2026-10-14 — The kart walls follow the drawn rail.** On the inside of the two hairpins the swept rail comes in (`trackmesh::rail_lat`, within 0.9 of the bend's radius), so the karts' wall does too (`Track::side_cap` in `kart.rs`): a kart stops at the rail it sees, never inside it or short of it. Geocheck's kart adapter still assumes one wall distance all round; its two hairpin probes are tagged with that reason.
- **2026-10-14 — Grandstand steps are plain boxes.** A bevelled box 20 m long slopes its top away under the crowd at the ends; plain boxes keep the steps flat and reach down to just over the meadow. The icing runs 7 cm past each step end (2 cm left the end faces within depth precision of the step's at 150 m).
- **2026-10-14 — Geocheck's structure and object size are 24 m for this kit.** A grandstand is 20 m long; with the default 10 m structure and 12 m object size its steps and back wall counted as structures and each stand split into many objects. The road, grass and mountains are still far longer than 24 m.
- **2026-10-14 — Ground patches, shadow discs, the stand strips and the start chequers are decals.** Anything lying flat on another surface is drawn with `draw_decal` (depth offset) instead of standing a few centimetres proud, so nothing fights for depth far off; `scenery::is_disc` marks them. The kerbs reach 2 cm in over the road's edge (`trackmesh::KERB_IN`) and 2 cm down into the shoulder (`SEAT`), and the grass reaches 10 cm up under the road (`UNDER_ROAD`), so no two track meshes share a face plane.

## Kit 002 2d-platformer core additions (2026-10-14)

Three small, additive renderer changes the 2D platformer needed (SPEC §6). Built-in atlas cells, the bench hot path and every existing kit are unchanged; the one new public struct field is `RenderWorld.target_px` (`RenderWorld` is built with `Default`, so no literal breaks).

- **2026-10-14 — A kit's own sprite sheet is the `SpriteSheet` resource.** `{ width, height, rgba8, frames: Vec<[x, y, w, h]>, revision }`, optional. When a valid sheet is present the atlas texture grows to `max(256, width) × (256 + height)`: the built-in 4 × 4 cells stay in the top-left 256 × 256 square and the sheet sits below (`atlas::SHEET_ORIGIN`). Sprite `cell` 16 + i draws frame i (`SpriteSheet::cell(i)`); a cell past the table wraps onto the built-in cells as before. The uv table (`shaders::sprite_uv_table`) is built only when the sheet or the filter changes, so packing stays one table lookup per sprite. The atlas is rebuilt when `revision` changes, so animation (palette cycling) uses extra frames, not re-uploads. A mirrored sprite is just a negative `Sprite.size[0]` (the sprite pipeline doesn't cull).
- **2026-10-14 — `render.pixel_art` (bool, default false).** A second sampler (nearest) and a second atlas bind group; the sprite pass picks one per frame. With it on, sheet frames use exact edges (every sample hits a texel centre inside the frame); off, they keep the half-texel inset that stops linear bleed. Pixel looks turn it on with `post_param`; kits read it to pick whole-number scaling. After merging main (2026-10-14) there is still one `render.pixel_art` param: it switches both this sampler and the canvas CSS `image-rendering` described in the sandbox review fixes.
- **2026-10-14 — `RenderWorld.target_px`.** The render target size in device pixels, set by the renderer next to `viewport_css` (zero natively and before the first GPU frame). A 2D kit divides it by its art height to draw every art pixel as the same whole number of device pixels.
- **2026-10-14 — The title screen plays a recorded attract demo instead of a camera pan (SPEC §2 changed).** At start the kit runs its lookahead bot (`bot.rs`: wait, run, jump and run-up plans tried on game clones 72 ticks ahead, any plan that loses a life rejected, the current plan kept on ties) through Bramble Hills once and records its input, with a game snapshot every 120 ticks. Playback is a pure function of `Time::render_seconds()`, so captures at held sim times are exact: 0 s shows the first jump at its apex, 20 s the stomp nearest the middle of the run (4 ticks after it: puff and score pop up), 40 s the castle gate (40 ticks after the win); the ticks between are played at the even speed that joins them (about 0.8x to 1.4x), the gate is held 4 s, then it loops (44 s). The demo keeps the tuning it was recorded with, so live changes never desync it; it is silent, and it shows the kit HUD like an arcade attract mode. Cost: about 0.1 s natively in release at start-up.
- **2026-10-14 — Whole-pixel scale also keeps 256 art pixels across.** `scale = max(1, min(round(target_h / view_height_px), floor(target_w / 256)))` with `render.pixel_art` on, so a portrait phone still sees ahead (the level then sits on the screen bottom with sky above).
- **2026-10-14 — Pixel art renders at full device pixels on every tier.** With `render.pixel_art` on, the renderer sizes its target to CSS size x devicePixelRatio (`control::render_size_exact`) and ignores the tier DPR cap, the tier render scale and the adaptive-quality scale. Any of those would resample the frame by a fraction (Low's 0.75 turns a 6x art pixel into 4.5 device pixels), which breaks the one-grid rule (Chris's review of kit 002, point 6). The cost is small for a 2D game (a few hundred sprites, no 3D passes); the tiers still differ by effects (parallax, particles, MSAA off). `capture.frame` accepts widths up to 4096 so a review can take a 1920 x 1080 capture with no downscale, and `tools/live-check/capture.mjs --viewport 1920x1080` sets the page size for it; captures.json now carries the tier and the engine commit of each capture.
- **2026-10-15 — The platformer's whole-pixel scale shows 224 to 288 rows, and the view is framed past 640 columns (fix 002, replaces the `round(target_h / 224)` rule above).** `s = ceil(h / view_height_max_px)` (288), or `floor(h / view_height_px)` (224) when that would show fewer than 224 rows; still at most `floor(w / 256)` and at least 1. Rounding to 224 picked the bigger scale whenever it was closer, so 1080p showed only 216 rows at 5x and a 3440 x 1440 screen at 125 % Windows scaling (3439 x 1389 device px) 231 rows at 6x: big, chunky pixels that looked "zoomed in" (Chris). Now 1080p is 4x (270 rows), 1440p 5x (288), that screen 5x (278). Exactly 224 rows at a whole scale fits only heights that divide by 224, so the catalog's "320 × 224 internal" is read as "about 224": the view shows a little more rather than cropping or scaling unevenly. A view wider than `view_width_max_px` (640) is centred on whole device pixels with black bars, and `wake_distance` went 320 -> 336 so no enemy is seen asleep at the edge of the widest view. Sprites map each axis by its own CSS-to-device ratio (`css[i] / target[i]`) instead of one ratio from the height.
- **2026-10-15 — `evani-render` feature `pixel-art`: the canvas backing follows the exact device-pixel box.** With it on and `render.pixel_art` set, the renderer observes the canvas's `device-pixel-content-box` and sizes the backing store to `devicePixelContentBoxSize`. At a fractional devicePixelRatio, `round(css x dpr)` can be a pixel off the box the browser actually composites; the browser then stretches the canvas by that pixel and one row or column of art pixels doubles. The box is used only when it is within 1.5 px of css x dpr (device emulation, as in DevTools and headless captures, reports it unscaled) and was measured at the current devicePixelRatio; otherwise, and in browsers without the box, it falls back to `render_size_exact`. A cargo feature, turned on by the 2D platformer kit, so games that don't use it get none of the code.

## Live code swapping (2026-10-14)

- **2026-10-14 — Live code swapping uses subsecond and a pinned dx from git main.** `subsecond` is pinned `=0.7.10` (MIT OR Apache-2.0) behind evani-core's `hotpatch` feature, which only evani-live's default feature turns on, so it rides in with each game's dev-only `live` feature and never reaches a shipped build (`cargo xtask build --ship` and `size-report` fail if it does). The builder is dx from dioxus git main at rev `f420ca3d6cb6812ed27a021e239d7ec0941ff317` (`cargo xtask dx-install`), because released dx 0.7.x can't read this workspace's wasm-bindgen 0.2.129 output; it is an alpha pin, so a dx update is a deliberate change re-proved with `tools/live-check/code-swap.mjs` on the kart racer and the sandbox. The bridge counts each patch's `ifunc_count` from its element section itself (dx main undercounts it), and falls back to Trunk when dx is missing or fails.
- **2026-10-14 — windows-sys `=0.61.2` in the live bridge, for a job object.** The bridge puts dx and everything dx starts (cargo, rustc, wasm-bindgen) in a kill-on-close job object, so closing or killing the bridge never leaves a compiler running on the shared PC. Pinned exactly like the other platform crates; Windows only, and only in the dev bridge.

## AI guidance ships with the engine (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — The engine owns a version-stamped block in AGENTS.md.** The rules an AI agent can't work out itself (the check gate, `new-game`, build-time on/off, the catalog, determinism, the tools in the order to use them) live in `tools/evani-guide/guide/` and are written between `BEGIN`/`END` markers carrying the engine version and a body hash, so `doctor` can tell missing, stale (older engine) and hand-edited blocks apart and refresh only the block, never the text around it. CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md and `.cursor/rules/evani.mdc` are thin pointers (imports, no copies); per-folder AGENTS.md files under `apps/<id>/`, `kits/` and `looks/` hold the short local rules. Copilot's file is opt-in (`--with copilot`) because this project keeps no `.github/` folder. Kept under about 5 KB, following the prior-art finding that long, generic context files make agents worse.
- **2026-10-15 — A separate light crate, `evani-guide`, no new external dependencies.** It uses only serde_json, toml and evani-lookguard (default features off), all already in the workspace. xtask (`init-ai`, `doctor`, `check`, `new-game`, `ai-eval`) and the live bridge (`evani_check` MCP tool) both call it, and a future standalone `evani` CLI can too. The Claude Code Stop hook runs it directly (`cargo run -p evani-guide --target-dir target/guide`) rather than through xtask, which pulls in wgpu through evani-bench and would take minutes to build at hook time. The hook only checks games the session's edits touched, counts review and geocheck evidence only when it is newer than the game's sources (older evidence is a "stale, re-run" warning), and lets the session stop after three blocks so it can't trap an agent.
- **2026-10-15 — The `evani-live` MCP server runs a prebuilt binary.** `.mcp.json` used `cargo run`, and the bridge's sources change almost daily, so most session starts rebuilt it (40 to 150 s at two jobs) against Claude Code's start-up timeout of about 30 s: the cause of the CONNECT_TIMEOUT. `doctor --fix` now builds it once into `target/mcp` and copies it to `.evani/bin/` (gitignored) with a source-hash stamp; `check` and `doctor` warn when the stamp is stale. A locked old `.exe` on Windows is renamed aside first. The path stays relative because `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` isn't expanded in `.mcp.json` commands.

## Kit 002 free roam (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — Free roam for the 2D platformer, kept inside the kit.** Chris asked to start Stage 2 kit work now so Fennel's Leap can be a playground. The kit reads `PlayMode` once when it is built (`Game::set_free_roam`) and the simulation does the rest, so Play mode runs the exact same code path as before and the attract demo stays a Play-mode demo. Under `FreeRoam`: the game starts straight in the level (no title; "back to title" is a fresh walk); the level timer does not run; the castle gate is ignored (no win screen, the run goes on); enemies are part of the world but cannot hurt (stomping still works); a pit, water, spikes or the map's edge put the hero back, free, on the last "safe" ground (solid or plank ground, not a moving platform, floor on both sides, nothing deadly under) or on the last checkpoint flag touched, blinking briefly; the HUD hides lives, timer and goal text and shows a small FREE ROAM label (six new 3 x 5 letters in the mini font, F R E O A M) in the timer's place. Each fail moment in Play mode (a life lost, the timer running out, the run ending by game over or by the gate) is counted by the game and handed to `PlayOutcomes` by `platformer.sim` every tick, so `free_roam_test` fails if any of them ever fires in free roam. The gate counts as the run ending (`game_over`) because `PlayOutcomes` documents "won-and-over" as a game over; it is what catches a regression that makes the gate end a free-roam run. The tuning params keep their existing names `kit.platformer.*` (owner `kit.2d-platformer`); free roam adds none, since it has no number worth tuning. No engine crate changed. Size: the shipped 2d-platformer goes from 308.5 KB to 309.3 KB gzip (+0.8 KB, 0.26%): the free-roam code, six glyphs and the outcome hand-over.

## Kit 001 free roam (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — Free roam for the kart racer: the player alone on the track, no rivals.** The kit reads `PlayMode` once when it is built (`Race::free_roam`); under `FreeRoam` the race starts already in the `Racing` phase with a grid of one kart (`reset_roam`), so Play mode runs the same code as before. Rivals are left out rather than driving as ambient traffic: the AI, its rubber band and its laps are all race logic, and a rival that finishes its three laps would stop. A grid of one also makes the item draw use the leader's odds and the Seeker Star fly straight (no target). Dropped in free roam: countdown and GO, checkpoint gates and laps, the finish and podium, the 15-minute limit, wrong-way detection and the title (start, restart and "back to the title" all mean a fresh start on the track). Kept: item boxes and every item, drifting and boosts, bumps against walls. Nothing needs an off-track reset: the road is walled, a wall pushes back as in Play. The HUD hides place, lap and time for a small FREE ROAM label and a 12 s controls line (there is no title screen to show the keys); the pause menu says "Back to the start" and hides "Quit to title". A race that ends (all humans over the line, or the 15-minute limit) now reports `game_over` (and `timer_expired` for the limit) on `PlayOutcomes` in Play mode, which is what `free_roam_test` watches. No new params (free roam has nothing to tune), no engine crate changed. Size: the shipped kart-racer goes from 387.7 KB to 389.1 KB gzip (+1.4 KB, 0.36%).

## Library naming (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — A playable, remixable game in the library is a playground (Chris's choice, replaces “biome”).** It is a complete single-player game made with Evani.Dev that anyone can play as made, free roam, or copy and change (remove things, add things, build levels). Picked over Starter games, Templates and plain Games; the options and the site wording are in docs/PLAYGROUNDS.md. Kit, look and model keep their meanings. Only the words people read change now; code identifiers and the library's internal kind value stay until Chris confirms a rename.

## Every playground supports free roam (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — Chris's rule: "make sure games do support free roam when building for Evani.app website and uploading a playground".** A game for the Evani.app library is marked `[playground]` in its `evani.toml` and must say `free_roam = true`, have a kit that returns true from `KitPlugin::free_roam`, and have a `#[test]` that calls `evani_core::play::free_roam_test`. `cargo xtask check` (and the MCP `evani_check`, both through evani-guide) prints an ERROR with a `fix:` line for each gap, `cargo xtask build <id> --ship` refuses to build, and review card item 29 checks it. The rule ships in the evani-guide AGENTS block and the kits and game notes, so every AI that uses Evani.Dev gets it. docs/PLAYGROUNDS.md has the details.
- **2026-10-15 — The engine owns the mode, the kit owns what it drops.** `evani_core::play::PlayMode { Play (default), FreeRoam }` is a world resource set in `App::new` (on the web from `mode=free` in `location.search` or `location.hash`, matching the site's `?mode=free` preview and `#mode=free` demo frame; anything else is Play) or with `App::with_play_mode` before the kit is built, after which it is fixed (a later change is logged as an error). A kit that doesn't return true from `KitPlugin::free_roam` is played normally, with a note, so a game can never claim a mode it doesn't have. `PlayOutcomes` counts the fail events kits report (game over, timer expired, life lost) in every mode, which is what the test helper asserts on; a kit that ends a run some other way is caught by a person or the critic. Additive: a default trait method and two resources, no new dependency (only web-sys's `Location` feature in evani-core, read once through `location.href`). Measured ship growth, all in core with every module unchanged: sandbox +1.8 KB raw / +0.8 KB gzip, kart-racer +2.2 / +0.8, 2d-platformer +1.7 / +0.6 (about 0.2%); reading the address is about 0.3 KB gzip of that. The test helper is native only, so it never reaches a download.
- **2026-10-15 — New games start as playgrounds; existing ones wait for Stage 2.** `new-game` writes `[playground] free_roam = true`, and the example kit (no goals or fail state) supports free roam with its `free_roam_holds` test, so a new game is compliant from the start. The sandbox, kart-racer and 2d-platformer stay unmarked: free roam for the kart and platformer gameplay is Stage 2 kit work, filed in ROADMAP.md and STAGES.md.

## Playgrounds: no world size, 10 MB package (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — Chris redefined playgrounds.** His words (relayed by Evani.App): "A play ground is a small interactive example of what can be created with Evani.dev Engine and Evani.app library. no longer limited by size ... at the moment it is 10mb" total file size. So a playground has no world-size requirement (the Small 25 m, Medium 50 m and Large 100 m sizes are dropped), and the whole uploaded game (wasm, js, html, assets) is capped at 10 MB. The free roam rule is unchanged. Enforcement: `cargo xtask build <id> --ship` prints the total for a `[playground]` game and fails with an ERROR and a `fix:` line when the ship folder is over 10 × 1024 × 1024 bytes, measured uncompressed on disk because that is what is uploaded; `cargo xtask check` warns when the last ship build is over. The limit is the constant `PACKAGE_CAP_BYTES` in evani-guide's `playground.rs`; Evani.App enforces the same number. docs/PLAYGROUNDS.md and the guidance block say so. No new dependency.

## The Evani.dev tab never looks stuck (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — The page reports its own state; the bridge answers `paused` for it.** A hidden or suspended tab runs no animation frames, and the page answers live commands only between frames, so every command waited out its 10 s limit while `/live/status` still said connected. The page (evani-live, dev only) now sends `page_state` (frame counter, `document.visibilityState`, Evani.App's `window.__evaniHost`) on every change and as a 1 s `setInterval` heartbeat, which keeps running when frames stop. The bridge calls a page paused only when it has a reason (Evani.App's `paused` text, or a hidden document) AND is stalled (silent past 2.5 s, or no frames between two beats), and then answers every command at once with error code `paused` and a fix line ("show the Evani.dev tab"), checking again every 100 ms for a command already sent. Both halves are needed: Evani.App now parks a hidden tab at full rate, so a reason alone would refuse a page that answers fine, and a silent visible page is a long task, not a pause. LIVE_API.md 3.5.
- **2026-10-15 — Parked pages draw about 5 fps; the simulation doesn't slow.** While `__evaniHost.shown` is false the renderer skips frames to at most one per 200 ms (`RenderPace` in evani-core's dev-only `capture` feature, checked at the top of `Renderer::frame`). The fixed 60 Hz step, live commands and the heartbeat still run on every frame callback, so determinism and answers are unchanged. Captures, a capture camera override, readbacks in flight and bench / compare / sweep runs always draw at full rate. Outside Evani.App nothing changes, and ship builds (no `capture` feature) don't contain it.
- **2026-10-15 — Baked data no longer reaches dx's dependency list.** The 339 s "code update" after a look bake was 8 small patches (params.json, bake-manifest.json, CHANGES.md and `.git`) queued behind a hidden tab that ran no frames to apply them. Cause: `tools/bake-index.rs` tracked the whole crate folder (`rerun-if-changed=.`) and `include_str!`'d the data files, and evani-core's build script tracked git files, so dx rebuilt and patched for every non-Rust file a bake or `git restore` touched, and a data-only patch changed nothing anyway (baked values are applied once at start). In hot mode the bridge now gives dx `EVANI_BAKED_STAMP` (an FNV hash of every look's and kit's baked files at dx start) and `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT`; with them set the build scripts write the data as literals and track no data or git file. The bridge keeps the files as built and injects the ones that changed into `/` (`<script id="evani-live-baked">`), and the page applies its own kit's and look's at start through the new `evani_core::app::apply_pending_baked`, so a reload shows the bake. Trunk and ship builds keep reading and tracking the files as before.
- **2026-10-15 — A stuck code patch reloads the page.** Patches are applied at the top of a frame; one that is neither applied nor failed 15 s after it was sent while the page ran 60 frames reloads the page (it gets the patch afresh), and a second stall drops the patch. A page running no frames applies a ready patch from its heartbeat instead, so a parked or briefly hidden tab no longer piles patches up. No new dependencies.

## Evani.Model track (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — The Evani.Model track started (Chris's brief, docs/EVANI_MODEL_BRIEF.md, placed between Stage 2 and Stage 3 in STAGES.md).** Evani.Model is an original, AI-only 3D modeler with its own geometry engine, driven by MCP tools. M0 (research and planning) is on the `evani-model` branch: clean-room notes and the known / ours / why / result table in docs/model_research/, design drafts docs/EVANI_MODEL.md and docs/MODEL_API.md, the benchmark set in tests/model_bench/ (30 intents, manifest, kart reference plan), the baseline, M1, hardware-matrix and originality-scan plans, and the decisions Chris must make before M1 (docs/model_research/plan-decisions.md). No code yet; M1 starts after Chris approves M0.
- **2026-10-15 — Where it lives.** `crates/evani-model` (library) and `tools/evani-model` (native command line and stdio MCP server); development-only, only compiled models (and later, optionally, recipes) reach players. It never modifies evani-core or evani-render; engine needs go to CORE_REQUESTS.md. It integrates through glTF, evani-convert (Stage 3) and geocheck.
- **2026-10-15 — Dependency rule.** Evani.Model uses only wgpu, glam, bytemuck and serde unless Chris approves more, each with a one-line reason recorded here. Pending requests (plan-decisions.md): native wgpu backends for the dev tool only (D1), serde_json and the already-pinned rmcp and tokio for the MCP server (D2). External tools (meshoptimizer, xatlas, CoACD, TRELLIS.2, the Khronos glTF-Validator, Blender) are run separately as benchmark baselines or checks only, never linked or copied; external generators (TRELLIS.2, OmniPart) may only be optional plug-in services behind our own interface.
- **2026-10-15 — Clean-room rule.** A research agent reads papers, standards, articles and other projects' documentation, and writes design notes in its own words, with no code, into docs/model_research/. Builder agents implement only from the brief and those notes and never open third-party source code for the functionality they build. No third-party source files or model weights live in the repository. Standards (glTF 2.0, KHR_mesh_quantization, the MikkTSpace convention, WGSL) are implemented ourselves. Before each milestone sign-off a licence and code-similarity scan (plan-m1.md, test I) runs against the referenced projects; any match is investigated and rewritten.
- **2026-10-15 — M0 signed off; dependency requests approved.** Chris answered "Proceed with all recommendations" and "Yes, sign off and start step 1": every decision in plan-decisions.md is decided as recommended, including native wgpu backends for the dev tool only (D1) and serde_json with the already-pinned rmcp and tokio for its MCP server (D2). D7 (renting test computers) is approved in principle; each rental is confirmed with Chris before money is spent. M1 starts.
- **2026-10-15 — M1 outcomes of D1, D2 and D17 (no new crate).** The "pending requests" above are settled; M1 added no third-party crate and Cargo.lock gained nothing. D1 (native wgpu backends for the dev tool only) is approved but unused in M1: M1 is the deterministic CPU path, and the `gpu` feature arrives in M2. D2 was taken as its lighter alternative: the MCP server in `tools/evani-model` is a small hand-written JSON-RPC loop over stdio on the already-pinned serde_json, so rmcp and tokio are not used by Evani.Model. D17 is option A: the `meshcheck` module in evani-model (std only, plain arrays in, sorted issues out, spatial-grid self-intersection test); geocheck's checker is only a cross-check from tools/model-bench-ref, and moving one shared checker into a tiny crate stays Chris's later call.
- **2026-10-16 — M1 signed off; the standard SHA-256 code is kept.** Asked whether to sign off Evani.Model's first milestone and keep the standard fingerprint code as it is, Chris answered "Sign it off and keep the code", and on the card about the SHA-256 routine "Yes, keep it as it is". Evidence: docs/model_research/report-m1.md, review-m1.md (approve with conditions, conditions met) and originality-m1.md (pass with notes; the SHA-256 round matches the published standard, as any correct implementation does, and is not rewritten). With the sign-off, the `evani-model` branch is cleared to merge into `m1-foundation` (this entry arrives there with that merge); Evani.Model stays dev-only and reaches no game. M2 does not start until Chris asks.

- **2026-10-16 — MC1: colour, skinning and characters extend the brief (approved by Chris with the MC1 sign-off, 2026-10-16).** The Evani.Model brief says "no colour"; plan-characters.md section 1 records the extension. Character templates now write per-vertex region colours (COLOR_0, linear values converted from sRGB by our own maths), a T-pose skeleton with skin weights (joint nodes, one skin, JOINTS_0/WEIGHTS_0, FLOAT positions for skinned meshes) and anchors on joints; models without regions or a skeleton write the same bytes as before (the 30 benchmark items are unchanged). No new crate. First character: the frontier marshal (tests/model_bench/characters/, not in the benchmark set).

## Render paths are reported, and core requests belong to their stage (2026-10-15)

- **Chris's rule.** A core request that belongs to a later stage is filed in that stage (a row in CORE_REQUESTS.md with status `filed for Stage N (Chris, 2026-10-15)` and a line under that stage's Includes in STAGES.md) and does not count as open for an earlier gate. Asked whether Gate 2's "No open core requests" counts rows filed to a later stage, he answered: "Yes, move them to their later stages."
- **Design.** `assets/render_paths.json` says, for each style class (2d, pseudo-3d, 3d) and tier, whether the path's base pass exists (`runs`), which parts of `catalog/tiers.json` `render_paths` are missing, and where each missing part is filed (`deferred`: stage and who builds it). `evani_render::render_path(tier, styles) -> RenderPathInfo` (crates/evani-render/src/render_path.rs) holds the same table as constants, so there is no JSON parse and nothing new in a ship download (2d-platformer ship build identical before and after: 667.8 KB raw, 287.4 KB gzip wasm). The test `table_matches_assets_json` keeps the two equal and `render_path_matches_catalog_for_every_tier_and_style` checks them against the catalog. evani-guide stays light (no engine crates), so it reads the JSON.
- **The check.** `cargo xtask check` (and MCP `evani_check`) gives an ERROR "render path" with a `fix:` line when the style of a game's look has no base pass on Low, Medium, High or Ultra (today every pseudo-3d look). The look decides the path, not the genre: kart-racer lists `pseudo-3d-road` among its cameras but ships the 3D `toon` look. VR is reported as a note per game and never an error, because the VR tier exists only inside an immersive session and reads unavailable otherwise (Stage 0; real VR is Stage 9). `cargo xtask render-paths <app>` and the top of `cargo xtask build <app>` print one line per tier.
- **Filing.** GPU-driven culling, four cascades, volumetric passes and temporal upscale are filed to Stage 8 rather than Stage 7, because STAGES.md lists GPU compute selection and indirect draws there; the pseudo-3D pass is filed to Stage 6 (the first Phase B kit that needs it); compute particles stay in the particles row (Stage 2, plan item C). No new dependencies.

## Sprite art: look- and kit-supplied sheets with mipmaps (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — A new `evani-render` feature, `sprite-art`, off by default.** CORE_REQUESTS plan item A. A kit that draws named art turns it on (like `pixel-art`); games that don't build the same atlas, sprite shader and sampler as before (the sprite WGSL is split into `sprite.wgsl` plus a fragment file, and without the feature the two concatenate to the old text byte for byte). The only change outside the feature is the additive `GeneratedAsset::SpriteSheet` variant in evani-core, so looks (which don't depend on the renderer) can supply art through their generators.
- **2026-10-15 — Our own deterministic shelf packer, no crate.** Prior art: .vibedeck/research/2026-10-15-sprite-atlas-packing-gutters-and-mipmaps.md (stb_rect_pack's idea, no code copied; etagere and guillotiere rejected as new dependencies built for dynamic atlases). One frame list in a fixed order (engine built-in cells, the kit's `SpriteSheet` frames, then named frames), sorted within each source by padded height, width and name, so a kit can't move the engine's cells; first-fit shelves; the atlas grows 256x256, 512x256, 512x512 ... up to the device's `max_texture_dimension_2d`; what doesn't fit is an `AtlasFull` error in the overlay (named art is dropped first, then the kit's sheet), never a panic. The name table is a sorted `Vec` searched by binary search. Named cells start at `ART_CELL0` (0x8000) in the order sheets were added, so a cell never changes once given; on a shared name the look's frame beats the kit's.
- **2026-10-15 — Four mip levels, built on the CPU per frame.** Sprites rarely draw below an eighth of their art size, so the chain stops at level 3 and the gutter is 2^(4-1) = 8 px of extruded edge pixels (not transparent padding); padded cells start on the 8 px grid, so every texel of every level belongs to one frame and nothing bleeds. The box filter averages alpha-weighted linear colour with the gamma 2.0 shortcut (square, square root; no `powf`), clamps at the edge for odd sizes, and is bit-exact in native tests. One `write_texture` per level; the linear sampler blends levels (`MipmapFilterMode::Linear`, anisotropy 1, only with the feature), level 0 keeps the half-texel inset.
- **2026-10-15 — The atlas stays straight alpha; the sprite blend is unchanged.** Averaging is premultiplied (so clear texels add no colour), but the result is stored un-premultiplied, and fully clear texels next to coverage take their neighbours' colour (one dilation pass at level 0), so bilinear filtering never pulls in invisible colour. Switching the atlas and the blend to premultiplied would have changed every existing game's sprites for no gain here.
- **2026-10-15 — Pixel art keeps level 0.** A pixel-art sheet (`pixel_art: true`) gets no gutter and no filtering; its uv rectangle is moved by 2.0 on u and the sprite shader (feature build only) reads it with `textureSampleLevel` at level 0 on the texel centre, so a crisp sheet can sit next to smooth art. With `render.pixel_art` on, the nearest bind group sees a level-0-only view of the atlas. An atlas of only pixel-art frames has one level.
- **2026-10-15 — Look art comes from the look's `sprites` generator, added when the game starts.** The render module calls it in `start` (after every plugin is built, before the first frame), at a requested edge of 128 px; a look without it supplies nothing and kits draw their built-in fallbacks. The GPU rebuilds the atlas when the `SpriteArt` revision or the kit's `SpriteSheet` revision changes.

## Touch controls: one engine layer, kits opt in (2026-10-15)

- **Why.** The Stage 2 device test on Chris's phone and tablet (`.vibedeck/screenshots/stage2-devices/results.md`, "Other findings") showed the kart racer and the 2D platformer can't be played without a keyboard. Several kits need on-screen controls, so the engine has them once. Prior art: `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-15-on-screen-touch-controls-for-a-kart-racer-and-a-2d.md` (a hand-built Pointer Events + CSS layer; Godot's "a control only sets a named action"; nipplejs's dynamic stick, dead zone and axis lock). Ideas only, no code copied, no new dependencies.
- **2026-10-15 — The runtime is Rust (web-sys), not shared page JS.** Both are deterministic when touch is queued and applied once per frame, so the choice was size and surface. Rust wins: the logic (finger tracking, stick maths, quantisation, the touch-only rule) is plain Rust with native unit tests; the page side is about 300 lines of web-sys calls already in evani-core's binding set (no new web-sys features); nothing is added to any page, there is no second file to keep in step and no wasm export for JS to call. A JS file would be a few hundred bytes smaller but needs an exported bridge, a copy step in every game's Trunk page and browser-only tests.
- **2026-10-15 — A feature of evani-core (`touch`), switched on by the kit, not an `evani.toml` module.** The layer is a kit's gameplay contract (which action each control drives), like `capture`/`focus` it is a core feature rather than a crate, and the kit crate belongs to one game. A kit that doesn't turn it on compiles none of it: the `Input` touch slots, the `TouchControls` resource and the DOM shell are all `#[cfg(feature = "touch")]`. Ship sizes of kart-racer, 2d-platformer and match-3 are unchanged (measured before and after: wasm 834.4, 668.0 and 659.8 KB raw; 355.2, 287.4 and 290.4 KB gzip). Opting in costs about +8.7 KB gzip (measured on a throwaway kart build with the README layout: wasm +18.2 KB raw, +8.5 KB gzip; JS glue +0.2 KB gzip).
- **2026-10-15 — Controls set actions; values are quantised.** `Input` gets one touch slot per action (`set_touch(action, q)` with `q` in -127..127, and `tap_touch` for a one-tick press edge); `Input::update` merges it with the bindings like one more binding (largest magnitude wins), with the same edge latching as keys, so a hold is the key frame for frame and a sub-frame tap is never lost. 127 reads as exactly 1.0. Several controls on one action add up and clamp (left -1 + right +1 = 0, as `keys:KeyA/KeyD`).
- **2026-10-15 — Shown only on touch.** Start from `(pointer: coarse)`; the first touch `pointerdown` shows it; any key or gamepad input hides it and lets go; blur, a hidden tab and Esc release everything; the kit can switch it off for menus. It never requests fullscreen or pointer lock. `hold_while_shown` (auto-accelerate) is an action held while the layer is in use, so even that reaches the sim as an action.

## Text in the renderer: a small bitmap glyph core (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — A new `evani-render` feature, `text`, off by default, turning on `sprite-art`.** CORE_REQUESTS plan item B. A kit that draws text turns it on; a game that doesn't downloads none of the font or the layout. Measured on the 2d-platformer ship build (fx, parallax, shake, physics-simple; same path for every build): off, 287.4 KB wasm gzip, 309.4 KB total gzip; with `sprite-art` alone 294.7 / 316.7 KB (+7.3 KB wasm); with `text` and one `text::draw` call 296.9 / 318.9 KB (+9.5 KB wasm over off, of which the font and layout are +2.2 KB over `sprite-art`). The plan's "3 to 5 KB" is for the font and layout; the rest is the packer and mip code that item A made necessary, which any game with named art pays. The wasm and js of the 2d-platformer and kart-racer ship builds are byte-identical before and after this change (same SHA-1), and their markers hold no `evani.text.` marker.
- **2026-10-15 — One original 5 x 9 font drawn as text art, two baked sizes.** The glyphs (`GLYPHS` in text.rs, ASCII 32 to 126) are designed here, in the same text-art form as the platformer's digits; no font file or table was copied. Caps and digits are 7 rows tall, lowercase has a 5-row x-height, descenders use rows 7 and 8. The small size is the bitmap as drawn (line height 10 px, advance 6); the large one is the same bitmap through Scale2x (EPX), which rounds the stairs of diagonals without inventing shapes (line height 20 px, advance 12). `size_px` picks the variant whose scale is nearest 1 in ratio (ties small) and scales that, so 10, 20 and their multiples are never resampled; the quads of other sizes are scaled and sampled crisply (nearest).
- **2026-10-15 — Fill and outline frames, not baked colours.** Each glyph is baked twice per size as white ink: `glyph.<code>` and `glyph.<code>.outline` (the ink grown by the outline width, 1 px small and 2 px large, first step 8 neighbours, later steps 4 neighbours so the large outline has rounded corners); the large size adds `.x2`. The draw tints them, so one baked set serves every colour. The drop shadow reuses the outline frame when there is an outline, the fill frame when not, offset by one outline width. A draw pushes all shadows, then all outlines, then all fills on one layer (the sprite sort is stable), so a neighbour's outline never covers a letter. Space draws no sprite. The cost is up to three sprites per character.
- **2026-10-15 — The font sheet is an engine sheet added first, so cells are constants.** `RenderModule::build` adds it to `SpriteArt` before anything else can (engine sources pack first, cells go in the order sheets were added), so the cell of frame `i` is `ART_CELL0 + i` and `text::draw(&mut RenderWorld, ...)` needs no resource, exactly the API the plan asks for. The test `frames_are_named_and_cells_are_constants` checks every glyph's name against its constant cell, and `the_render_module_puts_the_font_first` checks it through the module. The frames are pixel-art frames (crisp, no gutter, one mip level; the sprite shader already reads them on texel centres), so no shader changed and the WGSL tests are untouched. In an atlas with smooth art the 8 px alignment of the packer pads each 7 x 11 and 14 x 22 cell; the whole font still packs into 512 x 512 or less (test `the_font_packs_into_a_small_atlas`).
- **2026-10-15 — Whole-pixel layout, `?` for the rest.** The advance is the design advance times the scale, rounded to a whole pixel (at least 1); the start of the line is `pos.x` minus 0, a half or all of the width by `align`, rounded; `pos.y` is the top of the line, rounded. The size is `chars * advance` by the rounded line height (outline and shadow ink may reach one outline width past it). Any character outside ASCII 32 to 126, a newline and a tab included, draws `?`; line breaks, wrapping and widgets are Stage 3's text and UI layer, which grows from this API.
- **2026-10-15 — The size tools count the font by a marker.** The font carries `evani.text.font_5x9` (it sits in the error message of the one place the sheet is added), and `cargo xtask` now scans for `evani.text.` markers next to `evani.module.` and `evani.shader.` ones, so the build report lists it and `cargo xtask sizes` has a `text` column. The on/off check treats the new prefix like any other non-module marker (always allowed), since a kit decides whether it draws text.
- **2026-10-15 — The match-3 plug-in waits.** Floating scores and combo callouts move from DOM to this API when kit-003-review has merged; this change touches no match-3 file.

## Device bench on Chris's phone and tablet (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — The bench stays as it is for now (Chris).** On the Samsung SM-G991B (Mali-G78) and the Lenovo TB305FU (Mali-G52) all three games run at a steady 60 fps at the tier their warm-up picks, but the bench, which fills each tier's full triangle and sprite limits, fails at those tiers (phone passes Low and Medium only; the tablet fails even Low on its 3D phase). Offered: test each device at the tier it can sustain and add a lighter tier for budget tablets (recommended), or lower Low's limits. Chris chose "Leave the test as it is for now", so the Stage 2 gate line for real devices stays open with the evidence in .vibedeck/screenshots/stage2-devices/results.md.

## Match-3 rings: crisp segments, not sprite art (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — Kit 003's Pop Bomb halo and its bomb and Prism blast rings are drawn from short straight segments, not the `sprite-art` feature.** Critic round 1 (must-fix 1, 5 and 6) found the atlas's soft `RING` and `GLOW` cells blurring into glow blobs at blast size, spilling past the board and, on the Pop Bomb, hiding its piece. A small helper (`Painter::ring` in kits/evani-kit-match-3/src/draw.rs) lays tangent, sharp-cornered rectangles round the circle (20 to 64 per ring, about 7 px each), with an ink rim under them on the same layer, and leaves out segments outside a clip rectangle, so a ring stays inside the board even though sprites have no clipping. That needs no renderer feature, no art file and no extra download, keeps the flat ink-outlined cartoon2d style at any size and tier, and the colours stay plain parameters. Sprite art (a painted ring sheet) would add the `sprite-art` feature and its atlas and mip work to the match-3 download for three shapes, and a painted ring still blurs when scaled from a fixed size to a board-wide blast.

## Canvas HUD in the review (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — A HUD drawn in the canvas is checked from the captured frame (Chris).** The 2D platformer draws its HUD as pixel glyphs on the art grid (Chris's review, point 6), which the DOM-based captures item could not see. Chris chose to keep that HUD and teach the review to see it: a kit sets evani-render's `RenderWorld::hud_layers`, the renderer notes the HUD sprites of every captured frame (`CaptureResult::canvas_hud`, live `capture` feature only, nothing in a ship build), `capture.frame` replies with them, and `[review] hud = ["canvas"]` makes the captures item require at least one visible HUD sprite on the canvas in every capture. DOM HUDs (kart racer, match-3) are unchanged; `canvas` is never sent to the DOM probe, where it would match the game canvas itself.
- **2026-10-15 — Platformer params stay `kit.platformer.*`.** AGENTS.md asks for `kit.<id>.*` with `_` for `-`, but `kit.2d_platformer.*` is not a valid param name (every segment must start with a letter, `evani_core::params::is_valid_param_name`). The review's architecture item accepts params by owner (`kit.2d-platformer`), so the kit keeps `kit.platformer.*` with that owner.
