## Shared GPU particles (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — One always-present queue in evani-core, the system behind `particles`.** CORE_REQUESTS plan item C. `evani_core::particles::ParticleQueue` (`emit(Emit)`, `burst(rng, n, base, Spread)`) is in every game; with no particle system in the build nothing is attached, `emit` returns at once and the app drops the queue each frame, so kits emit without a feature (the `AudioMixer` no-op pattern). `burst` draws its randomness from the kit's own `Pcg32` even when nothing is attached, so a kit's stream advances the same with the effect on or off. The evani-fx `particles` effect turns on `evani-render/particles`, which turns on `evani-render/compute`; games that don't list `particles` get none of it.
- **2026-10-15 — Closed form instead of step-by-step integration.** The plan said a compute pass integrates every particle per 60 Hz step. Instead each emit becomes a fixed 96-byte spawn record in a ring of `cap` slots (slot `emitted % cap`, so the oldest is recycled at the cap), and the `particles.sim` compute pass evaluates every slot once per frame from its age: position under gravity and exponential drag (`exp(-drag * t)`), size, spin and fade are closed-form functions of age. Nothing drifts, stepped captures repeat exactly (`same_seed_same_particles`), switching the effect off and on shows every particle where it would be anyway, and the only per-frame upload is that frame's new records (at most two contiguous writes). The native `eval` is the reference for the WGSL.
- **2026-10-15 — Compute passes are a graph pass kind (feature `compute`).** `PassKind::Compute` writes `TargetKind::Buffer` targets, which the owning system allocates (not the renderer), and the graph orders it before the passes that read the buffer (`compute_pass_orders_before_the_draw`); a compute pass writing a texture, or a render pass writing a buffer, is a graph error. Built only when an effect needs it.
- **2026-10-15 — Two kinds of draw, premultiplied blending.** Screen-space particles (`Space::Screen2d { layer }`) draw between the sprite layers, one instanced draw per layer that has particles; world-space particles (`Space::World3d`) draw in the forward pass as camera-facing quads, depth-tested without depth writes, one draw (per eye in VR). Blending is premultiplied, so alpha and additive particles (`Emit::additive`) share one draw. Shapes are atlas cells looked up exactly like sprite cells, so with `sprite-art` a named frame (from `ART_CELL0`) works as a particle shape (`art::cell_uv`, shared by sprites and particles).
- **2026-10-15 — Tier caps are numbers in assets/tiers/*.json.** `max_particles` 2000 / 10000 / 50000 / 250000, VR 5000, converted from catalog/tiers.json's strings and read into `TierPreset` (`cap_follows_the_tier` checks them against the catalog); `fx.particles.max` stays the game's own lower cap (range raised to 250000). A cap change reallocates the ring (the old one is dropped first, so the peak is one ring).
- **2026-10-15 — The bench fills every tier's cap.** A particle phase over the same backdrop emits the tier's whole cap on its first frame (half world motes, half screen sparks on three sprite layers) and a slice of it again every later frame, recycling the oldest, so the upload and the compute pass both run at full size every frame. The tier passes if the phase stays in budget like the 3D and 2D phases.
- **2026-10-15 — `compute-particles` counts as built.** It leaves the `missing` lists of the render path table (2D Ultra, 3D High and Ultra) and its `deferred` entry; a game gets it by listing the `particles` effect.
- **2026-10-16 — match-3 emits its sparkles and confetti into the shared particle queue.** The kit's own `Sparkles` pool and its sprite drawing are gone; `sparks.rs` decides where, when and what colour and `ParticleQueue` carries them (piece colour x1.9 in HDR, or white-gold; star, circle, diamond and confetti strip cells; layer `SPARK`), so the shared system simulates and draws them. Four choices. (1) Two engine additions, both visual-only: `ParticleQueue::emit_ago(ticks_ago, emit)` stamps an emit with an earlier tick so it shows already aged (the review captures replay the moment and need each particle at its real age), and `clear()` drops what is alive and waiting (a restart; the render system reads it through `take_clear`). (2) Sprites and particles have no clipping, so the blast and pop sparkles stay inside the board by ending their life where their closed-form path (drag 1.0) reaches the board edge (1/60 s steps, 0.1 cell margin); the engine fades the last quarter of a life, so they melt away just before the edge. Confetti is free to fall past it, as before. (3) The cap is the engine's: the tier's `max_particles` lowered by `fx.particles.max`, not the kit's old `max_sprites / 8`. (4) The determinism hashes are unchanged in value: the sparkle pool was part of the folded state and no longer exists, so its fold block is removed, and the five scenarios hash to exactly what the unmodified code gave with that block left out (moves and outcomes unchanged: 0 moves on the title, 15 won, 18 lost, 11 won, 17 won). `EXPECTED` keeps the old values in a comment. The particles use their own random stream (`FxStream`, `Pcg32::new(seed, 99)`) and never feed back into the game. Differences a player may notice: particles do not follow screen shake, they keep animating while paused (age follows engine ticks), and a window resize leaves live sparkles at their old pixel positions for their last fraction of a second. Prism sparks are still white stars, as before. Size: 345,482 to 345,525 bytes gzip (337.4 KB both; +43 bytes): the pool and drawing code that went was about the size of the emitter that replaced it, so the 7.5 KB of the shared system is now used rather than saved. No new dependencies.

## Ship builds use relative paths (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — Ship builds pass `--public-url ./` to trunk, and the ship guard refuses root-absolute paths.** The first published copies showed a blank screen because trunk wrote `/evani-<id>-<hash>.js` into index.html and play.evani.app serves each game from a subfolder (`/<item>/<version>/`), so those requests hit the site root and 404. `trunk_build` now always builds with `./` (it also works from the site root), and `ship_guard` fails the ship build with an ERROR and a `fix:` line if index.html contains `src="/`, `href="/`, `from '/` or `module_or_path: '/`. Test: `ship_guard_refuses_root_absolute_paths`. No new dependency.

## Z-fighting found by the depth maths (2026-10-15)

- **2026-10-15 — geocheck's z-fight test works from the renderer's depth setup, not a tuned gap.** Two surfaces fight where `|delta| < eps` at one pixel: `delta = (d_B - off_B) - (d_A - off_A)`, `d = a + b/z`, and `eps = step(d) + xf_A + xf_B + snap_A + snap_B` (the depth format's spacing, f32 vertex-transform rounding, 1/256-pixel vertex snapping times the depth slope); in metres the smallest separable gap is `eps / k`, `k = |b| (r.f) / (z^2 |n.r|)`. `DepthSetup::engine()` takes near, far, the depth format and the decal offset from evani-render, and a unit test reads `pipelines.rs`, `frame.rs` and `forward.wgsl` so a renderer change fails it. It is judged from the game's recorded cameras (title, race) and, live, from the game camera. The old test (5 cm gap, `depth_k * z^2`, `zfight_area`) is gone; thresholds `zfight_angle` (10 degrees) and `zfight_px` (4) are the only knobs. Dev tooling only: nothing reaches a ship build. Details: docs/GEOCHECK.md "The depth maths".
- **2026-10-15 — the z-fight test also pairs an item with itself ("thin slab").** The camera pipelines cull no faces and `forward.wgsl` lights a face by its own normal, so a slab's underside is drawn, dark, just behind its top; thinner than `eps / k` it shows through. Title-camera captures of Sugarpeak Rally proved it: its 1 mm road repair patches look like a subtle tone from 6 m but a dark banded slab from the title camera (about 100 m), with or without the outline pass, and the speckle moves with sub-pixel jitter. Only opposite-facing faces of one item pair (same-facing neighbours never do); `DepthSetup::back_faces` records the engine fact and the renderer guard test checks it (no `cull_mode`, no `front_facing`).

## Licence: MIT (2026-10-28)

- **2026-10-28 — Chris made Evani.dev fully MIT and open source.** Chris: "Time to Opensource Evani.dev, Full MIT." The root `LICENSE` is the standard MIT text, Copyright (c) 2026 Chris Pearson, and every crate in the workspace (engine crates, tools, looks, kits and apps) says `license = "MIT"`. It covers Evani.Dev only; Evani.app (the platform and app) is a separate product and not part of this licence or this repository. `cargo xtask new-game` gives a new game a plain MIT `LICENSE`; `build --ship`, `package-check` and `publish` write and check `ENGINE-LICENSE.txt` as the engine's MIT notice (MIT asks for its notice to travel with every copy, the compiled runtime included), and a package README's licence line is `Game code: MIT. Includes the Evani.Dev runtime, also MIT (see ENGINE-LICENSE.txt).` (docs/LICENSING.md). Third-party code keeps its own licence (THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md).

### Earlier licence decisions (superseded on 2026-10-28 by MIT, kept as history)

- **2026-10-16 — (Superseded.) Evani.Dev and Evani.Model were put under a source-available licence that turned into Apache 2.0 two years after each release.** Chris's decision covered the engine, its crates and tools, the diagnostic tools and Evani.Model, and barred only using them to make a product that substitutes for the engine; games built with the engine were never restricted and always belonged to their makers.

- **2026-10-28 — (Superseded.) Games are free to adapt, by anyone and by AI tools.** Chris, about Sugarpeak Rally: "The AI's are allowed to adapt the game, no problem." A plain-words section went into every shipped `ENGINE-LICENSE.txt`, and `cargo xtask new-game` started giving a game's own crates `license = "MIT"`. Replaced the same day by the MIT decision above, which needs no extra wording.

- **2026-10-28 — (Superseded.) Existing games' own code is MIT (Chris approved).** The kits and apps of 2d-platformer, base-building-rts, kart-racer (including its referee), match-3, tactical-fps and top-down-action-adventure were given `license = "MIT"` while the engine crates kept the earlier licence. Now the whole workspace is MIT.

## Upscaling research: DLSS and the open alternatives (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Research done; DLSS cannot run in a browser; nothing built.** Findings in [docs/UPSCALING.md](docs/UPSCALING.md). Official DLSS needs a native app and NVIDIA's driver. DLSS 5 is a neural re-lighting model, not an upscaler. The only web version (OpenDLSS-NR) needs about 145 MB of weights that press says were leaked (unverified) and runs under one frame per second. XeSS and FSR 4 are closed or of unclear legal status.
- **2026-10-16 — Recommended path: a spatial rung first (FSR 1 style), a temporal rung on top (FSR 2/3 style).** pmndrs/upscaler (MIT) is the WGSL reference and AMD's MIT source gives the details; notices are kept when code is adapted. It would be an optional `upscale` feature (zero bytes for games that do not list it), with detection, a warm-up measurement and a ladder temporal, spatial, plain render scale, plus parameters `fx.upscale.mode`, `.ratio`, `.sharpness`, `.reactive`.
- **2026-10-16 — Staging.** The temporal rung stays in the existing Stage 8 `temporal-upscale` row. The spatial rung for lower tiers would change what the catalog says, so it is in the STAGES.md Parking lot and CORE_REQUESTS.md (`spatial-upscale`) as "proposed, not approved", and waits for Chris's approval. Anime4K's licence was read and is MIT.

## Draw joins found from the sprites (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — geocheck-2d check 8 (`join_gap`) predicts thin screen lines from the dumped sprites, not from pictures.** The line Chris saw running across Fennel's Leap was a 1-art-pixel gap between two copies of a repeated parallax strip: each copy at `-off + k * width` was snapped on its own with Rust's `round` (half away from zero), so when the offset ended in exactly .5 (far hills at camera x 10 mod 20, near hills at 5 mod 10) `-1.5` went to `-2` but `510.5` to `511` and the sky showed through. A frame-based scan could not tell such a gap from a sky-coloured column in the art, so the check rasterises each world layer's sprite rects and atlas opacity on the art grid and flags uncovered runs of at most `join_gap_px` (2) with opaque art of the same layer on both sides, at least `join_line_px` (4) long. Because the break needs a particular camera position, a dump adds `sweep` frames (one per whole-art-pixel camera step, read only by this check). The platformer dump now turns the parallax effect on as the game does (before, its frames had a flat sky and no planes). Fix in the kit: `Screen::px` snaps with `floor(v + 0.5)`, and a strip's offset is snapped once before its copies are stepped by whole widths. The live `debug_geometry` view is 3D-only (mesh instances), so the check runs through `cargo xtask geocheck <id>`. Details: docs/GEOCHECK.md "Draw joins".

## Kart racer: flicker fix, one-sided tops (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Cause and fix.** The dark patches that flickered on Sugarpeak Rally from the far title camera were thin closed slabs on a surface: road repair patches (1 mm), stains, braking and drift strips (1 cm), start-grid brackets, banner chequers and grass blobs. A closed slab has a dark underside, and at 70 to 150 m the depth buffer cannot tell it from the top, so it speckled through and changed with every half-pixel of camera movement (the detector's thin-slab rows). They are now drawn as one-sided tops: the built-in plane lifted by half the thickness (so the top sits where the old box's top was, and the decal depth offset still applies), and the grass blob mesh without its bottom fan. Engine culling and the other games are unchanged. Close up the patches look as before (picture difference at most 14 of 255 on a few edge pixels).
- **2026-10-16 — Result and what is left.** Z-fighting detector on the kart racer: untagged patches 492 to 279; the thin-slab causes for road patches, wear and skid strips, grid brackets, banner chequers and grass blobs are gone. Left, and not part of this fix: kart bodies and kerb pieces that overlap, decals lying behind another surface (the banner chequers behind the gantry's red blocks, road marks on the road mesh), and the 1 cm shadow discs and grandstand shadow strips (flat cones and slabs with a base: 42 patches on grass, 13 on the road, 2 under a stand) and one patch of the track's paint mesh, which have the same kind of underside and are the next candidate. Evidence: `.vibedeck/screenshots/kart-flicker-fix/` (before and after, at the camera's pixel and half a pixel off).

## Sandbox z-fighting: shadow discs wait for reversed-Z (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — What the detector found.** A fresh `cargo xtask geocheck sandbox` (the committed report predated the z-fight detector and said 0) reports 400 untagged z-fighting patches and nothing else (overlaps, floating, gaps, mesh, moving, camera and the render flicker test are all 0). All 400 are one cause: each spinner's shadow disc (a flat built-in cone drawn as a decal on Low, 1 cm high) is a "thin slab": near its rim the cone's top comes within 0 to 4 mm of its own base, and from the orbit camera (about 22 m) the engine's standard depth (near 0.1 m, Depth32Float) only separates surfaces 2.9 to 4.2 mm apart there. The candidates read from the code (the orb rings, terraces on terraces, column parts) give no patches.
- **2026-10-16 — Decision: no kit change, no tag; they wait for reversed-Z.** They are depth-precision cases, not visible flicker. The same detector with a reversed-Z depth setup (a temporary local change, not kept) reports 0 z-fighting for the sandbox. Captures in the live game on Low (the only tier that draws the discs) from the orbit camera, with the camera shifted by a whole pixel and by half a pixel, show no flicker on any disc: the whole-pixel flicker maps find 8 to 34 pixels per 960 x 540 view, all on object edges; the half-pixel shifts change only outlines (240 pixels round one disc in a 5 degree close-up at 23 m, up to about 7,200 in the crowded middle of the grid), never a disc's inside. Thickening the discs or tagging them would hide a case the reversed-Z depth change (branch `reversed-z`) is meant to clear, so neither was done; they waited for reversed-Z. **Resolved:** reversed-Z (df0a83e) is merged, and a fresh full `cargo xtask geocheck sandbox` on top of it reports 0 issues, 0 untagged (z-fighting 0, and every other category 0); the saved report and the stale 400-patch crops are replaced by that run. Nothing was thickened or tagged; the gate box in STAGES.md stays for Chris to sign. Evidence of the original finding, kept locally in the main checkout and not committed (the folder is git-ignored): `.vibedeck/screenshots/sandbox-zfight/` (`before/`: the captures, `jitter-*.png` half-pixel differences, `pxflicker-*.png` whole-pixel flicker maps).

## Reversed-Z depth (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 - The renderer's depth is reversed: near maps to 1, far to 0.** Cause: with near 0.1 m, far 1000 m, clear 1.0 and compare Less, f32 rounding in the vertex transform needed 72 to 240 mm of separation at 75 to 150 m, so thin parts (7 cm flag cloth, 11 cm chevrons, 3 cm steps) flickered from the title camera and no kit could fix that without making parts thicker. Reversed-Z with floating-point depth spreads precision evenly, so the error is well under 1 mm at any distance. One shared module, `evani_render::depth` (REVERSED, NEAR_DEPTH, FAR_DEPTH, CLEAR, TOWARD_CAMERA, COMPARE, helpers), holds the convention; kits and tools read it and never hard-code it. Kit-facing APIs are unchanged and no kit was touched.
- **2026-10-16 - What follows the convention.** `math::perspective` is reversed; the forward and particle pipelines compare Greater and clear to 0; the MSAA depth resolve keeps the nearest sample with `max` (was `min`); the decal pad is signed on the CPU (`depth::toward_camera`) and the forward shader adds `pad * w`, the same world-space offset as before; the outline pass linearises with `inv_dist = 1/dist` (an affine function of depth) from `depth::inverse_distance`; geocheck's `DepthSetup::engine` takes `reversed` from `depth::REVERSED` and a unit test pins it to the renderer. The shadow map keeps its own convention (orthographic, clear 1.0, Less, LessEqual sampler, bias unchanged): it has no far-distance precision problem and its bias is tuned.
- **2026-10-16 - The far plane stays finite (1000 m).** An infinite far plane would save nothing here: the outline and geocheck use FAR, and a finite plane keeps what is clipped today clipped, so no game changes what it shows at distance. Reversed finite-far still gives near-uniform precision.
- **2026-10-16 - Result.** Z-fight detector, kart racer: 279 patches in 46 groups to 27 in 7; all 84 thin-slab and 97 near-coplanar patches are gone. What is left is decal geometry (25 "decal behind surface") and 2 real coplanar faces, which are kit-side. Sandbox: 400 to 0. Captures: 2d-platformer identical; sandbox under 0.4% of pixels changed; kart 0.5 to 1.5% (faint pixels on depth edges and outline ink, plus far thin parts that no longer flicker); match-3 differs by no more than its own run-to-run noise.

## Kart racer: last flicker spots cleared (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Road decals sit 1 mm over the road mesh's real triangles.** The road mesh is flat quads between kept rings, but the paint, start chequer, wear and skid marks, repair patches, grid brackets and item box discs were laid on the smooth centre line, so in places they dipped under the asphalt (the grid brackets' arms were half buried) and flickered against it ("decal behind surface"). `trackmesh::road_top` gives the height of the triangles actually drawn; `roadmarks::seat` lifts any flat decal so its lowest point over them (footprint sampled every 10 cm) is `ROAD_CLEAR`; the grid brackets are `plate`s square to the heading and tilted to the slope; item box discs are tilted and lifted from a per-radius table. The paint and start chequer meshes are one-sided tops on the kept rings, lifted so the quad diagonal can't poke through. `ROAD_CLEAR` is 1 mm: with reversed-Z the depth buffer separates far less than that out to 150 m, and a first try at 5 mm made the ink outline pass draw a dark line round the repair patches (a crease at the step). Tests check every mark against the built road mesh by brute force.
- **2026-10-16 — Other kit fixes.** Lollipop swirl rings are washers (no hidden face behind the ring in front); shadow discs and the grandstand strip are one-sided tops; the start banner is built from solid pieces (chequer squares the banner's full depth), because chequers painted on both faces as decals showed through the banner's top from afar.
- **2026-10-16 — Detector: a closed concave mesh hides its own back faces.** `LocalShape`/`Shape` carry `closed`, and the visibility test uses it instead of "convex solid": two touching kerb runs (each a closed mesh of separate boxes) no longer pair their hidden end caps, while an open mesh's back faces still count (test `touching_closed_concave_meshes_do_not_fight_but_open_ones_do`, which fails under the old rule).
- **2026-10-16 — Result.** Z-fighting on the kart racer: 279 untagged patches in 46 groups to 0, none tagged (kit fixes 279 to 44; the 44 left were thin parts far off, flag cloth, chevrons, kart parts and sparks, which reversed-Z cleared). Full geocheck 140 issues, 0 untagged. Gameplay unchanged (race code untouched; kit tests including determinism pass). Evidence: `.vibedeck/screenshots/zfight-clear/` (before and after at the camera's pixel and half a pixel off, far and close up).

## Platformer touch controls (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 - Layout: left and right hold buttons, Run, a large Jump and a pause button.** Buttons, not a stick: the platformer wants digital walking, and a held button is the same as a held key. Run is a button because the keys have it and its jumps are longer; auto-run would change how touch plays. Drop-through (down + jump) has no button: no part of the level needs it (the bot never uses it) and the keys keep it. Jump is a hold button, so coyote time, the jump buffer and variable height (release cuts the rise) apply unchanged; a test runs touch and keys frame for frame and they end in the same state.
- **2026-10-16 - The pause button sits under the HUD, not beside the timer.** The HUD owns the top 24 art rows at the right edge (time, or the wider FREE ROAM label). A button left of them would depend on those widths; 10 vmin down starts below the HUD on every phone and tablet size tested (`the_pause_button_sits_under_the_hud_on_every_screen`, play and free roam). It sits over sky, not over play.
- **2026-10-16 - The layer is on only in `Phase::Playing`,** set from the sim as the kart does; the title, pause, settings and results are page menus tapped by finger. Input mapping only: no change to the simulation, the recordings or their hashes. The engine's touch layer is unchanged.

## Device bench: each device at its own tier, and a Lite tier (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Test each device at its own level, add a lighter one for cheap tablets (Chris).** Chris's answer: "Test each device at its own level, add a lighter one for cheap tablets". This supersedes "2026-10-15 — The bench stays as it is for now". The bench gains a device mode (`?device=1`): it clears the saved tier, lets the renderer's first-run warm-up pick a tier on this device, then runs every tier as before but judges only the picked one (the others are printed as information). A plain bench run (no `device=1`) still judges every tier, so the desktop gate is unchanged.
- **2026-10-16 — Device mode warms up on each tier's own 3D content, not the `?stress=1` scene.** While the warm-up measures, the bench draws the active candidate tier's own 3D phase (its limits, its effects). The stress scene (360k triangles plus 500 full-screen layers, sized for a desktop card) would push every phone to the lowest tier and so measure nothing about the device.
- **2026-10-16 — Lite, a tier below Low for budget tablets and low-end phones (Mali-G52 class).** Limits: 60 fps target with a 30 fps floor, render scale 0.65 at DPR 1 or less, 100 draw calls, 40k triangles, 3,000 sprites, 1,000 particles, 5 points, no MSAA (this changes to 4x with the tablet-flicker merge, see the MSAA entry below). The scale was measured on the Lenovo TB305FU (Mali-G52, 1340 x 688 at DPR 1.125): the bench's Lite phases cost at most 9.6 to 10.8 ms of GPU time at 0.6, 11.0 at 0.65, 12.1 at 0.7, 14.2 at 0.75 and 15.9 at 0.85 (with a 33 ms p99 hitch); 0.65 is the highest that keeps every phase under about 12 ms, leaving room for game logic. Low on the same tablet costs about 20.5 ms. It is auto-selectable, so a game's warm-up can pick it, and `fallback_tiers` stays Low on purpose. Its effects are Low's minus speedlines and vignette: with the vignette its effects cost 6 points against its 5-point budget and the budget shed particles.
- **2026-10-16 — Lite turns on 4x MSAA, pending the tablet-flicker merge (tier data on this line still has msaa 1; Chris saw distant curbs flicker on the tablet).** Chris watched the kart racer on the Lenovo TB305FU at Lite and reported flickering in the distance, mostly on the curves. Cause: Lite drew the 3D pass at 0.65 scale with no multisampling, so thin far-off curb stripes and road dashes stair-stepped and crawled as the kart moved (screenshots `.vibedeck/screenshots/stage2-devices/run3/flicker/before-lite-*.png`). Not z-fighting: the geocheck z-fight scan is 0 and Medium and up, which already use 4x MSAA, show no such crawl. Fix (owned by the `tablet-flicker` branch, which carries the tier change for Lite and Low; this branch carries no tier-data change, the diff is kept in `.evani-tmp-devices3/msaa-tier.diff`): `msaa` 4 in `assets/tiers/lite.json` (render scale stays 0.65). Measured on the same tablet: the bench's Lite worst phase costs 14.5 ms of GPU time (3D phase; it was 10.5 ms), average frame 16.59 ms, p99 16.7, still PASS; kart-racer driven at Lite keeps 16.59 ms average, p99 16.7 over 420 frames. Lite's note above ("under about 12 ms") now reads 14.5 ms: the headroom is smaller but real play holds 60 fps. Low would show the same crawl if a device picked it (none tested does); `tablet-flicker` also turns MSAA on there.
- **2026-10-16 — Decision: keep the original; the tablet-flicker branch is not merged.** Shown the tablet kart race side by side (original: crisp, stair-stepped edges, no ink; `tablet-flicker`: ink kept, smoother edges, a little softer), Chris chose "Keep the original". So Lite and Low keep msaa 1 and the entry above does not apply to shipped builds. Known issue: on the Lenovo TB305FU at Lite, thin distant curb stripes and road dashes in the kart racer still crawl as the kart moves. The branch stays unmerged at `../Evani.Dev-flicker` (`tablet-flicker`, 85f4f80), with the comparison in `.vibedeck/screenshots/tablet-flicker/compare-2026-10-16/`. Any later fix must keep the original's sharpness and is shown to Chris side by side before it merges.
- **2026-10-16 — The cost order includes Lite.** The bench and `cargo xtask check` require Lite < Low < Medium < High < Ultra in GPU time on the stress scene (desktop: lite 0.13, low 0.25, medium 0.61, high 1.20, ultra 3.95 ms). Fresh bench and review runs always include every builtin tier.
- **2026-10-16 — Known gaps.** kart-racer draws about 100k triangles in a race, above Lite's 40k limit, so at Lite it is over budget (it still ran at 60 fps on the phone at High); Evani.Model compiles LODs for low to vr only, so a Lite game uses the Low LOD. Neither is changed here.

## The 3D path is a build-time option: `forward` (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Feature `forward` on `evani-render`.** The forward pass and its WGSL, the `depth` target, 4x MSAA and the depth resolve, the mesh buffers and built-in meshes on the GPU, the lighting and shading uniforms, decals and world-space particles now compile only with it. Without it the graph has no `depth` target and no `forward` pass; `sprites` clears `hdr` itself (the magenta clear override still works) and the target order is unchanged. `shadows` and `meshes` turn it on, and so does the `outline` effect (`evani-fx`: `outline = ["evani-render/forward"]`); screen-space particles stay in `particles` and draw without it (match-3). A 3D kit opts in on its own renderer line, `evani-render = { workspace = true, features = ["forward"] }` (example kit, kart racer with `meshes`, bench); `evani.toml` is unchanged, because the 3D path is part of the kit's code, not an optional game module, and `cargo xtask new-game` copies the line from the example kit. Public kit-facing APIs, `RenderParams` and every `render.*` live parameter stay as they were, so the live schema is the same in both builds.
- **2026-10-16 — The name.** `forward`, as proposed: it is the pass's name in the graph, the render path's kind (`PathKind::Forward`) and the shader marker (`evani.shader.forward`), so one word means the same thing everywhere.
- **2026-10-16 — The guard.** A 3D draw (mesh instance, decal or world-space particle) in a build without `forward` is never silently lost: `RenderModule` logs one `ErrorLog` error naming what was dropped and the Cargo.toml line that fixes it (`dropped_3d`, test `a_3d_draw_without_the_forward_path_is_reported_once`). `cargo xtask check` adds area `render path`: ERROR when a kit draws 3D (its source uses `MeshInstance`, `draw_mesh`, `draw_decal` or `Space::World3d`) or its look's style is `3d` and no crate turns `forward` on; WARN when a 2D game turns it on and draws no 3D (tests `forward_must_be_on_exactly_when_the_game_draws_3d`, `the_repo_games_follow_the_forward_rule`).
- **2026-10-16 — Shader numbering.** `ENGINE_SHADERS` follows the feature (the forward shader is listed only with it), the engine pipeline slots are sized from that list, and the validate test checks every shader in both builds.
- **2026-10-16 — Result.** Measured on the same base (m1-foundation 6b25433, before; the branch with it merged, after), ship gzip total: 2d-platformer 328.3 to 313.7 KB (-14.6), match-3 337.5 to 326.7 KB (-10.8), kart racer 404.9 to 404.9 (+0.05), sandbox 256.6 to 256.6 (+0.07). The `evani.shader.forward` marker is gone from both 2D downloads. Captures at every review moment: platformer, kart racer and sandbox pixel-identical; match-3 within its own run-to-run noise (two runs of the same build differ as much). match-3's `fx.particles` line grows from 7.4 to 10.5 KB because the code it shared with the 3D path is now counted against it; the game is still 10.8 KB smaller.

## Review captures are the same picture every run (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Cause.** Two runs of the same match-3 build differed by 2–39 % of pixels in moment-*.png and 2–26 % in shot-*.png (`.vibedeck/screenshots/m3-capdet/before-diff`). Two causes. (1) Live stages a moment and holds the clock at the session's render time at that instant (18.57 s in one run, 18.64 s in the other), and match-3's draw animates Puff, the background drift, the breathing pieces and the shake from `Time::render_seconds()`. (2) The page screenshots were taken after `capture.frame` returned, when the clock was already released, so the game, its sparks and the page's CSS animations (banner pulse, card drop, bar transition) had run on for a varying time.
- **2026-10-16 — Fix.** `capture.frame` gains `hold_after` (LIVE_API 6.14): the clock stays held on the captured frame until the next run ends (`LiveSession.kept_hold`; test `capture_frame_hold_after_keeps_the_clock_held_until_the_next_run`). The review capture script uses it for every moment capture, and takes moment page and showcase screenshots with the page's CSS animations settled (finite ones finished, endless ones stopped for the shot and restarted). match-3 draws a staged moment at the moment's own replay time (`DrawState.moment_t`, from the replay's tick count; test `a_moment_is_the_same_picture_whatever_time_the_clock_is_held_at`). Draw-only and behind `showcase`: gameplay and the determinism hashes are untouched. Kits that animate from render time should do the same if their captures drift. The fixed time steps past Puff's blink (`draw::moment_clock`; test `a_staged_moment_never_catches_puff_mid_blink`), so a moment's one picture shows his face.
- **2026-10-16 — Result.** Two back-to-back runs after the fix: all 9 match-3 moments, 18 showcase shots and the page shots pixel-identical (`m3-capdet/after-diff`). Platformer and sandbox: every moment, page and showcase shot identical. Kart racer: every moment and page shot identical; the two `threat` showcase shots differ in 16 pixels (0.001 %) by one colour level on the rounded corners of the see-through item box, the browser's own GPU rounding (`m3-capdet/other-games`). Still free-running, and not part of the regression check: the `geo-boot` and `geo-path_*` world-edge views, which are taken from the running game outside any moment.

## Fast iteration: `cargo xtask check --quick` (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 - Why.** A timing review found about 13 h spent re-running full checks (`check --full` is minutes a run, cargo test 5.2 h, clippy 1.7 h in total) and about 13 h of workers polling logs in sleep loops. Agents now work with a quick, changed-only check and run the full one once, before review.
- **2026-10-16 - The flag is `--quick` (the default Chris confirmed).** It runs the same static checks as the plain check, then fmt (check mode), clippy and tests for only the workspace crates with a changed file since the last commit (staged, unstaged and untracked against `HEAD`, from `git`) and every crate that depends on them, transitively, from `cargo metadata --no-deps` (normal, dev and build dependencies all count: a dev-dependency changes that crate's tests). It prints each crate with its reason (`touched` or `dependent (via <crate>)`), says plainly when nothing changed, and ends with a line saying it is not a substitute for `--full`. `--quick` cannot be combined with `--full` or `--json`. A change to the root `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `clippy.toml` or `.cargo/` selects every crate; a change outside any crate (docs, catalog, assets) selects none.
- **2026-10-16 - fmt is advisory, and never writes.** The repository is not rustfmt-formatted (hand-laid-out code, long lines, CRLF) and must not be reformatted, so a scoped `cargo fmt --check` would always fail. Quick mode runs `cargo fmt -p <touched crates> -- --check` and reports the touched `.rs` files that differ as WARN lines; they never fail the run. `--full` and the plain check never ran fmt or clippy and still don't.
- **2026-10-16 - clippy has no `-D warnings`.** The workspace denies what it means to deny (`unwrap_used`, `expect_used`, `unsafe_code`) and those fail the run. A first try with `-D warnings` failed on a lint that Rust 1.98 added for old code in `evani-core` (`play.rs`, a manual char comparison), which would fail every run, so plain warnings are listed as WARN (distinct lines, first 10) and the run does not fail on them. **Superseded by the next entry.**
- **2026-10-16 - clippy now fails on any warning (`lint-clean`).** The old warnings are fixed, so quick mode runs `cargo clippy -p <chosen> --all-targets -- -D warnings`. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` reported 11 findings (12 counting the pi literal twice): manual char comparison (`evani-core` `play.rs`), `3.14159` test literals read as an approximate pi (`evani-core` `num.rs`, now `3.14151`, same test), two collapsible `if` (`evani-live` `commands.rs`, `evani-guide` `playground.rs`, now let-chains), `% 2 == 0` (`art.rs`, now `is_multiple_of`), a doc list indent (`evani-geocheck-2d`), two field assignments after `Default::default()` (match-3 `review.rs` and its geocheck dump), a needless `clone` (bench `check.rs`), a range loop (kart-racer `trackmesh.rs` test) and `len() > 0` (kart-racer `lib.rs` test). All fixed with no behaviour change and no `#[allow]`; the workspace lint config is untouched. fmt stays advisory. A unit test (`clippy_warnings_fail_quick_mode`) pins the flag, and a probe run with an injected warning failed quick mode. A newer toolchain may add lints: fix them the same way when quick mode reports them.
- **2026-10-16 - Load caps.** Quick mode's cargo children get `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` (the environment wins, else half the cores, at least 1) and `RUST_TEST_THREADS` (same value unless set), and on Windows start at `BELOW_NORMAL_PRIORITY_CLASS` (0x00004000, via `CommandExt::creation_flags`; a no-op elsewhere). `--full`'s children get the priority and the test-thread cap, not the job cap, so `--full` verifies exactly what it did and a build keeps every job it would use. The Stop hook is untouched and no dependency was added (the quick code is in `tools/evani-guide/src/quick.rs`, using `serde_json` already there).
- **2026-10-16 - Rules.** The engine block (`tools/evani-guide/guide/AGENTS.md`, regenerated with `cargo xtask init-ai`) has a new "Work fast, check once" section: quick modes while working (`check --quick`; `geocheck <id> --zfight --report-only` after a report-format change, `--sample 50` for a quick look, which is partial and never proof), the full check (and a full geocheck where the game needs one) once before review or hand-over, and never poll a log in a sleep loop: run long jobs in the background and wait for the completion notification. "Done means `cargo xtask check` is clean" is unchanged.
- **2026-10-16 - Timings (wall clock, warm cache, one shared PC).** Measured 2026-10-16 on the same worktree, back to back, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS=2`, below-normal priority, while other workers kept the PC at 50-75% CPU. "Touch" is a one-line comment appended to one file, after one untimed run to warm the cache.

  | After touching | `check --full` | `check --quick` | quick: crates | quick: clippy / tests |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | nothing | n/a | 9 s | 0 | not run |
  | `kits/evani-kit-match-3` (a real leaf: 1 dependent) | 323 s (as for the next row) | 7 s | 2 | 0.8 s / 4.4 s |
  | `kits/evani-kit-example` (11 crates depend on it) | 323 s | 52 s | 11 | 4.1 s / 47.0 s |
  | `crates/evani-core` (25 dependents) | 664 s | 151 s | 26 | 12.0 s / 132.8 s |

  `--full` has no clippy step; it runs each game's tests and its ship build. Split, after touching the example kit: tests 49 + 37 + 3 + 3 s, ship builds 24 + 58 + 36 + 112 s; after touching `evani-core`: tests 60 + 36 + 10 + 2 s, ship builds 109 + 197 + 114 + 131 s. `--full` was run once after touching the example kit, not the match-3 kit; its cost does not depend on which leaf was touched.

## match-3 HUD panel height follows the measured content (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — The panel is never shorter than its measured content.** The landscape panel was sized as 15 u + 32 px, a figure that was never measured (the content needs 13.45 u + 50 px at HUD units 13.3 to 30, taken from the review fit pass with the banner showing on level 3); it is now 13.6 u + 50 px (about 4 px of air). The portrait strip was 23 % of the screen height whatever its content needed, so its goal chips ran 8 to 29 px past its border on 320x568, 360x640, 390x844, 412x915, 430x932 and 768x1024 screens; it is now 6.2 u + 50 px at the unit it already had (the unit is unchanged, the strip is taller, the board keeps its size on phones). `Layout` carries the unit, so the page and the layout never derive it two ways. A test pins `panel height >= measured content height` for 13 landscape and 6 portrait sizes against the measured points. Short landscape screens still shrink Puff first and then the unit, never the panel alone.

## Geocheck z-fight scan: capture cache, `--report-only`, `--sample N` (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Flag names (Chris's confirmed defaults).** `--report-only` and `--sample N`, both with `--zfight` only.
- **2026-10-16 — The cache.** `cargo xtask geocheck <app> --zfight` saves its scan (the native replay plus the depth maths over every recorded camera, 13 s + about 400 s on the kart) under `<target>/evani-geocheck-cache/<app>/<key>/` (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, else `target/`; a `.gitignore` of `*` at its top, never committed; the newest 8 entries per app are kept). An entry is `capture.json.gz` (what the report needs: the static world's instances and custom meshes, the cameras, the untagged issues, the "what ran" text, the run's notes; floats stored as their bits, so they come back exactly), `shots/` (the debug images' raw live captures, named by the hash of what the game is asked to draw) and `manifest.json` (written last; the key's inputs, file by file).
- **2026-10-16 — The key: when unsure, invalidate.** SHA-256 over four parts. Build: the bytes of every file of the crates the scan compiles in or captures with (evani-geocheck and its workspace dependency closure, the app's crate and its closure, the live bridge, `tools/live-check`, files any of them `include_str!`/`include_bytes!` from outside, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `.cargo/config`, toolchain files), `rustc -vV` and the profile. Scene: app, replay length and sampling, the effective thresholds. Cameras: all, or `--sample N` with its seed. Settings: `DepthSetup::engine()`, viewport, still switch, tool version, cache format. Left out on purpose: Markdown, `geocheck.toml` (thresholds are in the scene part, tags apply when the report is written) and the report's own code (`src/cli/zfight.rs`, `src/cli/png.rs`), so a report-formatting change is a `--report-only` away. The exe's own hash would have been simpler but changes with every report edit, which defeats the point.
- **2026-10-16 — One report path.** A fresh scan is encoded, saved, decoded and the report written from the decoded capture, exactly as `--report-only` does, so a full run and a later `--report-only` write the same bytes (test `report_only_writes_what_the_full_run_wrote`, which also checks the result equals writing straight from the scan). The scan itself is unchanged: the full run verifies what it verified before.
- **2026-10-16 — Partial reports.** `--sample N` picks N of the recorded cameras with `Pcg32` (fixed seed, partial Fisher-Yates, sorted), writes `.vibedeck/geocheck/<app>/zfight-sample/` (with a `.gitignore` of `*`), never `zfight/`, and labels it `"partial": true`, `cameras` N of `cameras_total` and PARTIAL in the Markdown heading. Full reports say `"partial": false`. `cargo xtask check` refuses a partial `report.json` or `zfight/zfight.json` with an error.
- **2026-10-16 — `sha2 = "=0.10.9"` in evani-geocheck (cli feature).** The cache key's hash; already in the workspace (tools/evani-lookguard uses the same version), so it is not a new crate: nothing new is downloaded or built.

## Platformer held upright: a framed view (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — A portrait screen frames the view at `view_height_max_px` rows, centred, with black bars above and below.** On a 390 x 844 phone (dpr 3) the 256-column floor sets the scale to 4, and the view was 292 x 633 art px: the camera rests on the level's bottom, so play was a thin strip at the foot of a very tall sky, with the touch controls over the soil. Now any view taller than 288 rows is cut to 288 (the same rule as a view wider than 640 columns, turned on its side) and centred on whole device pixels: 292 x 288 at 4x on that phone, the camera's ground line back at 72 % of the view, the HUD at the view's top as on every other screen, the pause button in the bar above, the movement buttons, Run and Jump in the bar below (tests `whole_pixel_scale`, `the_pause_button_sits_under_the_hud_on_every_screen`, `upright_the_controls_sit_in_the_bar_below_the_view`).
- **2026-10-16 — Why not zoom in.** A bigger Fennel needs a bigger whole scale, and at 5x a 1170-pixel-wide phone shows only 234 columns, under the 256-column floor that keeps the look-ahead and the enemy wake-up margin (`wake_distance` is set against the view width) and the HUD (about 190 columns) readable. A fractional scale would break the whole-pixel rule (2026-10-15). So the art keeps its size and the frame is what changes: the view now shows 224 to 288 rows like every other screen, which the old portrait view (633 rows) did not. The trade-off: about half of an upright phone's height is bar, and Fennel is the same size as before; turning the phone sideways still gives the bigger picture (5x).
- **2026-10-16 — Drawing only.** `Screen::fit` and the bars change; the game camera, `wake_distance` and the simulation don't (the attract recording hashes the same before and after, 2640 ticks). Smooth looks get the same framing.

## Match-3 piece art by name (2026-10-16, S3-13c first half)

- **2026-10-16 — The kit asks by colour name, not shape name.** `piece.cherry`, `piece.toffee`, `piece.lemon`, `piece.mint`, `piece.berry`, `piece.grape`, `piece.prism`: the look's art is organised by the piece's colour, which is what the kit's data knows, and a look is free to redraw a colour's silhouette (a heart for the cherry) without the kit changing. A look without those frames falls back to the built-in shapes through `SpriteArt::cell`; `Style::default()` uses the built-in cells, so look-basic is unchanged and the kit never depends on the cartoon2d crate.
- **2026-10-16 — No heart or wrapped-sweet pieces.** The task named them as examples; the kit has six pieces (circle, rounded square, star, diamond, triangle, circle) and the look bible says "six piece shapes, silhouettes first". The silhouettes are kept so pieces read the same, only smoother. A heart in place of the cherry needs a look change and a look-bible change, so it is offered to Chris as a follow-up, not done.
- **2026-10-16 — Only the piece body layers use the art** (shadow, ink, shade, lit). Specials' add-ons, the tray, Puff and the particles keep the built-in shapes; the art is not asked for anywhere else.
- **2026-10-16 — The ink allowance follows the sprite's edge.** The kit grew the ink layer by a fixed 1 px for the built-in shapes' soft edge. Crisp frames have no soft edge, so the outline came out about 1 px too heavy (ink mass 5.1 px against the look's 3.5 px). `evani_render::SpriteEdge` (`Builtin`, or `Frame { width, pixel_art }`, `px(drawn)`) and `SpriteArt::edge(cell)` tell a kit how soft a cell's edge is, and `Style::ink_grow` uses it: built-in shapes keep exactly 2(o+1)/fill, so the fallback is pixel-identical; crisp frames now measure about 4.0 px (3 solid px plus antialiasing). It sits behind the `sprite-art` feature, so games that do not turn it on gain nothing (their ship builds are identical).
- **2026-10-16 — Cost.** Seven 128 px frames (six pieces and the prism) pack into about a 512 px square atlas with 4 mips: about 1.4 MB of GPU memory at most. Match-3's ship download grows from 316.5 to 325.4 KB gzip (+8.9 KB: the generator code and the named-frame lookups; the art itself is drawn at startup, not downloaded). 2d-platformer, kart-racer and sandbox: same wasm and js SHA-1 before and after. The engine bakes the git commit into every wasm (`EVANI_GIT_COMMIT`), so such a comparison needs that variable fixed on both sides; without it every commit changes every wasm hash.

## Lean data: built-in tiers compiled, no float formatting in shipped code (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Tier presets ship as Rust data.** `crates/evani-core/build.rs` parses `assets/tiers/*.json` at build time and writes a static table (`BUILTIN_TIERS`); `Tiers::builtin()` builds the presets from it, so no game embeds or parses tier JSON at start. The JSON stays the source of truth: people, the live tools and `bake.export` read and write it, the build reruns when a tier file changes, and a malformed file fails the build with its path. `Tiers::from_builtin_json()` (dev and tests) still parses the files, and a test checks both give the same presets. Live `tier.set` and `param.set` work on the presets in memory as before; `bake.export` writes the JSON, and the next build (or `cargo xtask dev --hot` rebuild) compiles it in (test `live_edits_and_dry_run_bake_work_over_the_compiled_tier_table`).
- **2026-10-16 — No float formatting in shipped code.** One `format!("{:.1}", x)`, `{}`/`{:?}` on a float, or an `f32::clamp` whose bounds the compiler cannot prove ordered (its panic message prints them with float `Debug`) links core's float-to-decimal code (Grisu, Dragon4 and tables) into the game. `evani_core::num` replaces them: `fixed(x, n)` is byte for byte `{:.N}` (padding, sign and zero flags included), `short(x, n)` the `{}` text for numbers with up to `n` places, `short_debug` the `{:?}` text, `clamp` the `f32::clamp` result without the panic. Tests compare them with std over many values and edges. Used in the overlay, render warm-up and quality log lines, touch-control CSS, `ParamDef::clamp`, the view rectangle, the three apps' volume text, the kart HUD (speed, lap time, layout CSS, AI lane clamp) and the platformer level checks. The compiled output of all four games now references none of core's float formatting (checked in the LLVM IR of each ship feature set). HUD text is unchanged; only two platformer error messages for broken level data could print a long fraction shorter.
- **2026-10-16 — Kit data stays JSON for now.** Levels, art, baked params, curves and slots are still embedded JSON parsed at start: the parameter registry, saves and bake files use the same serde_json path, so it ships anyway and compiling only the kit data would save little. Dropping serde_json from ship builds is the follow-up worth measuring (research note 2026-10-16-2d-size-modularity.md).
- **2026-10-16 — Result.** Ship gzip totals on the same base (m1-foundation before, lean-data with it merged after; baf10e9 for three games, f857d80 for match-3, whose kit changed in between): 2d-platformer 314.4 to 303.4 KB (-11.0, -3.5 %), match-3 326.8 to 316.5 KB (-10.3, -3.2 %), kart racer 404.9 to 393.5 KB (-11.4, -2.8 %), sandbox 256.6 to 245.2 KB (-11.4, -4.4 %). All of it is in the base line (core, render, kit, look) of `cargo xtask size-report --modules`. Compiler settings are unchanged. Nothing players see changed: the review-moment and page captures (HUD included) are pixel-identical in all four games, apart from the boot screen and the kart's camera-path debug views, whose differences are of the same kind and size as between two runs of one build; the kart race state after 90 s hashes the same on both builds; tests compare every changed HUD text with the old `format!` over its range. Startup (Trunk start event, median of 7 cold loads) is the same or 1 to 5 ms faster; bench 6/6 tiers pass, cost order holds.

## Our own model reader: `evani-gltf` (S3-2, 2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Design.** A new crate, reached only through the `models` module (an optional dependency of the game crate), so games without `models` carry none of it. It reads `.glb` (header, JSON chunk, BIN chunk) and `.gltf` with external buffers, and only the fields the engine uses (buffers, views, accessors, meshes, nodes, scenes, skins, every core metallic-roughness material field, textures, images, samplers, the root `extras.evani`). The JSON goes through `serde_json::Value`, which every game already carries: no new dependency, runtime or dev. Errors are a `&'static str` (no formatting code in the download). Every index, offset, stride and range is checked once at load (offsets that are negative, fractional or not numbers are errors, not a silent 0; every view and meshopt range must fit its buffer's declared length), so later reads cannot fail and bad input is an error, never a panic. EXT_meshopt_compression views are decoded once at load into plain bytes; fallback buffers are never fetched. KHR_mesh_quantization needs no special path: attributes are dequantised by the spec's rules and the position scale and offset are ordinary node transforms. The `anim-source` feature (animation channels and samplers, for the converter's clip baking) is native only: a wasm build with it on is a compile error, and a test scans every game manifest.
- **2026-10-16 — Ported versus written.** The glTF reader and the Evani metadata are written from the Khronos specification and docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md. The meshopt decoder is written from the extension specification (Appendix A bitstream, Appendix B filters), but its structure follows meshoptimizer's scalar `decodeVertexBuffer` / `decodeIndexBuffer` / `decodeIndexSequence` (MIT, Arseny Kapoulkine), so it counts as a port: the MIT notice is in `meshopt.rs`'s header and in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md. No `gltf`, goth-gltf or meshopt crate.
- **2026-10-16 — The corpus test caught a real decoder bug.** In TRIANGLES mode, codes below 0xf0 read the vertex FIFO one entry too new (`vo - y` instead of `vo - 1 - y`; the 0xf0+ codes keep `vo - f`). The unit tests from the spec's own examples passed; only decoding gltfpack's output and comparing it with the uncompressed file found it. So the reader's tests (gltfpack corpus plus Evani.Model's files) now run in every `cargo xtask check --full`, whether or not a game uses `models` yet, as well as in `--quick` whenever the crate changes.
- **2026-10-16 — How files are compared.** gltfpack orders vertices differently with and without `-cc`, so meshes are compared by vertex identity (the bytes of the unfiltered attributes), triangles rotation-canonical, filtered attributes within the filter's own precision (0.02 for octahedral normals and quaternions, relative 1e-3 for exponential). Evani.Model re-seats surface anchors on each tier, so tier anchors match the report within the tier's `cell_m`; anchors off the surface match within 1e-4.
- **2026-10-16 — gltfpack drops the root `extras`.** Even with `-ke`, gltfpack 1.3 keeps node names and node extras but drops the root `extras`, mesh names and texture duplicates, moves meshes onto unnamed child nodes and resamples animations to 30 a second. So identifiers live in node names, and the converter (S3-5) must copy `extras.evani` back into each output (docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md, "After gltfpack").
- **2026-10-16 — Size: over the planned 8 KB, accepted.** Measured in the sandbox ship build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9) with a probe that loads a GLB and reads every attribute, index, world matrix, anchor, part, LOD and texture set. First version: 230,573 B gz without the reader, 248,484 B with it, **+17,911 B gz** (+44,945 B raw). Where it went (raw, before gzip): JSON-to-struct parsing about 12 KB (every core material field and its checks), Evani metadata 2.2 KB, materials 1.7 KB, the whole meshopt decoder about 3.5 KB, the error texts about 1.2 KB gz. Chris's answer (2026-10-16): "Try to shrink it (likely still above 8 KB)". Shrinks kept (gz saved, measured on a standalone cdylib probe, 17,712 to 15,254 B): short error codes in wasm builds through an `err!` macro (-962; native builds keep the full text, the codes are in docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md "Error codes", kept in step by `tests/error_codes.rs`), building the whole `Gltf` value in one pass (-739), reading each JSON list once (-58), one shared accessor read loop (-230), component reads with no panic path (-311), a non-generic float conversion (-158). Tried and dropped: per-section parse functions, `#[inline(never)]` helpers and a shared non-inlined JSON lookup (all larger or no smaller). **Final, in the sandbox game: 230,573 to 246,518 B gz, +15,945 B gz (+37,605 B raw)**; with the whole `models` feature (the tier texture number too) +15,963 B gz. Left for later if size matters more: meshopt bounds checks, the `extras.evani` parser (2.2 KB raw) and the `Gltf` finishing pass (1.7 KB raw). The 8 KB figure was an estimate made before the reader existed. Games without `models` carry none of it (SHA-1 evidence in the S3-2 merge commit). Note for such comparisons: the wasm carries the engine's short commit id (`EVANI_GIT_COMMIT`), so two builds from different commits always differ; compare with `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT` set to one fixed value (the S3-2 final check rebuilt all four games with it pinned and matched the recorded SHA-1s exactly).
- **2026-10-16 — The `models` marker.** `evani_gltf::MODULE_ID` is `evani.module.models`; `Gltf::load` (every read goes through it) keeps it in the download with `std::hint::black_box`, so the build-time on/off check finds the marker exactly when a game's wasm links the reader (the probe build has it; the plain sandbox build has none). It costs nothing measurable (the probe stayed at 246,518 B gz).

## Textured models: `models` render path (S3-3, 2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 — Design.** evani-render's `models` feature (needs `forward`, pulls in evani-gltf and the tier texture number) adds a model pipeline beside the mesh pipeline in the same forward pass, lit by the same frame uniform, `toon_ramp`, fog and rim slots, shadow map and blob shadows. Vertices stay quantised on the GPU (Unorm16 positions over the part's box, Snorm8 normals and tangents, Unorm16 UVs; scale and offset per instance), so GPU memory matches the download. Materials: base colour (texture x factor x tint), MikkTSpace normal map, emissive, alpha opaque or mask, single- or double-sided (no culling; single-sided back faces are discarded, mirrored instances handled). Metallic-roughness and occlusion stay in the material data but the toon looks never sample them, and a map the look does not sample is never fetched (`unsampled_maps_are_never_fetched`). Models cast into the shadow map (`model_shadows.wgsl`) and get the outline effect from the shared depth. Texture memory is held to the tier's `max_texture_memory` by dropping the largest textures' top mips first, with an overlay note.
- **2026-10-16 — Texture sets: ASTC and BC7, then the file's own image.** At device creation, with `models` only, the engine requests `texture-compression-astc`, `-bc` and `-etc2` when the adapter has them and picks per KTX2Loader's order (ASTC 4x4, BC7, BC1/BC3, ETC2, then RGBA), preferring ASTC whenever reported (`format_choice_prefers_astc_over_bc`). The proof model ships two sets only: ASTC 4x4 (phones and tablets) and BC7 (desktop GPUs), each with mips, plus the glTF's own PNG/JPEG decoded by the browser (`createImageBitmap`) as the fallback, so no image decoder ships. ETC2 and BC1/BC3 are read if a file lists them but no tool writes them yet (S3-5's converter decides). The KTX2 reader takes levels and format only; no supercompression (the server compresses the bytes).
- **2026-10-28 — The public proof model is a plain stand-in.** For the open-source release Chris chose to keep the Frontier Marshal private, so the sandbox's proof model is `proof-figure`: an original, plain mannequin (about 27,000 triangles, 2.19 m tall, its own colour, normal and metallic-roughness maps, ASTC and BC7 sets made with the same `ktx` recipe as `cargo xtask convert`, packed with gltfpack). The entry below describes the original setup; the bench and the renderer's model tests use the stand-in the same way.
- **2026-10-16 — The proof model.** The sandbox draws four static copies of the Frontier Marshal (Chris's owned Meshy character, tangents baked, ASTC and BC7 sets; one copy mirrored) round the spinner grid. `cargo xtask new-game` leaves the proof out (`PROOF_FILES` and the `proof-model:` markers in the template), so new games don't inherit its 5 MB of model and texture files; `cargo xtask check` errors on a normal map without tangents.
- **2026-10-16 — The speck bug and its check.** In the browser the models were drawn as a speck: the per-part position and UV scales were divided by 65535 twice (the GPU's Unorm16 format already gives raw / 65535). Scales are now the box's whole extent; `dequant_matches_the_reader` reads vertices exactly as the GPU does, and `vertex_formats_are_what_the_test_reads` pins the pipelines' vertex formats and the shaders' dequantisation to that test, so the CPU reference and the GPU can't drift apart again.
- **2026-10-16 — Same look on every tier (Chris's rule).** The first captures showed the near figure's coat front much darker on Medium, High and Ultra than on Lite and Low. Cause: the normal map tips N.L above zero on a face turned away from the sun; the shadow map darkens that face on the shadow tiers, but Lite and Low have no shadow map, so it stayed lit. Fix (model.wgsl only): the light is also multiplied by `self_shadow(geometric N.L)` on every tier (0 turned away, 1 facing, a 0.1-wide linear blend across the terminator), so a face turned away gets the same darkest ramp band and shadow tint with or without the map. Same-moment captures with the map switched on and off now agree except for real cast shadows (the hat brim on the face), which only the map tiers have, as for every forward mesh. The check, for every game: `models_shade_the_same_with_and_without_a_shadow_map` evaluates the shipped `self_shadow` with naga's compile-time evaluator (its body is clamp and arithmetic only) and checks the ramp input and tint match with and without the map; `cargo xtask check --full` runs it once per project (`LOOK_PARITY_TESTS`, issue `look-parity`) whether or not a game uses `models` yet. Not changed here: forward meshes shade a face turned away from the sun without the shadow tint on Lite and Low (with it on the map tiers); changing forward.wgsl would change every 3D game's download, so it is left for its own item.
- **2026-10-17 — Lite and Low still looked brighter: the model's own cast shadow (review Q1).** After the fix above, Lite and Low were still brighter than Medium and up on textured models (the marshal's coat front, the brim's shadow on the face, the arms on the coat): those are shadows the model casts on itself, which the shadow map draws and nothing drew without one. Fix: a baked open-sky cone per vertex (`model::sun`). Of 64 directions over the half-sphere in front of the normal, the open ones (nothing of the model ahead, looked up in one small depth map per direction of a 64-cell occupancy grid of the whole model) are fitted with an axis and a cosine threshold, stored in the three vertex bytes that were free (position w, normal w), so the vertex size and layout are unchanged. model.wgsl lets the sun through where `dot(cone, sun)` is above the threshold, only when there is no shadow map; with one it shades exactly as before. The cone does not depend on the light, so the sun can move and instances can turn and mirror. Rejected: 32 directions (13 points off under a high sun), a cap on ray length (barely faster, less accurate), a per-vertex ray walk (257 ms native for the marshal, now 70). **When it runs:** not at load. Only frames without a shadow map read the cones, so the GPU side bakes a model the first time such a frame draws it, one model at a time and `sun::FRAME_UNITS` of work a frame (the marshal: 61 frames; in the browser on Lite median 2.1 ms, max 5.7 a frame; Medium and up spend nothing). A model that finishes loading on such a frame stays `Loading` (the kit's placeholder) until its cones are in; one loaded with a shadow map shows at once and keeps its vertices and indices on the CPU (about 26 bytes a vertex) so a tier drop mid-game still bakes it, drawn models first. The check, for every game: `models_cast_their_own_shadow_without_a_shadow_map` casts exact rays from the shipped marshal and a mirrored overhang under nine suns and holds the baked lit share within 5 points of the shadow map's under the shipped suns and 10 under extreme ones (a deliberately weakened bake fails it), and checks that a bake spread over frames writes the same bytes; `sun_cones_bake_only_where_they_are_read` pins which model bakes; both run in `cargo xtask check --full` through `LOOK_PARITY_TESTS`. **Evidence (browser, sandbox ship build, headless Chrome, sim time frozen so one pose is shot twice, shadow checkbox switched between the shots, mean luma 0 to 255 of the model's pixels, found with a flat-magenta mask build):** the map off minus the map on, before the cone then after, at the sandbox sun: Medium +8.7 then -0.3, High +9.4 then +0.5, Ultra +9.4 then +0.5; at a low sun (0.2, 0.5, 1.0): Medium +4.3 then -1.3, High +4.9 then +0.6, Ultra +5.8 then -0.5. The shadow checkbox changed the model's brightness by 0.0 to 0.3 on Lite and Low both before and after (switching it on there gives no shadow map on the model), so their reference is the same shader on the map tiers with the map off; the Lite and Low shots after the cone show the shaded coat and the brim shadow like the map tiers. Close-ups of the near marshal (brim on the face, arms on the coat, coat folds) show the same shading with the map and with the cone and no blotches. **Size:** sandbox ship build, gzip -9: 278,904 B before, 285,268 B after, **+6,364 B gz for the whole Q1 fix**, on top of the accepted 4 KB overshoot above; to be accepted by Chris or shrunk. Games without `models` are unchanged.
- **2026-10-17 — The baked sun cone and LOD levels (S3-6 gate).** The cone bake used every part of every LOD level as an occluder, but one level is drawn at a time, so hidden coarser levels darkened the drawn one (kart fixture: SSIMULACRA2 -3 on Low, dark blotches). Now each part is shaded only by the parts drawn with it: the shared parts plus its own level; a shared part (drawn by every level) is baked once against the shared parts plus the lowest level present (LOD0, the level drawn when the model is closest and the shading shows most). The grid box stays the whole model's, so a model without levels bakes the same bytes and costs the same; each extra level adds its own 64 depth maps over the shared cells plus its own (the vertices are split between the sets, so the per-vertex work is unchanged). Known limit: a shared part is shaded by LOD0 even while a coarser level is drawn (it has one set of cones for all levels), so at a distance it can keep a shadow from a LOD0 part that is not drawn then; the drawn level's own parts are right. Same budget per frame, same resumable stages. Check: `hidden_lod_levels_do_not_shade_the_drawn_one` (fails on the old bake; runs through `LOOK_PARITY_TESTS`).
- **2026-10-16 — Size: over the planned 12 KB, accepted.** Sandbox ship build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9): 251,098 B gz without `models`, 283,428 B with it, **+32,330 B gz for the whole `models` feature**; the reader's accepted share is 15,963, so S3-3's own share (pipeline, WGSL, KTX2, upload, budget) is about **16,367 B gz**, about 4 KB over the 12,288 budget. Shrinks kept: texture URI checks by bytes and vertices written in place (-682), the glTF JSON parsed from text through the engine's existing Value parser instead of a second byte-slice one (-1,584), the models build ships the forward and model-shadow WGSL without comments, stripped at compile time with the `//@slot` and `// evani.` markers and every line kept (-2,330, `models_wgsl_ships_without_comments_but_with_markers_and_lines`). The same-look fix costs +45. Chris's answer (2026-10-16): "Accept it as it is". Games without `models` are unchanged: ship wasm and js SHA-1 identical before and after (see the merge commit).

## Stage 3 modules: models, anim, ui, and the tier texture number (2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 - Modules are features first, crates later.** `models`, `anim` and `ui` are `[modules]` keys in evani.toml (`anim` implies `models`; both parsers set both). xtask maps them to app features of the same name; the four apps already declare them (`models = ["evani-core/texture-budget"]`, `anim = ["models"]`, `ui = []`). When evani-gltf, evani-anim and evani-ui land, each adds its `dep:` to its own feature line and nothing else changes (evani-gltf did in S3-2: `models = ["dep:evani-gltf", "evani-core/texture-budget"]`). A game that lists none of them compiles exactly what it did (checked by byte-identical ship builds). The optional-crate guard in evani-guide already knows the three crate names, so a plain (non-optional) dependency on one is an ERROR.
- **2026-10-16 - Asset checks.** `cargo xtask check` ERRORs, each with a `fix:` line: `.glb` / `.gltf` files in `apps/<id>/` or its kit without `models`; a model whose JSON declares a skin without `anim`; a font file (`.ttf`, `.otf`, `.woff`, `.woff2`) without `ui`. The folders `target`, `review`, `dist` and `node_modules` are skipped.
- **2026-10-16 - `max_texture_memory` in plain megabytes, behind a feature.** `assets/tiers/*.json` carry 64 / 96 / 192 / 512 / 1536 / 256 (Lite to VR; the catalog says "1.5 GB (streamed)" for Ultra, 1.5 x 1024). The field is `TierPreset::max_texture_memory` and the compiled tier table field exists only with evani-core's new `texture-budget` feature, which the `models` app feature turns on. Reason: the budget requires 0 bytes for every game without models, and an unread field in the table still changes the wasm. Rejected: always compiling it (24 bytes of table plus the copy, and a different hash for every game). Bake merges fields into the JSON, so the key survives a bake even in builds without the feature.
- **2026-10-16 - Sizes and markers.** `cargo xtask sizes` has `models`, `anim` and `ui` columns (module markers in the wasm); the build-time on/off check expects `evani.module.models` / `anim` / `ui` exactly when the game lists them, so each new crate must declare its marker.

## One-click publish: `cargo xtask publish <id>` (2026-10-16)

Chris (2026-10-16): "Start Stage 4's one-click publish now, alongside Stage 3". Built from the research report `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-16-one-click-publish-of-browser-games.md` (picks 1 and 2).

- **One command, never an upload.** `cargo xtask publish <id>` does the ship build, packaging, preflight, size report and diff; Evani.App's card (the owner's click) stays the only way a package is uploaded. Nothing in Evani.Dev calls the library.
- **Atomic packaging.** The package is assembled in a staging folder next to the destination and swapped in by two renames only after every preflight check passes; a failed run leaves `publish/<id>/` as it was and keeps the rejected package in `.evani/publish/<id>/failed/`. A folder that is not a package is never replaced, and an interrupted swap is recovered on the next run. Only the hand-made files (README, LICENSE, preview, evani.json, loader) are carried over, so stale hashed files cannot reach an upload.
- **Content-addressed versions, whole-file diff.** Each package is a manifest of path, SHA-256 and size; the package id is the hash over it. The diff against the version recorded as live (`--mark-published vN`, after the owner's click) lists NEW / MODIFIED / SAME / DELETED and says "nothing changed, skip" when identical (the idea of itch.io butler's push preview; no code borrowed). No binary delta patching: browsers fetch whole files, and the hashed engine files change whole on every build anyway.
- **Sizes labelled by basis.** The 10 MB cap is on the raw whole folder (what is uploaded); the download is index.html and the files it loads (what a browser fetches), in gzip and Brotli; modules first, then files. The catalog has no per-tier size targets, so none are invented: the report says so (Stage 7 plans them).
- **Summary outside the upload.** `summary.json` (schema `evani.publish.summary/1`), `summary.md`, the manifest and the version history live in `.evani/publish/<id>/` (gitignored), never in the package.
- **`sha2 = "=0.10.9"` in evani-xtask.** The package and file hashes; already in the workspace at that version (evani-lookguard, evani-geocheck), so nothing new is downloaded or built.

The untracked ones are gone; they were scratch captures, and the signed gates' machine evidence is reproducible with the same commands. ## The Evani.Model tab's page: `apps/model-view` and the bridge's model mode (T1a, 2026-10-16)

- **One bridge, a second mode.** The model page is served by the same `evani-live-bridge` with `--models <folder>` (port 8097, its own session file `.evani/live/model/session.json`), so the token, Host and Origin rules, paused pacing and live code swap are the tab's. Without the flag the bridge answers byte for byte as before (test `game_bridge_unchanged_without_models_flag`). Start it with `cargo xtask live --models` or the launcher `.vibedeck/dev-harness/model.mjs`.
- **Dev only, never published.** `apps/model-view` has no `[playground]`; `cargo xtask publish model-view` refuses it with a `fix:` line (test `model_view_is_never_published`), and the four games' ship wasm and js are unchanged (SHA-1 in the merge message).
- **Versions are folders, written last-file-first.** `cargo xtask model-import` (the stand-in for MT-a's `model_compile`) writes `v<N>.tmp-<pid>`, `version.json` last, then renames, retrying with N+1; the bridge never reads a folder without `version.json`. Model ids starting with `_` are refused (`_requests` is the bridge's). The model list is newest first by `version.json` modification time; `class` comes from `version.json`, else the metadata file.
- **Preview shading until T4.** The page draws each tier through the existing 3D path, one mesh per region colour; the corner note says so until S3-3's model pipeline is used (T4).

## Model-converter tools installed by `doctor --fix` (S3-5, 2026-10-16)

- **Pins.** `cargo xtask doctor --fix` installs gltfpack 1.3 (zip), KTX-Software 4.4.2 (`ktx`, silent NSIS installer) and the glTF-Transform CLI 4.5.1 (with sharp, from the committed lockfile `tools/evani-convert/npm/package-lock.json`, `npm ci`) into the gitignored `.evani/bin/`, checking each download's pinned SHA-256 first, and records version, source and checksums in `.evani/bin/convert-tools.json`. Blender 5.2.2 only with `--blender` (400 MB). The pins are in `tools/evani-guide/src/extools.rs`; `sha2 =0.10.9` was added to evani-guide (the version xtask, geocheck and lookguard already use) to check them. Missing converter tools are a `note:` in `doctor`, never a failure: only `cargo xtask convert` needs them, and it refuses a missing or changed tool with an error naming `cargo xtask doctor --fix`.
- **No administrator prompt, ever.** KTX-Software's installer asks Windows for administrator rights in its manifest, so launching it showed Chris an elevation prompt (that is what prompted during this item's first tool check, when the installer was run by hand once). It only writes into the folder given with `/D=` (checked: no PATH, registry or start-menu change), which is the user's own `.evani/bin/ktx-4.4.2`, so `doctor --fix` runs it with `__COMPAT_LAYER=RunAsInvoker` set on that one child process: Windows runs it as the user, with no prompt. It runs only when the installed `bin/ktx.exe` is missing or its checksum differs from the record. If an install without elevation fails, that is an error with a fix line; there is never an elevated retry. The download is saved as `...-installer.exe`, so nothing mistakes it for the tool, and the converter, its tests and scripts run only the installed programs by full path, never through PATH or the download folder. Tests never install or launch an installer. gltfpack (a plain exe from a zip), the npm tree (`npm ci`, no install scripts that need rights) and Blender (a portable zip) need no elevation.

## The model converter: `cargo xtask convert` (S3-5, 2026-10-16)

- **External pinned tools, run as processes.** `tools/evani-convert` (a native dev crate; no game links it) drives gltfpack 1.3, the glTF-Transform CLI 4.5.1 (with sharp) and KTX-Software 4.4.2 by full path from `.evani/bin/` (installed by `doctor --fix`, previous entry). Nothing is linked and nothing goes through PATH. A missing or changed tool stops the run before anything is written, with a `fix:` line naming `cargo xtask doctor --fix`. `cargo xtask convert` runs `cargo run --release -p evani-convert` from the engine root, with paths made absolute against the caller's folder, and passes exit codes 1 (problems found) and 2 (could not run) through.
- **Order.** glTF-Transform's `optimize` order: dedup, prune, weld, then per tier simplify (gltfpack `-si` / `-se`, attribute-aware), resize textures, encode texture sets, then quantize and meshopt-compress (gltfpack `-cc`) with `-kn -ke`. Tangents (MikkTSpace) by glTF-Transform `tangents` when a normal map needs them. Duplicate meshes become one mesh on several nodes (the engine draws them as instances); no EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing.
- **Per-tier ladder, levels named `<id>_lodK`.** `tools/evani-convert/ladder.json` holds each tier's LOD chain (gltfpack ratio, error limit, `min_screen` switch point) and its position, UV and normal bits. Each level is packed on its own and the levels are joined into one tier file under nodes `<id>_lod0`, `<id>_lod1`, ...; the input's part node names repeat once per level, while anchors (`anchor:` nodes with their extras) and the collision proxies stay on level 0 only, so their names are unique in the file. Ultra's level 0 is the master's geometry, only compressed. The master is copied untouched to `<id>.master.glb`. These are starting settings; the quality gate (S3-6) searches below them. They are not catalog limits.
- **Normals: 8 or 16 bits only.** gltfpack stores `-vn` widths other than 8 and 16 in the next wider integer without filling it, so `-vn 12` gave int16 normals of length 1/16 (ACCESSOR_VECTOR3_NON_UNIT). Found on Evani.Model's character, High tier. High now uses 16, and the converter refuses any other width in the ladder.
- **The validator twin.** The Khronos validator (glTF-Transform `validate`) cannot look inside EXT_meshopt_compression, so a compressed file's geometry passes unseen. Each tier is therefore also packed once more with the same settings without `-cc` (`.work/<tier>.packed.twin.glb`, never shipped) and that twin is validated too; its errors are the run's problems. The report shows both counts.
- **The shared model check (`evani_guide::modelcheck`).** Chris's rule that a mistake becomes a tool: the short-normals bug, LOD nodes that aren't in the file, referenced files that are missing and KTX2 files the engine's reader would refuse are checked with the engine's own reader (evani-gltf decodes meshopt, so it sees the real normals). The converter's read-back and `cargo xtask check` use the same code: `check` ERRORs, with a fix line, on every shipped model in a game, its kit or the shared `assets/models/` (masters and `.work` are skipped). evani-guide gains a path dependency on evani-gltf (its only dependency is serde_json), so the Stop hook still builds in seconds.
- **Texture size from the screen.** Per tier: rendered height (`target_resolution`, else the bench's 1080) x `render_scale` x `dpr_cap`, times the share of the screen height the model covers at its closest distance (`extras.evani.min_distance_m` or `--min-distance`; camera fov 50 degrees), rounded up to a power of two, between a floor of cap / 16 and a cap of the power of two at or above the rendered height. No distance: the cap. Never larger than the source image.
- **Texture sets.** The core glTF image stays a JPEG (opaque colour and data) or PNG (normal maps, alpha), the engine's RGBA fallback that any reader opens; `extras.evani.textures` lists `textures/<name>.<tier>.astc.ktx2` (ASTC 4x4, `--astc-quality medium`) and `.bc7.ktx2` (UASTC encoded, transcoded to BC7) beside it. Plain KTX2: no supercompression, mipmaps, sRGB for base colour and emissive, linear for data, which is what S3-3's reader accepts (the converter checks every file with the same rules). JPEG sources are decoded to PNG first (`ktx create` reads PNG only). No KHR_texture_basisu.
