- **Evani.Model files are never re-simplified.** `<name>.<tier>.glb` inputs with Evani metadata are only quantized and compressed per tier (their triangle count is checked equal), their normal-map PNGs are copied beside them and gain `sets { astc, bc7 }` in `extras.evani.normal_maps.maps[]`.
- **Same input, same bytes.** Tools run single-threaded where they have the option, and the converter writes JSON in a fixed order; two runs give identical files (test `same_input_same_bytes`; the reports differ only by the input's path).
- **No new crates.** brotli, flate2 and sha2 (`=0.10.9`) were already pinned in the workspace; evani-model's meshcheck and PNG writer and evani-core's manifest hash are path crates. No code was copied from the tools, so THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES is unchanged.
- **Tests and the tools.** The converter's tests need the pinned tools; on a checkout without them (no `doctor --fix` yet) they print `SKIPPED <test>` and pass, so the rest of the workspace still tests. With `EVANI_REQUIRE_TOOLS=1` a missing tool fails them instead; the S3-5 hand-over run used it. `cargo xtask check --full` runs them once per project (about 2 minutes), like the model reader's corpus test. Merging levels is refused (one level per tier, with a note) for skins, animations, cameras and any file-level or node extension data such as lights, whose indices the merge does not remap. The report lists a tier whose level 0 alone is over the tier's whole-frame triangle or draw-call limit as a problem, as it does for texture memory.
- **Gaps for Chris.** Low, Medium and High have no `target_resolution` in assets/tiers, so the bench's 1080 is used; High's texture cap (4096, from its DPR cap) is above Ultra's (2048: Ultra's `dpr_cap` is null, read as 1.0); the catalog has no texture floor or cap per tier, so the converter's cap / 16 floor is its own choice, reported in every report.

## MSDF text: sharp canvas text through the `ui` module (S3-13a, 2026-10-16)

- **2026-10-16 - The font: Atkinson Hyperlegible Regular (SIL OFL 1.1).** Made by the Braille Institute for low-vision readers: letters that are hard to confuse (I l 1, 0 O with a slashed zero, b d p q) stay readable at 12 px on a phone, which is where game UI text lives. Inter was the other candidate (also OFL); it is denser and more neutral but its I/l and small-size shapes are less distinct, and it is a larger file. The font sits in `assets/fonts/atkinson-hyperlegible/` with `OFL.txt`; a game lists it as `engine/atkinson-hyperlegible/AtkinsonHyperlegible-Regular.ttf`. Decision 9: open-licence fonts for UI text only, licence shipped (`fonts/<name>.LICENSE.txt`), and `cargo xtask check` errors on a listed font without a licence file beside it.
- **2026-10-16 - The tool: msdf-atlas-gen 1.4, pinned.** `cargo xtask doctor --fix` downloads the Windows x64 release zip, checks its SHA-256 (`55ab2bef...`, the one measured in spike (f)), unpacks the one exe into `.evani/bin/msdf-atlas-gen-1.4/` and records version and checksums in `.evani/bin/font-tools.json`; `doctor` without `--fix` only notes when it is missing (games without `[ui] fonts` don't need it). `--no-tools` skips it. Other platforms get a message saying to build that version into the same folder.
- **2026-10-16 - Atlas settings.** MTSDF (three channels for sharp corners, the true distance in alpha for outline, glow and shadow), 24 px per em, distance range 6 px, `-potr` packing, `-yorigin top`, 2 threads. A 24 px em with a 6 px range keeps 12 px text clean and lets the 200 px panel line and a 4 px outline stay sharp (captures in `.vibedeck/screenshots/s3-13a-msdf/`). The PNG is re-encoded at the png crate's best compression (msdf-atlas-gen writes a quick one): 71.9 KB to 69.4 KB. The browser decodes it (`createImageBitmap`), so no image decoder ships.
- **2026-10-16 - Charsets.** `ascii` (U+0020 to U+007E), `latin` (ASCII plus curly quotes, dashes, ellipsis, bullet, euro, degree, copyright, trade mark; the default) and `latin1` (U+00A0 to U+00FF, the accented letters). Space and `?` are always baked, and only listed characters are baked (`only_listed_charsets_are_baked`); anything else draws `?`. Complex scripts and typed input stay in the DOM.
- **2026-10-16 - The table, `.evf`.** Our own compact binary (magic `EVF1`, counts, metrics, per glyph the code point, advance and plane and atlas boxes in fixed point, kerning pairs as glyph index pairs); em values are 1/4096 steps. Kerning comes from the font's GPOS `kern` feature (pair adjustments, formats 1 and 2, all its lookups summed), falling back to the old `kern` table, read with ttf-parser at bake time; msdf-atlas-gen reads only the old table, which modern fonts like this one lack. Atkinson latin: 107 glyphs, 352 kerning pairs, 4.4 KB table, 67.8 KB atlas (256 x 256). The bake is cached in `target/evani-fonts/<app>/` by a key over the font, charsets, settings and tool version.
- **2026-10-16 - Runtime.** `evani-render` feature `msdf` (implies `text`); the app feature `ui` turns it on. `TextStyle` gained `font` (`FontId::BITMAP` by default, so every existing call keeps the 5 x 9 bitmap font for debug and pixel looks), `max_width`, and `glow`. Fonts are slots by name (`rw.fonts.request("ui")`, up to 4 fonts of 1,024 glyphs: the font and glyph fit the sprite cell's spare bits, so text goes through the existing sprite batcher and its layer order). Layout is one pass with a running pen (advance plus kerning), greedy wrap at `max_width`, all spaces at a wrap dropped, left, centre or right per line, line starts and baselines on whole pixels. The fragment variant `shaders/sprite_msdf.wgsl` takes the median of the three channels with a screen-space smoothstep (`fwidth`) and draws outline, glow and shadow from the alpha channel, under the fill on the caller's layer.
- **2026-10-16 - New dependencies (tools only, never in a game).** `ttf-parser =0.25.1` (xtask; GPOS kerning; no default features, `std` and `opentype-layout`), `png =0.18.1` (xtask; re-encodes the atlas), `sha2 =0.10.9` (evani-guide; checks the tool download, the version xtask, geocheck and lookguard already use).
- **2026-10-16 - Text sits under bloom and the tone map.** The sprite pass draws into the HDR target, so screen text gets the scene's bloom, vignette and tone map like every sprite: ink above about 1.0 blooms (a halo round big letters), and 1.0 comes out a light grey, not white. The proof panel uses ink 1.0. A UI pass after the tone map would change every game's frame, so it is not part of S3-13a; it is a note for S3-13b (the box layout and HUD).
- **2026-10-17 - No rotated MSDF text.** Glyph quads are always axis-aligned (rotation 0); `TextStyle` has no rotation and the spec doesn't ask for one. Screen UI text is upright; rotated labels can come later if a game needs them.
- **2026-10-17 - Outlines follow the true distance, so small counters close or leave pinholes.** The outline is the glyph grown by `OUTLINE_EM` (0.08 em) on the MTSDF's true-distance channel. A counter whose deepest point is less than 0.08 em from the letter closes (Atkinson's `e`: 0.062 em); one just deeper keeps a tiny hole (the slashed zero's two counters: 0.086 and 0.087 em, measured in the baked atlas), which at 200 px showed as specks about one pixel across (a hole finer than the 24 px/em atlas can place, so its exact spot follows the texel grid). The outline pass now closes such holes: a pixel counts as outline when the outline covers it on both sides along any of four directions within the larger of 0.75 screen px and half an atlas texel, so holes under about 1.5 px (or 1 texel) wide fill and open counters and outer edges stay as they were. Only the outline style pays for it (8 extra atlas reads on its edge pixels).
- **2026-10-17 - Size: over the planned 4 KB, against either baseline.** Which baseline: the plan's 4 KB reads as "for games with `ui`", and `ui` turns on `msdf`, which implies `text` (plan section 4), so the honest baseline is the game without any text (base): **+17,066 B gz wasm**. Against a game that already draws bitmap text the step is +6,095. Both are over 4 KB. Chris's answer (2026-10-17): "Accept now, split it apart in a later step": the split (`msdf` without the bitmap font and the art packer, about -10 KB for a game that only wants MSDF text) is its own later step, since it touches code every game compiles. Sandbox ship build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9, `models` on as in the sandbox) with a probe that draws one line, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT` pinned: no text 262,201 B gz; the existing `text` feature with one bitmap `text::draw` 273,172 (+10,971, the bitmap font plus the `sprite-art` packer and mips it needs); `ui` (`msdf`) with one MSDF `text::draw` 279,267, so **MSDF's own share is +6,095 B gz wasm** (+288 B gz js, the fetch and `createImageBitmap` glue), and a game with no text at all pays +17,066 B gz wasm for `ui`. Where it goes (raw, before wasm-opt): the font fetch, table parse and atlas upload future about 4.2 KB, the layout (inlined where `text::draw` is called) about 2.4 KB, the per-frame font work and the MSDF sprite packer about 2.4 KB, then the bind group, texture and kerning lookups. Shrinks kept: one small fetch helper with no error formatting, the proof panel's literal size labels (no float printing in the sandbox's proof build), the MSDF sprite WGSL shipped without comments through S3-3's compile-time stripper (-344, `msdf_wgsl_ships_without_comments_but_with_marker_and_lines`), short `font table evf<n>` error codes in the wasm with the full text natively (-117), no `format!` in the error path. Left for later if size matters more: one combined font file (one fetch instead of two), and letting `msdf` stop pulling in the bitmap font and the art packer (+10,971 for a game that only wants MSDF text). Font files are streamed assets: Atkinson latin 67.8 KB atlas plus 4.4 KB table. Games without `ui` are unchanged (SHA-1 table in the merge commit).

## Box layout: `evani-ui` and the sandbox HUD panel (S3-13b, 2026-10-17)

- **2026-10-17 - Design.** `crates/evani-ui`, no dependency, no allocation, no unsafe. A `Ui` is a flat preorder arena of at most 128 boxes (`CAP`) on the stack: `open` pushes a box, `text` pushes a leaf, `close` records the box's `end` index, so a subtree is a slice and there are no pointers. `layout(origin, avail, measure)` runs per axis: bottom-up preferred and minimum sizes (a text leaf's minimum is its longest word; a scroll box's is 0), top-down distribution (fixed as asked, fit as its contents, grow as contents plus a weighted share of the free room; when the room is short, fit and grow boxes shrink toward their minimum, then overflow), then placement (padding, gap, alignment on both axes, scroll offset clamped to the range, children clipped to their scroll box). The width pass runs before the height pass, so a wrapped paragraph's height follows its final width. Output (`commands()`): rectangles rounded to whole pixels and clipped, and text runs with their wrap width, each with its nesting depth (the game maps depth to a sprite layer). A text run not wholly inside the clip above it is left out, since glyph quads cannot be cut: scroll lists by whole rows. A full tree drops the extra boxes and counts them (`dropped()`), never panics. The idea of a Clay-style box layout (one pass of sizing rules, arena tree, measure callback) was borrowed as an idea only; no outside code, no dependency (Chris's rule: Evani.Dev's tools are our own code).
- **2026-10-17 - The measure callback.** `|text, max_width| -> [w, h]`, `max_width` 0 meaning one line. The game wires it to `text::measure_in(&rw, s, &style)` (from S3-13a, the same layout `text::draw` uses), so the layout's wrap and the drawn wrap are one function. Nothing in `evani-ui` knows about fonts.
- **2026-10-17 - `Mode::Solid`: a hard-edged rectangle in the MSDF sprite variant.** Box fills first used an atlas square stretched to panel size; its edge fades over about 1.5 atlas texels, so it blurred (3x zoom capture). A workaround of overlapping 64 px tiles rippled the outer border where the tiles doubled alpha. The fix is a fourth cell mode (`Mode::Solid`, `msdf::cell(0, Mode::Solid, 0)`): `pack_sprites` gives it no uv and `pad = [1, 3, 0]`, and the fragment shader returns the sprite colour untouched (`mode > 2.5`). It needs no font, takes a translucent colour, and lives only in `msdf.rs` and `sprite_msdf.wgsl`, which only `ui` games compile, so the art variant every other game ships is unchanged (SHA-1 table). Test: `solid_cell_packs_a_plain_rectangle_without_a_font`.
- **2026-10-17 - Size: +2,668 B gz wasm, within the 3 KB budget.** Sandbox ship build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9, features of `xtask features sandbox --ship` plus `ui`, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=a9d7522`): the S3-13a text-panel build 282,189 B, this build (HUD panel on the layout crate, the solid mode) 284,857 B, so **+2,668 B gz wasm** and +0 js. The number includes the HUD proof code and replaces the text proof panel, so the engine's own share is a little smaller. Games without `ui` pay nothing.
- **2026-10-17 - Finding: HUD text sits under bloom and the tone map, and no UI pass was built.** In the captures (`.vibedeck/screenshots/s3-13b-ui/`, High 1280x720, Low 1280x720, phone 844x390@3, short 700x360, and a 3x zoom) the dark panel fill is lifted to a blue-grey and ink 1.0 reads light grey; button text (dim ink on a mid-blue button) is the weakest contrast; on High a bright orb's bloom halo spills over the panel's corner. The panel is readable on all three views and looks the same on phone and PC (it is laid out in CSS pixels). A UI pass after the tone map would give true-white text and an untouched panel colour, but it changes every game's frame, so it is **not built**: a note for Chris. The proof panel's colours were picked by looking at the captures (darker button, ink 1.0).
- **2026-10-17 - Sandbox features.** `ui` shows the HUD panel (`hudpanel.rs`); `text-proof = ["ui"]` shows the S3-13a text panel instead. On a screen under 480 px high the HUD panel is top left with three list rows so it clears the debug panel at the top right and fits a 360 px high view.
- **2026-10-17 - Checks.** `cargo test -p evani-ui` (`layout_matches_golden_boxes`, `layout_does_not_allocate` with a counting allocator, `same_tree_same_boxes`, six rule tests); the shader test `msdf_wgsl_validates` covers the solid branch. The SHA-1 gate for non-users is in the merge commit.

## Model handles, LOD and tiered streaming (S3-4, 2026-10-17)

- **Handles.** `Models::handle("<id>")` returns a `ModelHandle` at once; `resolve(h)` is `None` until the file for the settled tier is ready, and the kit draws its own placeholder meanwhile (the sandbox: a grey post and ball per copy). A handle names a model, not a file: on each tier it draws `<id>.<tier>.glb` when the manifest lists one, else the shared `<id>.glb`, so a lower tier gets a lighter file of the same look (Chris's rule). Nothing is fetched before the tier settles (after the first-run warm-up), so a tier never downloads another tier's files; after a tier switch the old file stays on screen until the new one is ready. Render only: the simulation never reads handles, LODs or streams (`sim_is_identical_with_and_without_streamed_models`); anything gameplay needs comes from the boot group or build-time kit data.
- **LOD inside a tier file.** Levels from `extras.evani.lods` (most detailed first, each with `min_screen`); each drawn instance gets the level for its projected bounding-sphere height (largest over the views), with a 15% hysteresis band either side of every threshold, remembered per instance slot (`lod_hysteresis_does_not_flicker`). Parts outside any level are shared and drawn at every level.
- **Manifest v2 behind a feature.** `models/stream.json` (`cargo xtask manifest <id>` writes it; `cargo xtask check` errors when it is stale): per entry `tier`, `priority`, `group` (`boot` or later) and an optional `offset` for packed files; v1 manifests still read with every entry shared, boot, priority 0 (`manifest_v1_still_reads`). The v2 fields sit behind evani-core's new `stream` feature, which only evani-render `models` turns on, because even unread fields changed every other game's wasm. The browser reads the file through `serde_json::Value` (already linked by the model reader) instead of a derived deserialiser (-1.9 KB gz).
- **The `stream` shim** (`crates/evani-render/js/stream.js`, a wasm-bindgen snippet, so only games with `models` get it): fetch with an optional `Range` header and fetch priority, Cache Storage under one shared name keyed by content hash. Policy: files of 64 KB or more go through Cache Storage, smaller ones rely on the HTTP cache; a cached copy is used only if its FNV-1a hash still matches, otherwise (or when the browser evicted it) the file is downloaded again (`evicted_cache_refetches`); every download is hash-checked. `navigator.storage.persist()` is asked once, only on Chromium browsers (S3-0: they never prompt). The boot group is fetched at once (`Models::boot_ready` is what a title screen waits for); later groups follow by priority, one file at a time at low fetch priority, only after a frame has been drawn since boot and while no handle is still waiting for its own file. Texture entries are left out of the background plan: the model that samples one fetches the one format the device can use.
- **The superseded rule (bug found and its check).** The first Low run downloaded the shared `frontier-marshal.glb` as well as its own `.low.glb`. A shared entry that a tier has its own copy of (`hero.glb` when `hero.low.glb` exists, likewise textures) is now never fetched on that tier (`low_requests_only_low_files`), and `cargo xtask netlog <id> --tier low,ultra` checks it in a real browser for any game: it serves the ship build on ports 8240-8249, opens headless muted Chrome per tier (tier seeded in storage, throttled line), records CDP `Network.requestWillBeSent` and fails on another tier's file, a superseded shared file, a file that did not arrive, or no model at all; it prints the line speed and when each tier's model was ready.
- **Stale snippet (bug found and its check).** A non-user's ship folder carried `snippets/.../stream.js` copied from wasm-bindgen's shared output folder (its page never loaded it). `cargo xtask build --ship` now clears that folder first, and the ship guard fails on any snippet the page's js never imports (`snippet_the_page_never_imports_is_an_orphan`).
- **Size.** Sandbox ship build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=a9d7522`): wasm 262,201 → 270,829 B gz, **+8,628** (budget 3 KB); js +56 B gz plus the shim 768 B gz, **+824** (budget 2 KB). Shrinks kept: the Value-based manifest read (-1.9 KB), a plain insertion sort instead of the standard stable sort for the fetch plan, the superseded test without string formatting and one future type for both fetch groups (together -1.7 KB). What remains is spread over the tier sync, the fetch plan, the cached fetch path, the LOD choice and per-level draw runs, and the sandbox's own placeholder. **Decided 2026-10-17: accepted by Chris.** Asked whether to accept about 8.4 KB gz wasm against the 3 KB plan or keep cutting, Chris answered "Accept it as it is". This replaces the earlier acceptance by EVANI AI while he was away; no streaming trim is planned. This records the size decision only; Gate 3 is still Chris's to sign. Games without `models` are unchanged: ship wasm and js SHA-1 identical (table in the merge commit).

## Animation clip block and baking (S3-7, 2026-10-17)

- **Our own fixed-layout block, not glTF animations, in tier files.** The master keeps the standard glTF animations; each tier file carries one `EVC1` block in its binary chunk, named by `extras.evani.clips` (docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md "Clips"). Fixed little-endian layouts, per-clip headers, per-track headers, no general-purpose compression: the in-game decoder (S3-8) is a few loops and table reads, aimed at 1 to 2 KB of wasm. One reference reader and writer (`evani_guide::clipblock`) is shared by the converter, the model check and S3-8.
- **30 Hz samples, key frame numbers instead of times.** Every channel is resampled at 30 Hz, so a key is a frame number (1 byte up to 256 frames, else 2) and the decoder never searches float times. A clip starts at 0 s and ends at its last key time.
- **Rotations smallest-three, two widths chosen per clip by the error.** 10-10-10-2 in 32 bits when the bound allows, else 3 x 15 in 48 bits; the converter tries 32 first and keeps the smaller clip within the bound. Translations and scales are 16-bit over each track's own range (f32 min and extent stored once per track). A track that never changes is stored once in full f32 (12 or 16 bytes); one at the rest pose is dropped, so the tier file's rest transform is used.
- **Error at virtual shell points (ACL's idea, our code).** Six points around each joint at its shell distance (farthest direct child, rigid mesh radius, at least 3 cm), in world space, against the master resampled at 30 Hz, over every frame and every node below an animated one, measured on the decoded bytes. Per-track tolerances (translation x parent scale, 2 sqrt(1 - dot^2) x shell, relative scale x shell) drive a greedy forward key reduction on quantised values; the tolerance halves (bound / 2^k) until the whole clip is within the bound. Bounds per tier in the converter's ladder.json (`clip_error_m`: lite 2 mm, low 1, medium 0.5, high 0.3, ultra 0.2, vr 0.3), overridable with `--clip-error`; the quality gate (S3-6) will search this setting like the LOD settings.
- **Nodes by name.** Animated nodes and skin joints get unique names before packing (gltfpack keeps named nodes with `-kn`), gltfpack packs a copy without animations, and the clips name each tier file's node index; the converter checks that the tier file kept the master's rest pose for every animated node. An animated model gets one level of detail per tier file for now. Morph-target weights are not baked (no morph targets in the engine yet).
- **Same bytes on one platform and toolchain, not across them.** Tracks are sorted and nothing reads a clock or a hash map, but slerp's `sin` and `acos` come from the platform maths library; a last-bit difference could keep a different key elsewhere (still within the bound). Exact cross-platform bytes would need our own transcendental functions; not worth it for a dev tool whose output is committed once.
- **Bounds that cannot be met say why.** 16-bit translations over a long range (root motion) have an error floor of range / 131,070; when a clip stays over its bound with every key kept, the report names the track and the cause rather than storing f32 (a second value layout the decoder would have to carry).
- **No new dependencies.** Resampling, key reduction and encoding are plain Rust written for Evani.Dev (Chris's own-tools rule); `evani-gltf/anim-source` is turned on only by evani-convert (native), never by a game.

## Detail rules and part variants (S3-11, 2026-10-17)

docs/CONTENT.md has the four rules and the API. Choices behind it:

- **Node bits are the rank in the sorted name list, capped at 32 names.** Hide and recolour act on node names (a name covers every part under that node). Sorting makes equal node sets give equal bits in every tier file of a model, so bits looked up on one tier stay right after a tier switch. A model with more than 32 distinct node names keeps the first 32 in sorted order; the rest cannot be hidden or painted.
- **Recolour multiplies, never sets.** Paints go into a per-frame side list on `Models` (a range per draw), so `ModelInstance` stays `Copy` and there is no allocation per frame once the list has grown (65,535 entries per frame at most). Nothing does string work per frame: names are resolved once per base file.
- **Anchors are render data.** `anchor(name)` gives an index and `anchor_frame` the world frame, kept as the node matrix and decomposed only when used. Gameplay never reads them (it reads kit data or the boot group), so models stay render-only.
- **`Variant` is all or nothing.** `draw` returns false until the base and every part that can load are ready, so a variant never shows half built; a failed part file is skipped; names the base lacks are listed in `unresolved` for one error log.
- **Guide rules are warnings only.** A boot group over `[stream] boot_max_kb` (default 1024) and a hero model without per-tier files warn (hero = listed in `[content] hero`, or a shared `.glb` over `[content] hero_kb`, default 256); both are parameters, not rules.

## Animation runtime: `evani-anim` (S3-8, 2026-10-17)

- **Pure CPU scalar code, evani-core only.** The crate depends on evani-core just for its engine module and parameter (as evani-physics does): no glTF reader, no renderer, no new external crate. A game gets it only through `anim` (`anim = ["models", "dep:evani-anim"]` in every app crate, off in all of them). The cross-check against the reference reader lives in evani-convert's tests, so evani-anim never depends on evani-guide.
- **Decode once at load, into flat tables.** `Clips::decode` checks the whole `EVC1` block and unpacks every track into one keys table (u16 frame numbers) and one values table (`[f32; 4]`, smallest-three and 16-bit values already expanded). Sampling is then a cursor walk and one lerp or nlerp per track, with no bit unpacking per frame; a few KB of RAM per character set buys the simplest and smallest per-frame path. Bad blocks are `Error`s, never panics.
- **Same bits as the reference reader, and on every machine.** The decoder keeps clipblock.rs's order of floating-point operations, so samples match it bit for bit (`anim_decoder_matches_the_reference_reader`: five bounds, both rotation widths, forward and backward through one cursor, and the same refusals of corrupted blocks). Sampling, blending and IK use only `+ - * /` and `sqrt` (IK by geometry, no `acos`; soften as a rational curve), whose results IEEE 754 fixes, so a pose is the same on every browser and CPU.
- **Skinning matrices for S3-9: `Mat43`, row-major 3x4.** The top three rows of the 4x4 (48 bytes per joint), one flat slice per character from `Rig::skinning`, model(joint) x inverse bind in joint order. In WGSL the three rows read as the columns of a `mat3x4<f32>`, applied as `vec4(p, 1) * m`.
- **Simulation and render kept apart.** Gameplay reads only `Player` (state, integer ticks, crossfade progress), `Blend1d` phase and `Player::hit` events, stepped in the fixed 60 Hz update (two ticks per 30 Hz frame). Frame positions with the render sub-tick, crossfade weights, poses, IK and skinning are made from that state each drawn frame and never write back. Clip lengths in ticks come from kit data (or `Clips::ticks` at load), so the simulation never waits on a model download.
- **Three node types.** Clip, blend of two (w at 0 or 1 gives exactly one input), masked additive (base x nlerp(identity, conj(reference) x layer, mask x w); a joint with mask weight 0 keeps the base's bits). `Graph` preallocates its slots, so evaluation does no allocation.
- **IK assumes uniform positive scale on the chain.** Two-bone (pole vector, soften, weight; bones keep their length; non-finite or huge targets give a finite pose) and aim (forward axis at the target, up towards a pole) take rotations from the local rotations down the chain, which is right only without non-uniform or negative scale. Rigs from Evani.Model and the converter have none; a rig that does gets a wrong bend, not NaN.
- **One parameter.** `anim.crossfade_ticks` (default 12, 0 to 120, owner `anim`), the crossfade a kit uses when it gives none of its own.
- **Size: over the planned 6 KB, accepted by Chris for now.** Sandbox ship build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=a9d7522`, `models` on as in the sandbox) with a temporary probe system (removed) that every frame decodes the rig fixture's clip block, builds a skeleton, skin, graph and rig, evaluates two clips, a blend and two masked additive nodes, steps a player and a 1D blend space, then runs model space, two-bone IK, aim IK, an attachment point and the skinning matrices. Without `anim` 277,151 B gz wasm; the first version 296,941 (+19,790); **final 286,451 B gz, +9,300 B gz wasm** (+18,905 B raw; js unchanged within 1 B). With `anim` on and nothing calling it: 277,160 B gz, the same raw size (628,831 B): a game pays only for what it calls. Shrinks kept: (1) `f32::clamp` with bounds that are not constants keeps its `min <= max` assert, whose message drags in the whole float formatter: max then min instead, **-10.1 KB gz** (the guard `no_clamp_with_computed_bounds` keeps it out); (2) the skeleton's parents-first order by one pass per depth instead of std's stable sort; (3) short error texts in the wasm (`err!`: full text natively, `anim: evc1 track header` and the like in the download) and one error per header kind; (4) element-wise vector maths as array maps; (2) to (4) together -302 B gz in the game (more in a standalone probe, where nothing else shares the sort and string code); then a bounded last pass, (5) one interpolation body for all three channels in the sampler and (6) the after-IK model-space update without a marks buffer, -74 B gz. Before the shrinks +19,790, after (1) +9,676, after (2) to (4) +9,374, after (5) and (6) +9,300. Where the rest goes (raw, before wasm-opt, each function kept out of line in a standalone probe): the decoder 3.7 KB (clip parse 2.2, block header 0.9, readers 0.6), two-bone IK 2.5, the graph 2.2 (eval 0.9, additive 0.8, blend 0.5), skeleton, skin and rig setup 2.0, sampling 1.2, aim 0.8, model space 0.6; the rest is the probe's own setup, the player and the blend space. What a further cut would cost: IK as its own opt-in (a feature and a check; about 1.5 to 2 KB gz off for games that never reach), or a decoder that leans on the block's own sizes instead of checking each field (about 1 KB gz, but a weaker refusal of bad files and a re-proof against the reference reader); the 6 KB figure was an estimate made before the code existed. Asked Chris (2026-10-17) and merged over budget. **Decided 2026-10-17: accepted.** Chris: "Accept about 9 KB now, shrink later" (the measured +9,300 B gz wasm against the 6 KB plan). Shrinking later is tracked in STAGES.md's Parking lot, "Models and anim size trim" (S3-4's streaming size is not part of it: Chris accepted it as it is), with the two cuts named above: IK as its own opt-in (about 1.5 to 2 KB gz) and lighter clip checks in the decoder (about 1 KB gz). This records the size decision only; Gate 3 is still Chris's to sign. Games without `anim` carry none of it (SHA-1 evidence in the S3-8 merge commit).

## GPU skinning (S3-9, 2026-10-17)

- **One feature, reached only through `anim`.** `evani-render/skinning` (implies `models`) is turned on by every app crate's `anim` (`anim = ["models", "dep:evani-anim", "evani-render/skinning"]`), never on its own; a game without `anim` compiles none of it. Shared files carry it as same-line `#[cfg(feature = "skinning")]` hooks at existing line ends (panic locations in non-anim games stay put) and the rest lives in new files: `model/skin.rs` (CPU), `gpu/models/skin.rs` (GPU), `shaders/model_skin.wgsl` and `model_skin_shadows.wgsl`.
- **Linear blend skinning in the vertex stage, about 30 lines of WGSL.** `model_skin_vs` blends four `mat3x4<f32>` from one read-only storage buffer, folds the blend into the instance rows and then does what `model_vs` does (the same fragment stage and material). Normals and the baked sun cone turn by the cofactor of the folded matrix, tangents as vectors. Skinned parts draw after the static runs with their own pipeline (the model layout plus the joints group); shadows use `model_skin_shadow_vs` in the model shadow module.
- **One joint buffer a frame, per-instance offset.** Every drawn character's matrices (48 bytes each, evani-anim's row-major `Mat43` read as the rows of a `mat3x4`) go into one storage buffer, uploaded once; the instance's position-offset w (unused by static models) holds its first matrix. A posed draw (`Models::draw_posed(instance, rig.skinning(&skin))`) gets its own matrices; every unposed draw of a skinned model shares one copy of the model's bind palette. A posed draw with the wrong matrix count draws the bind pose, with one error in the overlay (`joint_offsets_per_instance` covers it), rather than reading another character's joints. The GPU side works out each draw's offset once a frame (`joint_offsets`, kept in the model list's pose state) and `Models::stage`, which S3-11's hidden nodes and recolours also go through, writes it into every instance it stages, so hidden parts and paints work on skinned models as on static ones.
- **With surface relief (S3-10).** A game with both `relief` and `anim` gets one forward module with both extra stages (model, relief, skin, then the shadow tail), and the skinned pipeline uses the relief fragment stage there, as the static model pipeline does; `skinning_wgsl_validates` checks that mix too.
- **Precision cut where players don't look.** Joints as u8x4 (256 joints at most; `K2` refuses more), weights as unorm8x4 renormalised on load to sum to exactly 255 (the largest takes the rounding), 8 extra bytes a vertex. The positions, normals and textures stay at the tier's own precision, so the look is unchanged. Soft cap 64 joints: over it a rig draws, but the converter report and `cargo xtask check` warn (each drawn character uploads one matrix per joint each frame).
- **Same look on Lite and Low: the sun cone is baked in bind pose.** Lite and Low have no shadow map; models get their self-shadow from a per-vertex sun cone (S3-3) baked over a model's own geometry, which a moving limb would invalidate. Options weighed: re-bake per pose (far too slow on a phone), drop self-shadow for characters (sheds the look, against Chris's rule) or bake once in bind pose and carry the cone with the limb. Chosen: the bake sees each skinned part through a proxy skinned into model space with the bind matrices (`skin::proxies`), bakes as for a static model, then `skin::unproxy` carries each vertex's cone back into its own space with the transpose of its blended bind matrix (the inverse of the shader's cofactor turn, up to scale), so at draw time the shader turns the cone with the limb exactly as it turns the normal. In bind pose the result is the static model's cone (`sun_cone_survives_the_bind_pose_round_trip`); in a pose the occluders are where they were in bind pose, which for a character's own body is the right answer for most of a clip and costs nothing per frame. The shadow on the ground under a character on Lite and Low is the kit's blob disc, as for every other model there.
- **Bind pose draws like the static mesh.** The CPU test `bind_pose_matches_the_static_mesh` skins the rig fixture's vertices with its bind palette and compares them with `rig.static.glb` (the same tube with no skin); the capture comparison (sandbox `skin-proof`, `?skin=bind` against `?skin=static`, same camera, all five tiers, the kit's drifting screen sprites dropped from the proof's frames) differs in no pixel at all, on the rig or anywhere else, on Lite, Low, Medium, High and Ultra.
- **Skinned shadows follow the pose.** The proof's three panels bend three ways under the same sun: straight (bind), bent away from the sun (idle plus a hand bend: a long shadow) and bent towards it (`pose`: a short one). The fixture's tube is open at both ends, so where it points along the light its shadow is a ring, which Medium's coarser map shows as a broken outline; that is the fixture's shape, not the skinning (the shadow pass reads the same joint buffer and instance offsets as the main pass).
- **What the S3-6 quality gate would need for skinned models:** build its renderer with `skinning` and draw the model through `Models::draw` (no pose): that is the bind pose, which already looks like the static mesh, so no evani-anim and no clip is needed.
- **Public API that remains (2026-10-20, review).** `ModelInstance::joints` and the helpers `blend`, `bind_positions`, `proxies` and `unproxy` are `pub(crate)`: the joint slot is set by `Models::draw_posed` and nothing outside the renderer should write it, and the helpers are the bind-pose bake's internals. (`proxies` and `unproxy` are also built only for wasm and tests, as only the GPU model bake and the tests call them, so a native build carries no dead code.) What a game can use, all behind `skinning`: `Models::draw_posed`, the `model::skin` module with `Mat43` and its constants (`MAX_JOINTS`, `SOFT_JOINTS`, the skin-stream sizes), `Part::skin`, `Prepared::bind` and `Pipelines::skin`.
- **Needs core WebGPU.** The joint matrices are a read-only storage buffer read in the vertex stage, so skinning needs vertex-stage storage buffers: core WebGPU, not compatibility mode, which allows none in the vertex stage. A device that cannot give one is, for a game with `anim`, the same as a device without WebGPU; the engine has no WebGL or compatibility fallback anyway (AGENTS.md).
- **Size: over the 2 KB budget, not trimmed (2026-10-20).** Sandbox ship build (`cargo xtask build sandbox --ship`: Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=a9d7522`, `models` and `anim` on, on the tip after merging m1-foundation ff4f189), measured as S3-8 did, with a temporary probe (removed) that makes one posed draw (`Models::draw_posed` with one matrix) in place of one `Models::draw` in the proof model system: `anim` alone (no `evani-render/skinning`) 290,178 B gz wasm (654,363 B raw, js 17,718 gz); `anim` with skinning and the posed draw **296,862 B gz wasm, +6,684 B gz** (670,224 B raw, +15,861; js 17,720 gz, +2). That is about 3.3 times the 2 KB budget. It was not trimmed (the brief was to record it). **Decided (Chris, 2026-10-20): accepted for now, and the trim is a priority next step** (lightness first; the open "Size trim" task in STAGES.md Stage 3). Where the bytes go has not been broken down; the skinned vertex and shadow stages join the forward and shadow modules, a second model pipeline and its layouts, the joint buffer and the bind-pose cone bake all sit behind the one feature.
- **Games without `anim` are byte-identical (2026-10-20).** Ship builds (`cargo xtask build <app> --ship`, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=a9d7522`) on m1-foundation ff4f189 and on the S3-9 tip give the same SHA-1 for the wasm and the js (full hashes below; base = tip in every row; gz wasm in bytes):

  | game | wasm SHA-1 | js SHA-1 | gz wasm |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 2d-platformer | 47b711bab02bd5138fcadd1cf9d328a3c5e02d3f | 3e4e0704a9947a038e6ca8eea9eabb511f77887f | 288,745 |
  | match-3 | 10687623e996833f3b4206c5ea9c4076d72a9da5 | 19b69c6854cd764c881d214d8261b249f0ea6aac | 303,552 |
  | kart-racer | c92d1bad7a1b1df51959dffffc5080aec1645014 | 1154b914d097e7d041d751dcd56cb7aed91bfc2a | 367,430 |
  | sandbox (default) | 85b92bfcf34df8f2458496723f41a45a3ca4391b | 2ad2ea4c7d47b00a0ca5eca923ff6c2f12bcab5d | 290,165 |
  | model-view (default) | 14b04953c00fbcc69d9cdac0c3d56a26eff4ce7c | 37c3aba012f95531d83efe7bda0cf8405b8b4a1d | 253,657 |

  Built only; no game was run, played or captured for this table.
- **Bench, all six tiers, character phase passes (2026-10-20).** `cargo xtask bench` (headless Chrome 155 on the RTX 4060 Ti, muted, pointer and fullscreen locks stubbed by the runner, below-normal priority) with the character phase (256 posed rigs, 300 frames). Run 2 failed Lite only, in the 2D phase (p99 37.5 ms over the 33.3 ms floor) while other workers held the PC near 98% CPU; its Lite character phase was avg 0.54 ms, p99 1.2 ms. Run 3, with the PC quiet, **passed 6 of 6 tiers** and the GPU cost order holds. Character phase, run 3 (avg / p95 / p99 ms, GPU ms, triangles): Lite 0.55 / 1.0 / 1.8, 0.066, 10,682; Low 0.66 / 0.9 / 1.3, 0.098, 13,610; Medium 0.98 / 4.5 / 6.3, 0.472, 28,982; High 1.07 / 2.0 / 6.5, 0.779, 108,770; Ultra 1.89 / 2.9 / 3.3, 1.586, 308,606; VR 1.02 / 5.3 / 7.3, 0.367, 46,550. (Run 1 hit a transient Windows file lock on Chrome's profile and never started; it was re-run with nothing changed.)
- **Bind pose against the static mesh, redone on the rebased tip (2026-10-20).** Sandbox `skin-proof` (`trunk build --features skin-proof`), `?skin=bind` against `?skin=static`, same camera, 1280x720, 8 s, overlay hidden, headless muted Chrome, on Lite, Low, Medium, High and Ultra: **mean difference 0, max 0, no pixel over 8 levels** on every tier, over the rig's region (400x520 px = 208,000 pixels) and over the rest of the frame (713,600 pixels). The pictures show the rig, so it is not blank against blank. Pictures and the difference sheet are in `.evani-shot-s3-9/captures/` (git-ignored; `compare.json`, `sheet-bind-vs-static.png`).
- **model-view's `gate` feature stays without `skinning` (2026-10-20).** Its comment said `anim` would join `gate` at S3-9. Adding `skinning` would also change how the gate treats skinned models (it skips a skinned master as "not gated" today), which is a change to the S3-6 gate's behaviour and tests, not a feature-line edit; so the smaller, safer fix was taken: the comment now says what is true, and the follow-up (turn on `skinning` in `gate`, draw the bind pose, drop the "not gated: skinned model" skip, add the clip error to the ladder) is filed in STAGE3_PLAN S3-9.

## The HUD after post: the `ui` pass (S3-13b fix, 2026-10-17)

- **2026-10-17 - A `ui` render pass after the tone map, only in `ui` games.** The S3-13b finding ("HUD text sits under bloom and the tone map") is fixed: with `msdf` (which `ui` turns on) the standard graph gets one more pass, `ui` (owner `render`, reads and writes `ldr`, order 900: after the tone map at 100 and every effect on `ldr`, the vignette at 200, and before `present` at 1000). It draws the HUD sprites with the existing sprite batcher and the MSDF fragment variant (text and `Mode::Solid` boxes) into the `Rgba8UnormSrgb` `ldr` target, so a HUD colour lands as authored: linear 1.0 is 255, no bloom halo, no vignette, no exposure, no tone curve. Blending stays in linear light (the sRGB target encodes on write), as in the scene. The pass is a pipeline key `Key::Ui` (the sprite shader, `ldr` format) and a `Gpu::draw_ui` method, both behind `msdf`; games without `ui` compile none of it and their wasm is byte-identical (SHA-1 table in the merge).
- **2026-10-17 - What goes after post: a layer rule, `msdf::HUD_LAYER` (16384).** Every sprite is already in screen space, so the split is by layer: sprites on `HUD_LAYER` and above are the screen HUD (panels, menus, buttons, scores, timers, bars) and draw in the `ui` pass; sprites below are the scene (2D world art and text that belongs to the world: a name over a kart, a floating score where a match popped, a sign) and keep bloom and the tone map like the art around them. The sorted sprite list is cut once (`hud_from`, a binary search); the sprites pass draws the scene part, the `ui` pass the rest. Screen particle groups always draw in the scene (their split is clamped below the HUD). A layer rule, not a flag per sprite: no change to `Sprite` (shared by every game), and a HUD stays one sorted run. Written in docs/UI_LAYOUT.md "HUD after post".
- **2026-10-17 - The sandbox panel's colours are kept.** The HUD panel and the text proof move to `HUD_LAYER + 40`; their linear colours are unchanged, so the panel fill now shows its authored near-black (sRGB 39, 44, 60) instead of the lifted blue-grey, and ink 1.0 shows white.
- **2026-10-17 - Checks.** `hud_draws_after_the_tone_map` (graph order: `ui` after `tonemap` and every `ldr` effect, right before `present`), `hud_layers_split_after_the_scene` (the cut and the pass), and `hud_white_stays_white` (`node tools/hud-check/run.mjs`: the sandbox `ui` build on Lite, Low, Medium, High and Ultra at 1280x720 and on a phone 844x390@3; each capture's brightest ink in the probe boxes must read 254 or more (200 on a tier whose `render_scale` is under 1, see the next bullet) and every pixel of a strip of the panel fill must be the authored colour within 2, which also catches a bloom halo; `--expect fail` on a build from before the fix shows it catches the defect). The check hides every DOM element but the canvas (a new optional `css` field in the bench runner's jobs, injected before the page loads), so the debug overlay cannot cover a probe on the phone view.
- **2026-10-17 - Lite and Low: exact colour, softer strokes (render scale, not post).** On Lite (`render_scale` 0.65) and Low (0.75) the panel fill is exact but the thin white strokes peak about 220 to 237 instead of 255 (under the tone map they read 145 to 182). The cause is `render_size` (crates/evani-render/src/control.rs): render scale shrinks the canvas backing store itself and the browser stretches it to the page, which softens every sprite's thin edges, HUD or not. So `hud_white_stays_white` asks 200 on those tiers and 254 elsewhere. Making the HUD as sharp as Ultra there would mean drawing the scene into an internal scaled target with the canvas at full size; that changes every game's frame path, so it is filed for Chris (CORE_REQUESTS.md) rather than built here.

## Drop-to-convert (S3-14, 2026-10-20)

- **The upload's name is never a path: the trust boundary.** A dropped file's name is only a label. The page checks it first (`convert::accept_name`: no `/`, `\`, `:`, control character or leading dot, at most 255 bytes, `.glb`/`.blend`/`.zip` only), then its size (256 MiB, the bridge's `MAX_UPLOAD`) and its first 16 bytes (`glTF`, a zip, or a `.blend` plain, zstd or gzip), so a wrong file is a sentence before anything is sent. The bridge repeats all three and is the one that counts: the token is checked first (cookie `evani_live_<port>`, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path `/live/v1/convert`, or `Authorization: Bearer`; a cross-origin call from any other origin is refused), the declared `Content-Length` and the read body are capped, and the first bytes must pass `Kind::sniff`. The job's folder is named by the content hash under `.evani/convert/`, never by the uploaded name; the id is only `[a-z0-9_-]`; a served file's name must be a plain relative one. Nothing outside `.evani/convert/` is written, and a conversion cannot touch the game's or the engine's sources.
- **One at a time, below-normal priority.** A second upload answers `409 busy` with the running job's id; the converter runs at below-normal priority, and its notes reach `/ctl` and `/evani-dev` as `convert_progress` and `convert_done`.
- **The page's button is the way that always works.** S3-0 found a file dropped in the Evani.dev tab did nothing. The page installs a window-level `dragover`/`drop` handler (dev builds of model games only, `models` feature) and still carries the "Convert a model" button, so a drop the host swallows is not a dead end.
- **The split view is one frame drawn twice.** `model.load` uses focus `live.model` with the game paused and the frame counter still running; the slider sets a scissor at its position and draws the original left and the converted tier right, so both halves share camera, lighting and tone map and cannot drift apart. A load that fails leaves the game as it was.
- **2026-10-20 - Bridge dependencies.** `sha2 =0.10.9` (default features off): the content hash that names a job's folder, the same pin evani-convert and xtask already use. `flate2` (the workspace pin `=1.1.10`): inflating deflated zip entries and their CRC-32 check, already used by xtask and evani-convert. No new crate enters the lock file.
- **2026-10-20 - evani-render public API, `hooks` only.** `pub mod model::split` with `Split {at, left, right}` (re-exported as `model::Split`), the field `Models.split: Option<Split>` (the before/after view: the renderer scissors `left` to the part of the frame left of `at` and `right` to the rest), `Models::sphere(id)` (a loaded model's bounding sphere, to frame the turntable) and `Models::arrived(id, glb)` (bytes fetched by the page for a model the renderer did not fetch itself). All behind evani-render's dev-only `hooks` feature, so no ship build has them.
- **2026-10-20 - Page error codes are a fixed list.** `LiveError::new` takes a `&'static str` code; codes from the bridge's JSON are mapped through `convert::page_code` onto `PAGE_CODES` (the bridge's refusal codes), and anything else becomes `internal`, so a bridge answer can never put an arbitrary string into the page's error codes.
- **2026-10-20 - The contract for `model.compare`.** `{at?: 0..1, split?: bool, tier?}`: `at` 0 shows all converted, 1 all original; `split` false shows the converted tier whole. The MCP tool, LIVE_API 16.5 and the page agree; the page's slider is labelled "converted" at its left end and "original" at its right. `model.convert` is a change: in the mutating list, its MCP tool needs a `note`, kept in the job's `job.json` and first `convert_progress`.
- **2026-10-20 - Hardening after review.** The run limit (30 minutes) ends the converter's whole process tree, not only cargo: the process is adopted into a kill-on-close Windows job object and tree-killed (`taskkill /T`) when the run is dropped (`convert::run_tree`; test `run_limit_ends_the_whole_process_tree`). Cookies ignore the port, so the convert routes refuse any `Sec-Fetch-Site` but absent, `same-origin` and `none`, and every answer carries `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` (test `convert_routes_refuse_other_sites`). Old conversions are removed when a job starts (newest 8, none past 7 days; the current and latest kept; test `old_conversions_are_pruned`). Blender opens a dropped `.blend` with `--disable-autoexec`. Zip entries with Windows reserved names or parts ending in a dot or space are refused.
- **2026-10-20 - Drop-to-convert is wired into the four model games only; the model viewer is left out on purpose.** Only `sandbox`, `2d-platformer`, `kart-racer` and `match-3` turn on `evani-live?/models` from their `models` feature. `apps/model-view` (the Evani.Model tab's viewer and the quality gate's renderer) is deliberately not wired: it has its own load and tier commands (`view.load`, `view.tier`, with `view.camera` and `view.state`); the split view clears the kit's model draws every frame while it is shown (`models.draws.clear()` in evani-live's `convert::frame`), so it would wipe the viewer's own model draws; and it would add a second drop bar and a second report panel next to the viewer's own. Written in docs/EVANI_MODEL_TAB.md too.

## Blender add-on (S3-15, 2026-10-17)

- **A separate GPL folder, outside every build.** `tools/blender-addon/evani_export/` is GPL-3.0-or-later with its own LICENSE and an SPDX line in each .py; it has no Cargo manifest, so no crate links it, and `cargo xtask check` (area `licence`) errors if any file under crates, apps, kits or looks names `tools/blender-addon`. The engine licence and THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES are unchanged.
- **The button runs the converter, as a child process.** Blender exports a temporary .glb (extras on), then `cargo xtask convert <file> --id <id>` runs at below-normal priority from the chosen Evani.dev folder (files go to assets/models/<id>). The result line comes from the converter, so the pass or problem count is the same one `cargo xtask convert` prints. No Rust code, no new dependency.
- **Bridge option, live since S3-14.** "Send to the running game" posts the .glb to the S3-14 endpoint (`/live/v1/convert`, port 8096 by default), sending the session's dev token as `Authorization: Bearer`; it no longer prints "needs S3-14", and without a running bridge it says the bridge could not be reached and sends nothing.
- **Built with Blender itself.** `cargo xtask blender-addon` runs `blender --command extension build` into `target/blender-addon/` with a scratch BLENDER_USER_RESOURCES so a build or test never touches the person's own Blender; `--test` installs that zip in a headless Blender, makes a sphere in the script, presses the button and checks the tier files and report appear. No Blender: it prints SKIPPED and passes; `EVANI_REQUIRE_TOOLS=1` fails instead.
- **Not tried: a screenshot of the panel.** Blender draws no interface in `--background`, and a visible Blender window was out of bounds on this shared PC.

## Surface relief (S3-10, 2026-10-17)

- **Per material, behind two features.** A material's `extras.evani.relief` (height map with depth, plus a micro-detail recipe: pattern, size, depth, angle, mask; docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md "Surface relief") is read only with evani-render `relief` (implies `models`), which adds a relief variant of the model fragment stage (`model_relief.wgsl`), and the look's `relief` (evani-look-toon), which fills the new `micro_detail` slot. Games turn both on only when their models carry relief; the sandbox does it in its `relief` proof feature (fixture `tests/fixtures/models/relief-tile.glb`, made by `relief-tile.mjs`), so its default build and every other game stay byte-identical (SHA-1 table in the merge commit).
- **Cheap parallax on every tier: a deliberate change to the plan line.** The plan said parallax on High and Ultra only. Same-camera captures at a grazing view showed Lite, Low and Medium reading clearly flatter without it, against Chris's same-look rule, so those tiers (and VR) take a 2-step march (3 height samples, the last two blended); High takes 8 and Ultra 16. The test `parallax_off_on_low_and_medium` is renamed `parallax_cheap_on_low_and_medium`.
- **Slope ink.** The micro pattern's bent normal alone vanished inside the toon ramp's flat bands, so the relief shader also darkens the base colour by the pattern's slope (`1 - depth x min(|slope|^2, 1)`) before the shared lighting tail; knurl and ribs now read the same on every tier. Faded with the pattern below about 1.5 px (screen-space derivatives), so it never shimmers.
- **Patterns written from scratch.** Four arithmetic patterns (knurl, ribs, dots, grain), no texture reads, no new dependencies, no ported code (Chris's own-tools rule). The model check validates the recipe fields.
- **Size.** Sandbox toon build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=a9d7522`), measured on the branch after merging m1-foundation 33e97ea (S3-11): engine-only relief (features on, no proof model) 290,570 → 292,965 B gz wasm, **+2,395 B** (budget 3 KB; +2,711 B on 38257fb, +2,616 B on a9d7522); js 16,757 → 16,758 B gz. The proof build with the embedded fixture (12 KB: plate with knurl, wall in three columns with ribs, dots and grain, so all four patterns show; head-on wall view from 16 s) is 298,079 B.
- **Stair-steps on bevels (review, two causes).** 4x close-ups against the same scene with the fixture's relief stripped (.vibedeck/screenshots/s3-10-relief/r2-zoom4x-bevel-*): on the wall (no parallax) the stair-steps along the bevel band edges are the same with and without relief, so they come from the toon ramp's hard bands over a normal-mapped bevel (S3-3), not from this work. On the floor, parallax adds one more stepped edge where a panel top hides the bevel behind it: an occlusion edge made in the fragment shader, which 4x MSAA (geometry edges only) cannot smooth; the same on every tier and one pixel wide at 1:1. Not fixed: smoothing it needs per-sample shading on relief surfaces (about 4x their fragment cost, against the same-look rule on phones) or the Stage 8 temporal AA.
- **After merging m1-foundation 38257fb (sun-lod-fix) and 33e97ea (S3-11).** Five-tier sheets retaken on each merged head (.vibedeck/screenshots/s3-10-relief/r3-postmerge/ at 6, 13 and 21 s, r4-final/ at 13 and 21 s, overlay hidden): knurl, ribs, dots and grain read on every tier. Lite and Low show a purple patch over the lower wall and the back of the plate, Medium and up none; the same scene with every material's relief stripped (r3-norelief/) shows the same patch, so it is the baked sun cone that tiers without a shadow map use (S3-3), unchanged by sun-lod-fix, not relief. Fixed on `sun-smear-fix` (2026-10-20; next entry).
- **The purple patch on Lite and Low: cause, fix, tests, size (2026-10-20, branch `sun-smear-fix`).** Cause: the baked sun cone (`evani-render` model/sun.rs, S3-3) is stored per vertex and the GPU blends a triangle's three corner cones across it. A vertex where another surface meets the face (the wall's foot on the floor, the floor's back edge at the wall) starts its rays inside that surface's grid cells and sees almost no sky, so on a 2 m face that one dark corner shaded metres of it along the triangle's diagonal, where the shadow map (Medium and up) lights the whole face. Not relief and not sun-lod-fix. Fix (sun.rs, module notes "Big triangles"): a corner of a triangle with an edge over `COARSE` (8) cells takes its cone from votes cast inside its big triangles (from the middle of the corner's share, weights 1/2, 1/4, 1/4, weighted by area, only faces within 60 degrees of the corner's normal, a direction no face voted on keeps the corner's own ray); a model of small triangles has no votes and bakes the same bytes as before. The second part (found by the S3-6 quality gate, kart_fixture on Lite and Low) lays the bake's grid on the drawn level's box (shared parts plus the lowest level, grown by whole cells to hold the others), so a converted file whose hidden level is 1 cm wider bakes like its master (0.0% of the sunlit area differs under all four test suns, 2.1 to 2.3% before); a model without levels bakes the same bytes. Tests, all in `crates/evani-render/tests/model_wgsl.rs` (the whole file runs in `cargo xtask check --full`): `big_faces_get_no_diagonal_sun_smear` (the relief tile drawn as the GPU draws it against exact casts; before the fix 40.3% of the tile the shadow map lights whole was drawn dark and 60.6% of its big faces were smeared under the sandbox sun, now none), `baked_sun_draws_no_smear_on_any_model` (every shipped and fixture model: smeared share at most 1% of the sunlit area, lit share within 6 points of the shadow map's) and `bake_is_stable_under_position_quantisation` (a hidden level a fifth of a cell wider changed 580 of 2,356 cones of the drawn level before, 0 now; positions one 16-bit step off change under 1% of the sunlit vertices). Run on the old sun.rs (6231a1b) the first fails at its first assertion, the second at the relief tile and the third at the moved cones; on the new one all eight tests of the file pass. Size: sandbox ship build (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9, `cargo xtask size-report sandbox`, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=smear00`) wasm **290,165 -> 292,314 B gz, +2,149 B**; the relief proof build 297,946 -> 300,256 B gz, +2,310 B; js unchanged; kart-racer (no `models`) byte for byte the same size. The commit message's "about 1.9 KB smaller" is against the branch's own earlier two-sort version, not against m1-foundation: against m1-foundation the fix costs about 2.1 KB gz in every game that turns on `models`; Chris, 2026-10-20: trimming it is a priority next step (lightness first; the open "Size trim" task in STAGES.md Stage 3). Captures (headless Chrome, audio muted, locks stubbed, the sandbox `relief` proof, 6, 13 and 21 s, overlay shown): `.evani-shot-smear/` (not tracked) holds `old/` (m1-foundation's bake) and `new/` for Lite, Low, Medium and Ultra. Lite and Low: the purple patch over the wall and plate is gone at all three views (pixels more than 30 levels darker in the old picture: 2.8 to 3.7% of the frame on Lite, 2.8 to 3.5% on Low at 6 and 13 s, 6.7% and 4.3% on the head-on view at 21 s). Medium and Ultra (shadow map): the same pictures, mean brightness within 0.05 levels and only animation noise left (0.5 to 3.6% of pixels differ by more than 2 levels, under 0.7% by more than 30). The r3 sheets' pictures are not kept in the repository (only their `captures.json`), so the old-bake build of the same proof scene stands in for them. Not re-run here: the S3-6 quality gate on kart_fixture Lite and Low (about 10 minutes, `cargo xtask convert --gate`); the commit's 0.0% master-against-converted figure is the evidence so far, so those two rows stay as recorded above until the gate is run on the merged head.
- **The smear test scores each LOD level as drawn (2026-10-20, s3-integration).** `baked_sun_draws_no_smear_on_any_model` cast its exact shadows against every LOD level of a model at once, so on S3-12's two-level `look-match.low.glb` the hidden fine level shaded the drawn coarse one and the test read 7 points of shadow the GPU never draws (no pass draws a hidden level). The test now reports each level with the shared parts, as the game draws it; the 6-point limit stays and every level must pass alone. The bake was already right; no engine change, 0 B.
- **Not done here.** The metal-rough and occlusion maps are still read but unused (CORE_REQUESTS `smooth-lighting`, Stage 5, Chris to decide); `extras.evani.normal_maps` is still not read; Evani.Model has no preview renders yet, so captures compare the tiers against Ultra.
- **Look guard (run 2026-10-17, live sandbox, default build).** `evani-look-basic`: guard `failed: record_missing` in that headless session only, because it was not started by Evani.App and could not see the approval store; in Evani.App's own live session the guard is `verified` (Chris's approval seq 1, limits 69044ce5…), checked 2026-10-17 after Chris answered "approved". `look.check` flags four small drifts (log-average brightness 0.311, bloom 18.6%, colourfulness 55.4 just under their bands); the unchanged base build (883d78a) gives the same four values and the same captures hash (963cf5ff…), so they predate this work and are Chris's to approve or re-baseline. `evani-look-toon`: unguarded (`no_limits`). No toon baseline proposed: live builds use default features, so relief can't be shown to the guard yet.

## The quality gate (S3-6, 2026-10-17)

- **What it is.** `cargo xtask convert <model> --gate` renders the master and each tier's file in the engine's own renderer (`apps/model-view`, feature `gate`: `models` and `capture`, a held clock, the game's look; `anim` joins once skinned models draw, S3-9) through `tools/convert-runner/run.mjs` (Node built-ins, CDP, one headless muted Chrome at below-normal priority, ports 8230 to 8239, Chrome finder and pointer stubs shared with the bench runner in `tools/bench-runner/chrome.mjs`). Eight fixed views per tier at the tier's reference resolution and render scale (VR with its mono preset, as the bench does): four around at 20 degrees up, two high three-quarter, one low front, one close-up on the densest detail. Each view fits the model's box (bisection, 6% margin) rather than its bounding sphere, so the model fills the picture.
- **SSIMULACRA2: the `ssimulacra2 =0.5.1` crate (BSD-2-Clause), converter only, no default features.** Decision 3 (Chris, 2026-10-15). Its tree is small and permissive: yuvxyb 0.4.1, yuvxyb-math 0.1.1, v_frame 0.3.9, av-data 0.4.4, num-traits, thiserror, log (MIT, BSD or Apache). No game links the converter. No code was copied, so THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES is unchanged.
- **Worst pixel: our own metric, after FLIP's idea.** Both pictures lightly blurred (3x3) in linear light, then CIELAB: a colour term `min(1, dE76 / 50)` raised to `1 - e_f`, where `e_f` is the square root of the Sobel edge difference on L*, so a lost or added edge pushes the colour difference toward 1; the view's score is the 99.9th percentile, so one bad patch fails a view whose average is fine. Written from scratch (`tools/evani-convert/src/metric.rs`); FLIP is an idea only.
- **Back-off over the whole ladder.** Each tier's ladder runs from the best setting (ratio 1, 16-bit positions, normals and UVs) down past the tier's own setting: a greedy binary search per knob in turn, each setting rendered at most once (simplify ratio, texture size for textured models, then position, normal and UV bits), on the worst of the eight views. The smallest setting that passes wins, and the tier's file is converted with it and verified once more. A tier whose own starting setting fails backs off instead of failing. Evani.Model inputs keep their geometry and textures (they are never re-simplified), so only the bits are searched. The clip error (S3-7's `clip_error_m`) joins once model-view can play clips on skinned models (S3-9).
- **Found by the gate: meshes without normals shaded differently after conversion.** gltfpack's vertex merge changed the normals the engine makes for a mesh that has none, so kart_pip's Ultra file scored SSIMULACRA2 -51 at full quality. The converter now gives such a mesh the engine's own smooth normals before packing (`normals.rs`); the master plus those normals scores 100 against the master.
- **Found by the gate: a skinned model renders blank, and blank against blank scores 100.** The Evani.Model character is skinned all through, and the engine's model path skips skinned nodes until GPU skinning lands (S3-9; S3-8 is the CPU side), so every tier "passed" at 100 on empty pictures. The gate now refuses a reference render whose best view the model covers under 0.5% (pixels more than 12 levels off their row's backdrop), and skips a model whose mesh nodes are all skinned with the report note "not gated: skinned model" (a partly skinned one is gated with a note that those parts are not seen). The character is therefore not gated until S3-9, and its blank calibration pairs were removed.
- **Found by the gate: its trials were not the shipped files.** kart.low.glb as a general model (kart_fixture) scored 93 to 95 in its trials but 84 to 86 as tier files: a trial was one gltfpack level, while the tier file is the merged LOD chain the engine picks from by screen size. The tier files and the gate's candidates now come from one builder (`Geometry` in lib.rs: the LOD chain, merge, quantize, `extras.evani.lods`), and `verify` checks the finished file twice: its geometry must be the chosen candidate's (`geometry_same`, every tier, textured or not: each drawn primitive's counts and attribute formats plus the level list), and an untextured tier must not score more than 3 under its trial (`drift`; a textured tier carries other texture sets than its trial, so only its geometry is compared). Test: `gate_judges_the_file_that_ships`.
- **Found by the gate: Lite and Low still fail, on the baked sun, after its LOD fix.** First finding: the baked self-shadow cone (`evani-render` model/sun.rs, S3-3), which only the tiers without a shadow map read, baked over every LOD level's parts, so hidden coarser levels shaded the drawn one (kart_fixture Low: -3.1 at the best rung, the file -56.0). That was fixed on m1-foundation (38257fb: a level is shaded by the shared parts plus its own level). Re-run on the merged branch (2026-10-17): Low best rung -2.5 (worst pixel 0.81), file -56.0 (0.85); Lite best rung 3.8 (0.81), file -62.2 (0.89); both fail, and the floor is not used (under 78). The best-rung pictures have the same outline as the master (same geometry, 16-bit vertices); what moves is where the baked shadow patches fall (one patch on the side pod shows on the master and not on the converted file). So the remaining gap is the bake's sensitivity to how a file is packed (its 64-cell grid spans every level's box, and positions are 16-bit), not lost detail. The bar stays where it is; Medium and up (shadow maps) are not affected. A fix belongs in `evani-render` (for example a grid over the drawn level's box, or cells snapped to a fixed model-space lattice), not in the converter.
- **An Evani.Model file renamed without its tier** (extras.evani.tier set, but no `<name>.<tier>.glb` beside it) now says which file names the converter expects, instead of a missing-file error on the master copy.
- **The format floor.** A conversion nobody can see still moves pixels: on kart_pip at High (1920x1080) gltfpack without quantization scores 100, but 16-bit positions score 81.2 on the worst view (worst pixel 0.036; toon outlines shift by a fraction of a pixel) and float positions (`-vpf`) 84.3. Earlier probes on the same views: a 1 px shift scores 28.6, noise of plus or minus 3 levels 86.0, the picture darker by 4 levels 90.6. So a fixed 90 cannot be reached by any packed file. Each tier therefore renders its best rung first; its worst view is the tier's floor, and the tier's bar is `min(90, floor - 5)` when the floor is at least 78 and its worst pixel within the ceiling, else 90. 90 stays the cap and the starting bar of decision 4; the floor only lowers it to what the format itself costs. The report shows floor, bar and whether the floor was used, per tier.
- **Thresholds: SSIMULACRA2 90 (capped bar), worst pixel 0.35, floor slack 5, floor minimum 78. Provisional until Chris's blind test.**
- **The blind test.** `--gate-pairs <folder>` writes about five pairs per model spanning its scores plus one identical control into `<folder>/key.json`. `cargo xtask convert --calibration ../Evani.Dev/.evani/gate-calibration` serves the page on the first free port of 8230 to 8239: pairs in a shuffled order, random tokens per pair (the page never sees which side is the original or which pair is the control), "same" or "different" per pair, answers saved to `answers.json` beside the key. `cargo xtask convert --calibration-result <folder>` suggests the bar, the worst-pixel ceiling and the slack from the answers (just past every pair judged different) and warns when a control was judged different.
- **kart_pip per tier** (the shared-geometry pipeline). Floors 81.8 (Low, 1440x810), 81.3 (Medium), 81.2 (High), 80.8 (Ultra, 2560x1440), so bars 76.8, 76.3, 76.2, 75.8. Every tier chose ratio 1 (no simplification) with 16-bit positions and 10-bit UVs, 16-bit normals on Low and 8-bit on the rest; the tier files score 81.8 / 79.7 / 80.5 / 79.9 on the worst view (the close-up), worst pixel 0.035 to 0.071, drift 0 and the same geometry as their trials on every tier. The model has only 1,408 triangles, so any simplification shows: on Medium ratio 0.75 scored 13.1 and 0.5 -51.9; 14-bit positions scored 68.1 and 12-bit 35.5 (Low also refused 8-bit normals, 59.8). The package grows from S3-5's 29 KB to 125 KB (55 KB brotli) because the gate no longer lets the lower tiers simplify; the visible cost of the old Low (354 triangles) is what the gate exists to catch.
- **kart_fixture per tier** (kart.low.glb as a general model, 36 renders in about 10 minutes). Medium 84.9 (bar 81.2), High 84.5 (79.2), Ultra 83.0 (80.1), each at ratio 1 with 16-bit positions, 8-bit normals and 10-bit UVs, drift 0, same geometry; Low and Lite fail (the baked sun above).
- **Tests.** `identical_input_scores_perfect`, `damaged_model_fails` (10% of triangles kept), `search_is_monotonic_and_repeatable`, `views_are_deterministic` (two runs, same pixels), `format_floor_sets_the_bar`, `blank_render_and_skinned_master_are_caught`, `geometry_signature_sees_geometry_not_textures`, `gate_judges_the_file_that_ships` (fails when the candidates are built as one level again); with `EVANI_REQUIRE_TOOLS=1` they render for real (about five minutes). Nothing ships: no game turns on `gate`, and the four non-user games' ship builds are byte-identical (SHA-1 table in the merge commit).

## Texture supercompression: decided, keep postponed (2026-10-20)

- **Status: Decided: keep postponed (Chris, 2026-10-20).** The reopening condition below is unchanged. Was: proposed, awaiting Chris. Proposal: defer KTX2 supercompression; engine KTX2 sets stay plain, as S3-3's reader and S3-5's converter already assume (DECISIONS "Texture sets: ASTC and BC7, then the file's own image" and "Texture sets"; docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md line 96). Chris made this call on 2026-10-20 (keep postponed), closing the Next step "Decide: allow texture supercompression". An earlier version of this entry said Chris's Stage 3 call settled it ("the server compresses the bytes on the wire"); no such call exists, and that claim is withdrawn.
- **Measured: two different things, not to be mixed.** Files are the shipped sandbox sets in `apps/sandbox/assets/models/` (plain, `supercompressionScheme` 0, sizes matching the plain encodes in `.evani-tmp-supercomp/m1/plain-*.ktx2`). (a) Wire-compression bound: whole file through brotli -q 11 (brotli 1.2.0, generic stream). It is what a host that brotli-compresses `.ktx2` would already save, not KTX2 supercompression. (b) Measured: KTX2 Zstandard supercompression, real encodes with `ktx create --zstd` (KTX-Software 4.4.2), file sizes from `.evani-tmp-supercomp/m1/` (`z19-*` level 19, `z3-*` level 3), header scheme 2 confirmed.

  | File (plain bytes) | (a) brotli | (b) Zstd 19 | (b) Zstd 3 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | base ASTC 1,398,608 | 1,221,370 (-12.7%) | 1,261,993 (-9.8%) | 1,315,117 (-6.0%) |
  | base BC7 1,398,576 | 1,062,211 (-24.1%) | 1,133,835 (-18.9%) | 1,211,605 (-13.4%) |
  | normal ASTC 350,016 | 319,658 (-8.7%) | 325,605 (-7.0%) | 333,988 (-4.6%) |
  | normal BC7 349,968 | 298,402 (-14.7%) | 309,334 (-11.6%) | 315,670 (-9.8%) |

  Careful with the other files in that folder: the top-level `base-*.ktx2` / `normal-*.ktx2` there are already Zstd (scheme 2), so comparing a `z19` file with them shows 0 to -6.4% (BC7 base 1,211,637 to 1,133,899; ASTC base 1,262,057 to 1,262,089, no gain); that is Zstd against Zstd and says nothing about plain against supercompressed. Against the plain files the gain is 5 to 19%.
- **Unverified: whether the host compresses `.ktx2` on the wire.** Not checked. Cloudflare does not compress image types by default, and `.ktx2` may not be on its list. If the host does brotli it, supercompression adds close to nothing on the wire (the two overlap); if it does not, Zstd saves the figures in (b). One check settles it: serve a plain `.ktx2` from the real host (or `cargo xtask build --ship` then publish dry-run `--serve` for the local server) and read `content-encoding` and transferred bytes with `cargo xtask netlog <id> --tier low,ultra` or a `curl -H "accept-encoding: br, gzip" -I`.
- **What the package costs now (recorded, not measured here).** docs/STAGE3_PLAN.md line 157, S3-5's real runs (2026-10-17, merge 6a15487): kart_pip 29 KB package; the Evani.Model character 8.3 MB (2.5 MB brotli), within the 10 MB playground cap; the Frontier Marshal 165 MB, **over** the cap, because plain KTX2 at 2048 and 4096 costs about 17 to 34 MB per tier. Texture count and resolution break the cap, not the missing supercompression: 5 to 19% would still leave that asset far over 10 MB.
- **Why defer (proposed).** (1) Zstd gains 5 to 19% on the 1024 sets, and against a host that already brotlis them, about nothing; the real lever is texture resolution and count (the converter's report lists bytes per file per tier). (2) Doing it touches three places at once (converter writes, `evani-gltf`'s KTX2 reader, per-level decompression), so it is not a size-only edit. (3) The reader grows by a Zstd decompressor (wasm size against the accepted budgets), ship builds and download sizes change for every game with models so SHA-1 evidence must be retaken, existing sets must be re-converted, and a reader that starts requiring a scheme would refuse older plain files unless it reads both.
- **Reopens when** a game with models ships a package over the 10 MB playground cap (the converted package, not the master) **and** its report shows the KTX2 sets are the part to cut after resolution and texture-count reduction, **or** Chris asks for it directly.
- **Next step if approved.** First run the wire check above. If the host does not compress `.ktx2`: add Zstandard level reading to `evani-gltf`'s KTX2 reader behind `models`, write one sandbox set with `ktx create ... --zstd 19` (KTX-Software 4.4.2, pinned by `doctor --fix`), then measure bytes plain, brotli and Zstd per set, the sandbox ship wasm gzip delta, and download bytes per tier with `cargo xtask netlog <id> --tier low,ultra`. Accept only if the wasm delta and the download gain both pass Chris's budget check.

## Netcode core: inputs, snapshots, prediction, interpolation, lag compensation, loopback (S5-N1, 2026-10-20)

- **Scope.** `evani-net` grows from the M1 stub into a deterministic netcode core for the tactical shooter (K6b, Hexagon): a `NetGame` trait (fixed-step `step`, `Input`, `State`, `Pose`, `PROTOCOL`), input and snapshot packets, a `Server`, a predicting `Client`, a `Transport` trait with an in-page `Loopback`, and `LocalSession` (server, local client and loopback in one page). Still only built for games that list `net = true`; no new dependencies; nothing outside `crates/evani-net` changes. No tests yet: Chris deferred testing for Stages 4 to 6 to one combined pass (2026-10-20).
- **Server-authoritative inputs, full snapshots.** Clients send inputs, never state. The server queues each peer's inputs by tick and steps once per tick with everyone's; a late or lost input repeats the peer's last one (so steps treat inputs as held state and find presses by comparing with the previous input). Every `snapshot_every` ticks (60 Hz / `net.send_rate_hz`, 3 by default) each client gets the whole state, floats as exact bits, plus every peer's last input and a pacing report. Delta snapshots are left for later; the input packet already carries the snapshot ack they need.
- **Prediction and reconcile.** The client runs ahead by about the one-way delay plus one tick, steps its own copy with its input and the others' last known inputs (`InputSource::Guessed`), and keeps a checksum per predicted tick. A snapshot whose state has the stored checksum is confirmed with no work; a mismatch takes the snapshot and replays the stored inputs with `Role::Resim` (effects that must happen once check `Role::first_time`). The server's slack report (how early inputs arrive) moves the client one tick at a time: two steps in a frame when inputs are late, none when they are too early, then a wait until the server has seen the change.
- **Interpolation and lag compensation share one history.** The server records every entity's pose at each snapshot tick; clients push the same frames from snapshots. Remote entities are drawn `net.interp_delay_ms` behind the newest snapshot, and each input carries the view time of the frame the player saw. `StepCtx::rewind` samples the server's history at that time (clamped to `net.lag_comp_max_ms`, 200 ms by default, 0 turns it off), with integer sub-tick times so both sides blend the same frames with the same weights: a shot is tested against what the shooter saw.
- **Bots and offline play go through the same path.** Bots are server-side peers whose inputs enter the same per-peer input list (seeded `Pcg32` per bot from the session seed); the offline human is a normal client over the loopback. The loopback can add latency, jitter and loss (`net.loopback_latency_ms`, `net.loopback_jitter_ms`, `net.loopback_loss_pct`, all 0 by default), driven by its own seeded generator, so offline runs are repeatable.
- **Determinism.** Fixed 60 Hz ticks, no clock reads, randomness only from `StepCtx::rng` (session seed, tick and stream through SplitMix64 into `Pcg32`), peers and poses in sorted order, sorted `Vec` and `VecDeque` only (no hash maps). Every decode is bounds-checked and a bad packet is dropped and counted.
- **Overlay.** The module inserts a `NetStats` resource; `LocalSession::publish_stats` fills it and the net line reads "idle · 20 Hz" or "loopback · N peers · N bots · N rollbacks · 20 Hz".
- **Lean (Chris, through the coordinator, 2026-10-20: small wasm, low bandwidth).** `Server` and `Client` hold a `Box<dyn Transport>`, so their code is built once whatever the link; the loopback and the server's input queues are sorted `VecDeque`s, not tree maps; pacing is a small non-generic struct. Input frames store the view time as a change from the frame before (`ViewCodec`, usually one byte).
