The snapshot header has a `baseline` tick, `None` today (plain body); a tick is reserved for a later delta body (XOR against the snapshot the client acked, zero runs packed), and this version drops one that arrives. Expected payloads (transport headers extra): an input packet is about 8 bytes plus 1 to 2 bytes of view and the input per frame, so a 10-byte shooter input with 4 frames is about 50 bytes, about 3 KB/s up at 60 Hz; a snapshot is about 8 bytes plus about 11 per peer plus the state, so 10 players at about 40 bytes each with exact floats is about 540 bytes, about 11 KB/s down at 20 Hz, about half with millimetre `i32` or quantised fields, and about 60 to 150 bytes once the delta body exists. Not built, suggested: send inputs every second tick (two new frames per packet) to halve the packets up.

## Photoreal look: the grade lives inside the tonemap slot (S5-L3, 2026-10-20)

- **Decision.** `looks/evani-look-photoreal` puts its colour grade in its own `tonemap` slot body, as a marked stage (`// @photoreal-grade begin` / `end`) after the filmic stage, instead of a new engine `grade` slot. A new slot needs a `//@slot grade` marker in the renderer's tonemap shader, which Stage 3 is merging into; the look-side stage needs no engine change. If a later piece adds an engine `grade` slot, the look's grade body moves there unchanged (it already reads and writes one colour).
- **Params feed the body.** Slot bodies see only their arguments, so `look.photoreal.tonemap.*` and `look.photoreal.grade.*` are written into the body as constants; a frame system re-composes it and queues a detached tonemap rebuild when one changes, and never replaces a body it did not compose (hand edits, baked `slots/tonemap.wgsl`).
- **Hook for the `grade` LUT effect (S5-FX1).** `look.photoreal.grade.source` = `look`, `custom` or `none`; `none` leaves grading to the LUT pass. No new dependencies.

## Pointer lock and the touch look pad (S5-E1, 2026-10-20)

- **What.** `evani-core` feature `pointer-lock` (mouse-look through the browser's pointer lock, released on Esc, blur, hidden tab, unload, fatal error, panic and a stalled loop) and a `LookPad` control in the existing `touch` feature (a drag area that adds finger movement to the same axis actions `mouse:dx` / `mouse:dy` drive). No new dependency; web-sys needed no new feature (`Document`, `Element`, `Window` and the event types were already listed).
- **One action, not a second path.** Look stays an ordinary axis action bound to `mouse:dx`; the look pad adds to that action's value through `Input::add_touch_delta` (1/16 px steps, cleared at the end of the tick like a mouse delta). The lock only changes whether the mouse delta is unbounded.
- **Release inside the handler.** Every release runs in the event handler that saw the cause, because a hidden or crashed page gets no next frame; the panic hook and the fatal-message path call `exitPointerLock` themselves, and a 500 ms watchdog lets go when frames stop for 1.5 s while locked. A release the kit didn't ask for also drops its wish to be locked, so the engine never re-grabs the mouse by itself.
- **Opt-in twice.** The feature compiles the code in; `kit.pointer_lock()` installs the listeners and the timer. A game that lists neither is unchanged.

## Look matching in the converter (S3-12, 2026-10-20)

- **It lives in the converter, not in the engine or the looks.** `cargo xtask convert <model> --look toon [--snap-palette]` (`tools/evani-convert/src/look.rs`) edits the document before the clean, the tier files and the quality gate: it drops the texture maps and material factors that look never samples (toon: metallic-roughness and occlusion), merges materials that are equal in everything the look reads (fewer draw calls), and, only with `--snap-palette`, moves an untextured base colour to the nearest palette colour of the look's own crate (`evani-look-toon`, in-repo, so one source and no copy). One table, `SLOTS`/`LOOKS`, says what each look reads; a test holds it equal to what `model.wgsl`, `model_relief.wgsl` and `fn material()` really read. A look with no row keeps everything and the report says so; nothing is dropped or merged without `--look`, and an Evani.Model file (no glTF materials) is unchanged.
- **What it does to the asset.** Nodes, anchors (`anchor:*` names), extras, accessors and geometry are byte-for-byte untouched; only `materials`, `textures`, `images` and now-unused `samplers` change. The master GLB keeps the original materials (4 in the test fixture), the shipped tier file carries the matched ones (2). Relief height maps are moved to a free image slot before the clean, not dropped, so a look never removes a map that another feature (S3-10) still reads.
- **The quality gate still decides.** Matching runs before the gate and the gate scores the file that ships, so a match that visibly hurts fails; the bytes and draw calls saved are measured per tier in the report (`look_match`). The test `gate_still_runs_after_matching` asserts that the matched low tier file passes the gate (with `EVANI_REQUIRE_TOOLS=1` it renders for real: tier file SSIMULACRA2 79.1, worst pixel 0.169, against a bar of 77.7 from a format floor of 82.7 at the S3-12 branch; on m1-foundation after the bake fix below, 78.7, worst pixel 0.169, bar 77.2 from a floor of 82.2), that the master is the original and that the matched file is the one that ships.
- **Relief parking also runs without `--look`.** glTF-Transform's `prune` cannot see a height map named only by `extras.evani.relief.height.index`, so the converter used to drop relief height maps with or without a look (an S3-10 bug, found here). Parking now runs on every general conversion: the map sits in the material's free `occlusionTexture` (else `metallicRoughnessTexture`) slot through the clean and the packing, then moves back (docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md "Look matching"). Tests `relief_maps_survive_toon` and `relief_maps_survive_without_a_look`.
- **`look::park_relief` returns `Result`.** A material whose `pbrMetallicRoughness` is present but not an object is now a conversion error naming the material, not a panic (unit test `a_broken_pbr_field_is_an_error_not_a_panic`); its callers in `lib.rs` pass the error on.
- **The gate's reference gets the tier files' tangents.** The engine draws a normal map only on a primitive with TANGENT, and the clean gives the tier files MikkTSpace tangents when a normal-mapped primitive has none; the gate used to render the bare master, so a tier file's normal map counted as damage (the look fixture's best rung scored SSIMULACRA2 -71.6, with or without `--look`). `gate::reference_for` now renders, for such a master, a reference made by the clean's own steps (the engine's normals where a mesh has none, weld, glTF-Transform `tangents`) in the gate folder; the master file is unchanged and the report names the reference. Not caused by look matching, and not by the gate page's picture decoding (`new Blob([bytes])` in evani-render `decode()` was already right; a comment there now says why).
- **Two fixture changes, so the gate test is fair, not easier; the bar rule is unchanged.** (1) The fixture says `min_distance_m` 40, which sizes Low's maps to 64 px from 128; the gate's views fill the picture (the close-up nearer still), so the fine checker softened and every rung failed (best rung -26.1, so the bar stayed 90; the tier file -57.9). The gate test alone now converts with `--min-distance 1` (old: 40 m from the fixture, new: 1 m), so the maps stay at their 128 px; the other look tests keep 40 m. (2) The fixture's normal map was the base colour checker reused (normals jumping every 16 px), which no baker writes; it is now a gentle bump map (unit normals tilted at most about 23 degrees). With the checker as normal map and full-size maps the best rung scored 85.5 (bar 80.5) and the search's choice 80.7, but the finished file 77.0: the trial renders resized PNGs while the shipped file carries BC7 sets on desktop, and toon banding turns the compressed normals' small errors into band-edge lines. With the bump map that drop is 1.8 (80.9 to 79.1) and the file passes. **Independent review (2026-10-20, at the merge):** change (2) is fair: a colour checker read as normals is not a normal map any baker writes, and the harsher case it exposed is the open trial-vs-shipped finding below, which stays filed. Change (1) is fair for what this test checks, but it steps around a real gate gap rather than a fixture fault: the gate's views always fill the picture and never respect `min_distance_m`, while texture sizing does, so any honestly far-seen textured model fails every rung as a "broken conversion". Not fixed here; filed in docs/STAGE3_PLAN.md "Deferred and open Stage 3 items" for Chris to schedule.
- **Open, predates S3-12 (S3-6): textured trials don't see texture compression.** *Handled by S7-5 (2026-10-21): "The quality gate scores what each device samples (S7-5)" below.* The gate's search scores each setting with resized PNG maps and then picks the cheapest passing one, often by a fraction of a point; the finished file carries ASTC/BC7 sets, whose loss the search never saw, and `drifted` deliberately ignores textured tiers. Measured: Medium with the old fixture, trial 81.1, finished file 76.7 (bar 80.4, fail); Low as above, 80.7 to 77.0 and, after the fixture fix, 80.9 to 79.1. A fix would encode the trial's maps as the tier does (or score the trial with the device set); filed in docs/STAGE3_PLAN.md under S3-6, not built here.
- **`gate_still_runs_after_matching` failed on m1-foundation; the cause was the sun-cone bake, not the gate (fixed 2026-10-20, bar, skips and fixture untouched).** After the merge the test failed with `EVANI_REQUIRE_TOOLS=1`: best rung SSIMULACRA2 30.3, tier file -18.0, so the bar stayed 90. The gate's reference is the master re-welded (3 parts, each keeping and packing only its own vertices over its own box), the tier file 1 part; the sun-cone bake (`crates/evani-render/src/model/sun.rs`) shaded them differently. Two causes. (1) The big-triangle votes were grouped by vertex index, so a point carried by two parts heard only its own part's triangles. Fix: a vertex's copies share the first copy's votes (`firsts()`), matched within a 64th of a grid cell because each part packs positions to 16 bits over its own box, so copies sit a step apart (found with counted hash buckets, no sort or map). Best rung 30.3 to 61.6. (2) On the symmetric sphere, equal faces vote exactly half open and half blocked; the rounding of the area sums then decided, and it differs between the reference and the trial because each packs over its own box (two equator vertices flipped, a streak at the edge of the trim's shadow). Fix: votes within 0.1% of even (`TIE`) count as open, as an exact tie always did, so rounding cannot flip them. (Letting the vertex's own ray decide a tie was tried first: it passed the gate but broke `big_faces_get_no_diagonal_sun_smear`, relief tile with the sun behind, 54.1% baked lit against 65.6%, because a corner at a step edge has its own ray blocked.) Now: floor 82.2, bar 77.2 (from the floor, by the gate's own rule), tier file 78.7, worst pixel 0.169; all 9 `model_wgsl` tests pass. Guard test `bake_does_not_depend_on_how_a_model_is_split` (`relief-tile.glb` and `look-match.low.glb` split into halves: over one vertex buffer every cone byte matches the whole; halves that pack their own vertices change under 1% of the sunlit vertices' shade under any shipped sun). Byte effect: sandbox ship wasm 482,767 B raw, 217,005 B gzip, the same size as before both fixes.

## Stage 3 size trims, lightness first (2026-10-20)

Chris, 2026-10-20: the engine must stay light, so people can play bigger worlds on low bandwidth. Method: `trunk build --release` with each game's ship feature set (defaults minus `live`; models games add `models`), `wasm-opt -Oz`, gzip -9 of the `.wasm`. Baseline commit 7fc8a52; final commit is the head of `s3-size-trim`. Script and raw log: `.evani-tmp-sizetrim/measure.sh`, `sizes.log`.

- **Model loader without standard sorts, glTF `extras` moved not cloned (6ac61ac).** Sorted inserts replace `sort`/`sort_by` in the loader (pulls the generic sort machinery out of the wasm); extras are moved into the model, not cloned. Behaviour is identical (render model tests pass).
- **`--low-memory-unused` in every game's `data-wasm-opt-params` (d976694).** It lets wasm-opt treat the first 1 MB of linear memory as unused. That is safe here because the wasm puts its 1 MB stack first and the data segments start at 1,048,576 or above; a game whose layout differs must drop the flag. It applies to all six `apps/*/index.html`. Tried and not kept: `--converge` (0.1 to 0.2 KB, slower builds), `-O3 -Oz` (worse), `--zero-filled-memory` and the SIMD flag (0 B).

| Build | gzip before (7fc8a52) | gzip after | Saved |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2d-platformer | 289,725 | 288,337 | 1,388 |
| match-3 | 304,643 | 302,991 | 1,652 |
| kart-racer (no models) | 369,306 | 366,909 | 2,397 |
| kart-racer + models | 424,421 | 417,541 | 6,880 |
| sandbox, no models | 232,633 | 231,569 | 1,064 |
| sandbox ship (models) | 293,371 | 287,222 | 6,149 |
| sandbox skin-proof | 313,156 | 306,400 | 6,756 |
| model-view | 254,162 | 252,776 | 1,386 |

Non-model games are all smaller (their hashes change because of the flag; no code in them changed). Of the model savings, about 4.6 KB (sandbox) and 4.0 KB (kart) come from the loader sorts and extras; the rest from the flag.

Findings (no change made):
- Streaming/handles/LOD (S3-4), the animation runtime (S3-8), GPU skinning (S3-9), the smear fix and the Lite/Low sun-cone bake were checked symbol by symbol. The 29 KB growth in `Gpu::frame` is `ModelGpu::prepare`, `upload`, `upload_texture` inlined: real code, no duplicate. About 20 KB raw is generic overhead spread over 230 small items, no single duplicate. IK as its own opt-in saves 0 B. The skin build's extra 2.2 KB is the sandbox's own `proof_skin` string search (app proof code, not the engine).
- WGSL is already comment-stripped and shipped once; stripping indentation would save about 120 B gzip (not done: not worth touching the shader tests).

Proposals for Chris (need approval, not done):
1. **Simpler sun-cone bake** (`sun::Bake::new`, 7.5 KB raw, dense numeric code): going lower needs a coarser bake, which can change shadow looks. Expected: about 1 to 2 KB gzip.
2. **Builder paths in the download.** Shipped games contain 20 full builder home paths (`C:\Users\<name>\.cargo\registry\...`): a privacy leak, 86 B gzip. `trim-paths` is unstable on this Cargo, so the fix is `--remap-path-prefix` passed by `cargo xtask build --ship`. Done: see "Ship builds strip the builder's paths" below.
3. **Nightly `build-std` with `panic_immediate_abort` / `-Zlocation-detail=none`** drops panic strings and locations (locations alone about 6.5 KB raw). Needs a nightly toolchain. Partly measured: see "Panic locations: measured, not adopted" below.

## Text split: pixel text and sharp text are separate features (2026-10-20)

Chris, 2026-10-20 (lightness first): the split that the MSDF text entry (S3-13a) left for later is built. Branch `s3-text-split`.

- **Two independent `evani-render` features.** `text` is pixel text: the 5 x 9 bitmap font, its sheet baking and `text::measure` (`bitmap.rs`; it still turns on `sprite-art`). `msdf` is sharp text, the `ui` module: it no longer implies `text` and no longer implies `sprite-art`. `text.rs` keeps only the shared types (`Align`, `FontId`, `TextStyle`) and the dispatch (`draw`, `measure_in`) and exists when either feature is on. Either alone builds and passes its tests; both together behave exactly as before.
- **Mapping is unchanged for games.** `ui = true` / `[ui] fonts` still turn on the app's `ui` feature, which is `evani-render/msdf` (plus `evani-ui`). A game that also wants pixel text turns on `evani-render/text` itself, as kits already did. `cargo xtask check` is unchanged in what it enforces: a font file or `[ui] fonts` entry without `ui = true` is an error, `.glb` without `models`, a skin without `anim`; `ui` is the module that reads the fonts, and it is the sharp-text feature.
- **Public API.** No signature changed. `text::draw` and `text::measure_in` exist with either feature; `text::measure`, `font_sheet`, `GLYPHS`, `FONT_ID`, `GLYPH_COUNT` need `text` (re-exported from `bitmap.rs`, so the paths are the same). `msdf::pack_sprites` keeps its signature (`art_base` is ignored without `sprite-art`).
- **One behaviour difference, only for a `ui` game that never turns on `text`.** `FontId::BITMAP` (the default style font, and what `Fonts::request` answers past the font limit) now draws nothing and measures `[0, 0]`, because the bitmap font is not in the download. Name a font from `rw.fonts.request(..)`. No game in the repo does otherwise: the sandbox's `ui` build requests its font first.
- **Size (Trunk release, wasm-opt -Oz, gzip -9 of every dist file, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT` pinned to 7fc8a52, ship features without `live`), games built with `ui` on, before to after:** 2d-platformer 321,432 to 311,547 B (-9,885); kart-racer 403,187 to 394,400 (-8,787); sandbox (with models) 331,434 to 321,112 (-10,322); model-view 286,690 to 276,292 (-10,398); match-3 333,140 to 330,910 (-2,230: its look and kit already turn on `sprite-art`, so only the bitmap font goes). Games without `ui` are byte-identical (the five wasm files have the same SHA-1 before and after).
- **Why it matters for the byte-identity of other games.** Panic locations in the wasm carry line numbers, so the module lines in `lib.rs` keep their count (two lines), and the edits in shared files (`gpu/frame.rs`, `shaders.rs`) change lines in place.

## Ship builds strip the builder's paths (2026-10-20)

Chris's standing approval, 2026-10-20 (privacy fixes need no separate sign-off). `cargo xtask build <game> --ship` adds `--remap-path-prefix` for the builder's home folder (`/home`), Cargo's home (`/cargo`), rustup's home (`/rustup`) and the workspace (`/evani`), passed through `CARGO_TARGET_WASM32_UNKNOWN_UNKNOWN_RUSTFLAGS` and merged with anything already in it (plain `RUSTFLAGS` would replace `.cargo/config.toml`'s wasm flags). Panic locations then read `/cargo/registry/src/...`, never `C:\Users\<name>\...`. A folder with a space in its path cannot be passed that way; the build says so. Non-ship builds are unchanged (dev and live builds keep real paths for debugging). Commit 1433922; unit test `ship_rustflags_remap_home_folders_wider_first` in xtask.

Proof (the shipped wasm, searched for the user's home folder and registry paths): 2d-platformer 0 home paths (655,697 B raw, 283,314 B gzip -9, SHA-1 df449209), sandbox 0 home paths (482,767 B raw, 217,005 B gzip, SHA-1 f779e0f8); before the fix each game carried 20. Bytes: the paths get shorter, about 86 B gzip less per game (the size-trim measurement of the paths' own bytes).

## Panic locations: measured, not adopted (2026-10-20)

Measured only, as asked; nothing changed in the build. A full nightly `-Zbuild-std` with `panic_immediate_abort` needs the nightly wasm32 target and `rust-src`, which are not installed here (installing toolchain parts was out of scope), so that figure is not measured. What was measured: the stable toolchain with `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1` and `-Zlocation-detail=none` in the ship build's wasm flags, which drops file, line and column from our own crates' and dependencies' panics (the prebuilt standard library keeps its own).

| Game (ship build, wasm only) | Raw before | Raw after | gzip -9 before | gzip -9 after | Saved gzip |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2d-platformer | 655,697 | 642,633 | 283,314 | 278,337 | 4,977 |
| sandbox | 482,767 | 470,947 | 217,005 | 212,624 | 4,381 |

Proposal for Chris (not adopted): about 4.4 to 5.0 KB gzip per game, more with `build-std` (std's own locations and panic strings too). Against it: `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP` on a stable compiler is unsupported, and a crash report from a shipped game would no longer say where it happened. If wanted, the cleaner route is a pinned nightly for ship builds only, with `build-std` and `panic_immediate_abort`, measured first.

## Point lights and clustered forward (S5-R3, 2026-10-20)

- **Two features, off by default.** evani-render `point-lights` (needs `forward`; turns on evani-core `point-lights` for the tier field) and `clustered` (= `point-lights` + `compute`). A kit pushes `Light { position, color, range, intensity }` each frame with `RenderWorld::draw_light`; the list clears with the frame like blobs. No new dependencies.
- **Tier cap from data.** `max_point_lights` in `assets/tiers/*.json` (optional, 0 = none): lite 0, low 0, medium 8, high 64, ultra 256, vr 0. Over the cap the lights whose sphere comes nearest any view's eye are kept (`light::nearest`, O(n x cap), no sort, so no sort code in the download). Medium's 8 is the catalog's ("up to 8 point lights", `catalog/tiers.json` render path); High (clustered forward with compute light binning) and Ultra give no count, so 64 and 256 are provisional, Chris to confirm.
- **One uniform, bind group 3.** Header `[count, binned views, slice scale, near]`, with `clustered` four view records (pixel rect, eye, right + tan x, up + tan y, forward), then 8 floats a light (`[position, range]`, `[colour x intensity, 0]`). Groups 1 and 2 get an empty group where a pipeline has none (forward `[frame, empty, empty, lights]`, models `[frame, material, empty, lights]`, skinned `[frame, material, joints, lights]`).
- **The light term is spliced, not written into the WGSL files.** At pipeline build `gpu/lights.rs::patch` rewrites the lit shading line (`frame.ambient.rgb + ...`) of `fs_main`, `model_fs` and `model_relief_fs` and appends the light code, so slot line numbers stay valid and every game without the feature ships the same shader text. Unlit materials and sprites never reach those lines. Falloff: a smooth window to zero at `range` over an inverse-square core, through the look's `toon_ramp` slot (lit side only), so lights band like the sun.
- **Clustered: 16 x 9 tiles x 16 exponential depth slices a view, up to 4 views.** `shaders/cluster_bin.wgsl` (one thread a cluster, before the graph runs) tests each cluster's view-space box against every light's sphere and keeps up to 31 as u8 indices in 8 words (count in byte 0). The fragment finds its view by pixel rect and reads its cluster's list. Falls back to the plain loop over the first 8 lights on a tier capped at 8 or less, when the binning pipeline fails its error scope, or for a pixel outside every view rect.
- **Shared-file edits are count-neutral.** Every hook in existing files is appended to an existing line behind `#[cfg(feature = ...)]`, because panic locations are in the wasm. Proof: kart-racer and 2d-platformer ship builds are SHA-1 identical (wasm and js) to the pre-S5-R3 builds when built with `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT` pinned to the same commit; without the pin they differ only by the 7-character commit id evani-core's build.rs stamps into every game.
- **Cost (sandbox ship build, measured, then reverted):** `point-lights` +5,065 B raw wasm, +2,284 B gzip total (312,470 to 314,754). `clustered` +12,926 B raw wasm, +5,390 B gzip total (to 317,860), of which the js glue is +271 B gzip (the sandbox has no other compute pass, so this includes the first compute pipeline's bindings).
- **Validation.** naga 30.0.1 (the version wgpu already pins) parses and validates the spliced forward module (plain 3D, and models + skinning + relief + shadows) with the linear and clustered light code, and `cluster_bin.wgsl`; group 3 is used only by the three lit fragment stages. Done in a gitignored scratch crate; naga is not a project dependency. Runtime tests and captures are deferred (Chris, 2026-10-20).
- **Catalog gaps.** `catalog/tiers.json` gives a point-light count only for Medium (8); High names clustered forward with compute binning but no light count, cluster grid or depth slices, and Ultra's path does not mention lights at all. `catalog/effects.json` has only `lights2d` (2D dynamic lights) and no 3D point-light effect (count, radius, colour, falloff). `catalog/looks.json` says nothing about point lights; the light term follows each look through its `toon_ramp` slot. `assets/render_paths.json` still lists `point-lights` missing on medium/high/ultra and `clustered-forward` missing on high/ultra; it should drop those once Chris accepts this (not edited here, per the brief).

## Kit material and the first-person view model (S5-E3, 2026-10-20)

- **Two features, off by default, both need `forward`.** evani-render `kit-material` and `view-model`. No new dependencies; the WGSL files that existed before are untouched.
- **Kit material.** `KitMaterial` (a world resource) holds a kit's WGSL `fn kit_material(p, n, material) -> vec4<f32>` and a `kit_data` array of vec4 (read-only storage, group 1 binding 15, clear of the model material bindings). At pipeline build the forward module's base-colour line is rewritten to multiply by the result's rgb and `discard` where its alpha is below 0.5, and the code is appended; the shadow pass switches to a cut-out pipeline (`shaders/kit_shadows.wgsl` plus the kit code) only while the kit has code, so holes cut colour, depth and the shadow map alike. Without kit code the default returns `vec4(1.0)`. Meshes only: models and skinned models do not run it. A kit WGSL that fails its error scope leaves the forward pipeline missing (logged), not the old one.
- **View model.** `RenderWorld::draw_view_model(MeshInstance)` in camera space (x right, y up, -z forward); `ViewModel { meshes, fov_y_deg: 55, near: 0.01 }`, far 50 m. Each frame the meshes go to world space through the frame camera (the first split-screen view if any; rotation = camera quaternion from the columns right, up, -forward times the local one) and are appended after the world's instance runs, so the shadow pass never sees them (they cast no shadow). A frame uniform of their own (their projection, the world's lighting and shading tail) and a `view_model` pass after the forward pass and its MSAA resolve: `hdr` loaded and stored, a one-sample Depth32Float of its own cleared to `depth::CLEAR` and discarded, the one-sample forward pipeline, the same lights and kit groups. They never clip into a wall the player stands against.
- **Gaps (accepted for now).** No MSAA on the view model; depth-reading post effects (such as the outline) do not see it; the clustered light lookup picks clusters from the world's depth slices, so it is slightly off near screen edges for view-model pixels; no models in the view model; capture jitter is not applied to it.
- **Shared-file edits are count-neutral.** Every hook in an existing file is appended to an existing line behind `#[cfg(feature = ...)]` (lib.rs, world.rs, gpu/pipelines.rs, gpu/frame.rs), or new code at a file's end, because panic locations are in the wasm. Proof: 2d-platformer and kart-racer ship builds (`EVANI_GIT_COMMIT` pinned) are SHA-1 identical, wasm and js, to the pre-S5-E3 base 561432b.
- **Cost (sandbox ship build, `--no-diff`, measured, then reverted):** base 312,470 B gzip total (wasm 659,185 B raw). `kit-material` +8,442 B raw wasm, +3,450 B gzip total (to 315,920). `view-model` +4,226 B raw wasm, +1,917 B gzip total (to 314,387). The js glue is unchanged within 2 B.
- **Validation.** wasm32 and native `cargo check` with no features, each feature, and `clustered,skinning,relief,kit-material,view-model`; clippy `-D warnings` clean on wasm32 for each feature and with shadows, point lights, clustered, meshes and particles; naga 30.0.1 validates the spliced forward and kit shadow modules (gitignored scratch crate). With `models` on, clippy reports `large_const_arrays` at shaders.rs `FORWARD_BYTES` (S3-3), also without these features. Runtime tests and captures are deferred (Chris, 2026-10-20).

## Hexagon's look: photoreal, the held weapon and hole cut-outs (S5-K6c, 2026-10-20)

- **Look.** `apps/tactical-fps` runs on `photoreal` with effects bloom, shadows and fog (grade and vignette left out: the plan keeps them for Ultra, and the look already grades in its tonemap). The toon look stays an optional dependency behind the `toon` / `relief` features. The kit still names no look: it finds materials by role name (`concrete`, `concrete_dark`, `steel_worn` / `steel` / `metal`, `paint_blue` + `paint_orange` / `team`, `lamp` / `glow`, `muzzle_flash`, `rubber`, `cloth`, `dirt` / `dust`); a material made for the role shows its own colour (instance colour white), a generic one is tinted with the look bible palette. Markings (hazard stripes, lift frames, core zones) use the brightest lit non-metal paint, tinted so the result is the wanted colour, because the look's emissive is not tinted by instance colour.
- **Hole cut-out (kit material).** `src/cut.rs` + `src/cut.wgsl`: `kit_data` holds a header (ring cos/sin, slots, floor heights, slab, arena radii), the `holes.rs` bucket table as (first, count) pairs built by a counting sort (each hole in every bucket its rim reaches, 1.12 x 1.6 x r, newest first, at most 256 read per fragment), the standing cover cells as bits (24 per float, whole numbers, never bit-cast) and the hole entries. The `concrete` slot is discarded inside a hole's jagged sphere (r x (1 + 0.12 jag), jag a smooth wave sum seeded by the centre) and darkened towards the rim; `concrete_dark` crater walls (inside-out spheres at 1.12 x 1.04 r, newest 600 holes) are kept only inside solid concrete and outside every other hole. Same code in colour, depth and shadow. Rebuilt only when the arena seed, hole version, hole count, newest hole, fallen cells or slots change; a ring turn alone patches the header. Over `MAX_DATA` the oldest holes drop out of the drawing only. Without a `concrete` material nothing is cut and the old dark hole balls are drawn. Original code; Hexagon's `keep()` was read for the idea only (no Voronoi chunks or crack network yet).
- **Held weapon.** Drawn with `draw_view_model` in first person only (own 55 degree lens and depth, so it never clips a wall); pistol, sniper, launcher and grenade from cubes, cylinders and a sphere, with a walk bob (render time, drawing only), a recoil kick from `Soldier::recoil`, and a muzzle flash for 0.06 s after one of our shots or rockets. The player's own body is not drawn in first person.
- **Pointer lock.** Wanted in warm-up and play while not paused, given back on the title, results and pause; death keeps it so a respawn needs no click. The engine releases it on Esc, blur, hidden tab, exit and errors. Touch: a right-half look pad drives the mouse's `aim_x` / `aim_y`.
- **Cost (ship build).** 834.9 KB raw / 337.4 KB gzip total (wasm 730.2 / 319.0) against K6b's 794.8 / 319.2 (wasm 690.9 / 301.0): +40.1 KB raw, +18.2 KB gzip for the look, fog, kit material, view model, pointer lock and the cut-out together.
- **Validation.** naga 30.0.1 validates the cut-out WGSL inside the shadow-pass module; kit clippy `-D warnings` native with net off and on, wasm32 `cargo check` net off and on, app clippy, existing tests compile. No new tests or captures: testing deferred (Chris, 2026-10-20).

## Shadow cascades and soft shadows (S5-R4, 2026-10-20)

Two evani-render features, both implying `shadows`: `shadow-cascades` (two cascades for the sun) and `pcss` (blocker search plus variable-width filter; `soft-shadows` is an alias, matching the stylizedpbr look). Both are shader variants built at compile time from new WGSL files, so a game without them keeps the exact same source and wasm.

- **Fit:** the far cascade is today's whole box in the left half of one depth atlas; the near cascade shares its centre and depth range, with half-size = far half x `render.shadow_cascade_split` (0.3, min 1 m), in the right half. Each box snaps to its own texel grid and sizes never change as the camera turns, so nothing shimmers. A blend band (`render.shadow_cascade_blend`, 0.15) fades near into far.
- **PCSS:** adopted from ChimpDuty's `pcss.wgsl` with its origin note kept. 16 taps with a PCF radius (High, Ultra), 8 without (Medium, VR). Slot `pcss_params` (penumbra per metre, search radius, min and max filter radius in texels). Lite and Low keep blob shadows.
- **Tiers:** `shadow_cascades` is 2 on High and Ultra, 1 elsewhere. Catalog gap: Ultra's 4 cascades are Stage 8 work.
- **Cost:** sandbox ship build, gzip: base 305.1 KB; `shadow-cascades` +1.2 KB (+3.1 KB raw); `pcss` +0.6 KB (+2.1 KB raw). Ultra's atlas is 8192x4096 depth, about 128 MB against 64 MB today.
- **Proof:** with the commit stamp pinned (`EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=r4pin00`), the 2d-platformer and kart-racer ship builds have the same SHA-1 as before (wasm 14a80570..., 633a0446...; js 3e4e0704..., 1154b914...).
- **render_paths should say:** each feature is a cascade or PCSS shader variant. The final test pass must check seams at the blend band, shimmer while turning, contact hardening, and that `shaders.rs` slot-list test gains `pcss_params` under `pcss`.

## Sky lighting (`ibl`) and ambient occlusion (`gtao`) (S5-R5, 2026-10-20)

Two evani-render features, both off by default and compiled out when off. They add frame-group hooks (`fwd_amb`) and shader variants built at compile time, so a game without them keeps the exact same source and wasm.

- **`ibl` design:** the sky becomes lighting. Diffuse is a 9-coefficient spherical-harmonics ambient taken from the sky; reflections come from an analytic sky (or a small sky cube where the tier asks for one) with Karis's mobile environment-BRDF fit, so there is no BRDF lookup texture and no downloaded cubemap. The sky cube is redrawn when the sun or sky parameters change.
- **`gtao` design:** ground-truth AO as fragment passes only (no compute or storage limits): a depth prepass at AO resolution (the shadow depth pipeline with each view's matrix flipped to conventional depth), a linear-depth chain of 5 mips (XeGTAO's weighted 2x2 filter), XeGTAO's slice integration with the normal rebuilt from depth into R8Unorm, then one depth-aware 3x3 denoise. AO darkens only the ambient term.
- **Tier ladder (`TierPreset::gtao`, `ibl_cube`):** gtao 0 off, 1 half resolution with 1 slice x 2 steps, 2 full resolution 2 x 2, 3 full resolution 3 x 3. Both tier fields are optional and appended, so existing tier files stay valid.
- **Cost (sandbox ship build, stamp pinned `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=r5pin00`, wasm + js gzip level 9):** plain 310.0 KB (765,281 B raw); `ibl` 316.5 KB, +6.5 KB (+16.8 KB raw); `gtao` 316.5 KB, +6.5 KB (+17.8 KB raw; its gzip total matched `ibl` to the byte, which is a coincidence: the raw sizes and file hashes differ); both 321.6 KB, +11.6 KB (+30.3 KB raw).
- **Proof (off = byte-identical):** with the stamp pinned, 2d-platformer and kart-racer ship builds have the same SHA-1 as the pre-change builds for both the .wasm and the .js (2d: wasm 76c85dde..., js 3e4e0704...; kart: wasm 5d4a9762..., js 1154b914...). `cargo xtask build sandbox` and `kart-racer` pass; `cargo xtask check` is clean (4 warnings, none new). cargo check matrix (native and wasm32, all feature mixes) is clean; WGSL validated. No tests were written or run for this stage.
- **Clippy:** clean except two old `large_const_arrays` errors at shaders.rs:1001 and :1230, which fire without these features too; not fixed here.
- **Credits:** the AO structure follows Bevy's SSAO (MIT OR Apache-2.0, the Bevy contributors) and XeGTAO (MIT, Copyright (C) 2016-2021 Intel Corporation); the notice is in the gtao.rs header and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. The WGSL is our own fragment-shader version. The mobile environment-BRDF fit is Karis's (Epic, "Real Shading in Unreal Engine 4").
- **Gaps and notes for the test pass:** `assets/render_paths.json` should drop `image-based-lighting` from High and Ultra once accepted (not edited here). HEXAGON_PLAN has ssao off on Medium while our Medium gtao tier is 1. There is no per-material roughness yet, so reflections use one value. The AO prepass reuses the shadow depth bias. AO covers up to 4 views. Final pass must check halo/leak at depth edges, AO on the ambient term only (direct light untouched), sky cube refresh when sun/sky parameters change, and the phone cost of tier 1.

## Per-game statement: build-facts.json (2026-10-20)

`cargo xtask build <id> --ship` and `publish` write `build-facts.json` and `ENGINE-LICENSE.txt` beside index.html: engine version and commit, licence, modules, code sizes, network, data, ads/analytics/tracking, storage, input, AI use and content rights, generated from evani.toml and the build, the same for every portal. Nothing loads it, so it adds 0 bytes to the download; no new dependency. Only the optional `[statement]` table (game licence, third-party files) is author-written. Basis: the portal reply to ChimpVibe (a short statement beside the game, no toolchain versions or import lists).
## Rival karts from a shared part pack (S4-1b, 2026-10-20)

- **Decision.** Rival karts are built from one shared part pack recombined and recoloured by recipes in `kits/evani-kit-kart-racer/data/rivals.json`, never one model per rival (Chris: games stay light). The kit refuses a recipe file that uses more than 12 different models. No new dependency.
- **Cost seen.** The `models` module adds about 72 KB gzipped code to the kart racer (`cargo xtask build kart-racer` size table, 2026-10-20), on top of the part pack files, which stream after the title. Chris decides whether that is accepted; the kart racer already shipped without it.
- **Decision (Chris, 2026-10-20: "Use your recommendation").** The `models` module cost (about 72 KB gzipped code) is accepted for the kart racer on the condition that the part pack goes ahead; the shared-pack rule (at most 12 models) and streaming after the title stay. Revisit if the real part pack is not built.
## Positional (3D) audio, S5-E2 (2026-10-20)

- **Own feature, off by default.** `evani-core`'s `audio-3d` (a game's app feature forwards to it). Off, none of the positional types, mixer methods, backend methods or `web-sys` panner bindings are compiled, so games that don't list it are byte for byte what they were. The feature turns on four web-sys features only (no new crate): `PannerNode`, `PanningModelType`, `DistanceModelType`, `AudioListener`.
- **Existing audio API unchanged.** No signature changed. `AudioBackend` gains four methods (`set_listener`, `play_at`, `voice_start_at`, `voice_set_position`) that exist only with the feature and have default bodies, so a custom backend still compiles either way. `AudioMixer` gains `set_listener`, `listener`, `play_at`, `play_at_on`, `voice_start_at`, `voice_set_position`, `voice_position`; stopping, pitch and gain of a positional voice use the existing `voice_stop` and `voice_set`. New types: `Listener`, `Spatial`, `Falloff`.
- **Web Audio mapping.** Source -> gain -> `PannerNode` (equal-power panning, the chosen distance model, ref/max distance and rolloff) -> the bus, so bus volume, master and mute apply as before. The listener is `AudioContext.listener` (`setPosition`, `setOrientation`), resent when the context is first built. Coordinates: metres, right-handed, y up, default facing -z (the Web Audio convention). A kit sets the listener once a frame; core does not read the camera.
- **Cheap by design.** A one-shot beyond `Spatial::max_distance` of the listener is not started at all. Equal-power rather than HRTF panning (HRTF costs more on phones); it can become an option later.
- **Not done here.** No Doppler, no cones or occlusion, no automatic listener from the camera, no tests (deferred to the combined pass).
## The stylizedpbr look (S5-L2, 2026-10-20)

- **Ported, not rewritten.** `looks/evani-look-stylizedpbr` is ChimpDuty's prepared crate (branch `chimp-model`, `handover/evani-dev/gaps/evani-look-stylizedpbr`, Chris's own project, written from scratch; outside work read for ideas only), with its origin note kept in the crate docs and README. No new dependencies.
- **The catalog wins.** Where the handover differed from `catalog/looks.json` the look follows the catalog: a vibrant, saturated palette instead of muted military swatches, rim 0.55 instead of 0.3 ("strong rim"), saturation 1.15, and it asks for the catalog's `ssao` effect (the handover asked for a `gtao` effect id the catalog does not have).
- **Engine half left to S5-R5.** No evani-render change here. The look exposes named hooks (`src/hooks.rs`: material bindings 5 to 9, `model_lit_vs`/`model_lit_fs`, `shadow_vis` for PCSS, slots `specular`, `surface_params`, `ibl_params`, `pcss_params`, generators `surface.detail`, `surface.clump`, `surface.env`); ChimpDuty's unapplied reference patch is in `r5/`. Image-based light is a hook only: the handover lit from a sky/ground hemisphere.
## S5-K5b: Cinder Ridge art pass on stylizedpbr (2026-10-20)

- **Art as data.** kits/evani-kit-base-building-rts/data/art.json holds the target pack's palette, nine material roles (each a list of names to try, first match wins, else the look's first material) and part recipes from the built-in meshes; src/art.rs resolves it and divides each palette colour by the chosen material's base colour, so the look decides how a surface takes light while the palette stays what the player sees. The kit never names a look; any 3D look with none of the names still draws.
- **Kit turns on evani-render point-lights and `frustum-cull`.** Lights mark crystal, hub beacons, foundry glow and shot impacts (the tier cap keeps the nearest); culling drops the off-screen scenery and the backdrop ring of ridges and pines. No new dependencies.
- **Sun.** The kit applies `art.json` sun_dir (key light from the north-west, look bible) to `render.light.direction` once, on its first drawn frame; the look keeps the light's colour and strength, and live tuning can move it afterwards.
- **Draw-only memory.** Rubble where a building fell and each bastion's turret turn are remembered in the draw cache, never in the match, so the simulation stays lockstep-safe.
- **Cost.** Ship build 320.2 KB gzip total (wasm 302.4, js 16.2) against the 293.7 KB greybox baseline: +26.5 KB for the look switch, both render features, the art data and the drawing.
- **Render path note.** The check's render-path line still lists cpu-frustum-culling and point-lights as missing for this game: it reads the app's features, and these come in through the kit. Left for the join (render_path.rs is off-limits to this branch).
## S5-K5b fix: culling checked, bloom note (2026-10-20)

- **Frustum culling is correct for the RTS camera; no engine change.** Chris's overlay showed `culled 2289/2640` and 12 draws, which looked like most of the scene vanishing. Checked in Chrome (RTX 4060 Ti): at the start zoom the camera sees only fog-covered ground round the hub (the map is 128 m across; unexplored fog is drawn as dark slabs on purpose), and zoomed out to the map edge the backdrop ridges and pines draw right up to every screen edge while the count falls to `1077/2136`. Draws count instanced batches, not objects, so 12 is normal. The planes, reversed depth, quaternion order (same as `forward.wgsl`: rotate(scale * local) + position) and every built-in mesh radius match. Added `cull::tests::oblique_camera_never_drops_what_it_sees` (Cinder Ridge's camera over 640 terrain runs, 600 yawed, tilted and stretched pieces and an 800 m ground plane): nothing with a corner on screen is culled, and over half the off-screen map is. Test-only; shipped bytes unchanged.
- **The stylizedpbr look posts `fx.bloom.threshold` only when bloom is built.** Cinder Ridge leaves bloom out on purpose (evani.toml: strategy clarity, no bloom, vignette or blur; the catalog does not ask for it), so adding bloom for a log line would cost bytes and go against the game's plan. The look now checks the param registry before posting (the fx module always registers before the look in the app template), so the start log no longer shows "fx.bloom.threshold not in this build"; with `--features bloom` the threshold is still 2.5 (`modules_match_features`), and `overlay_lists_modules` fails if any "not in this build" note comes back. The toon, basic and cartoon2d looks post the same param unconditionally, so a game on those looks without bloom (model-view) still shows the note; same one-line fix when wanted.
## Join: touch look pad behind its own switch; match-3 byte for byte again (2026-10-20)

- **2026-10-20 — The S5-E1 touch look pad is its own evani-core feature, `touch-look` (brings `touch`).** Chris's standing rule: a game downloads only what it uses, and a game that doesn't use a new feature builds byte for byte as before. His choice for the look pad: "own switch, so only games that use it download it". tactical-fps's kit turns it on; every other game (plain `touch` or none) carries none of it. `LookPad`, `quantise_delta`, `TouchLayout::look_pad`, the look resolve and drag code live in `touch/look.rs` and `web/touch/look.rs`; the hooks in `input.rs` (`TouchSlot.delta`, `add_touch_delta`), `touch.rs` and `web/touch.rs` sit on the ends of m1-foundation's lines, so every line keeps its number (panic locations are in the wasm).
- **Two more join changes in match-3's build were folded the same way.** FX1's per-frame `Effect::frame` hook (now compiled only with `fog`, `decals` or `grade`, the three effects that use it) and its module lines in `evani-fx/src/lib.rs`, and E3's pointer-lock lines in evani-core `lib.rs`, `plugin.rs`, `web/mod.rs` and `web/runner.rs`. Behaviour with the features on is unchanged.
- **Proof.** match-3 ship build (`cargo xtask build match-3 --ship --no-diff`, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=join0000`, 2 jobs) SHA-1: wasm `da15bb776a9a564ca420812c1862e9f225626706`, js `19b69c6854cd764c881d214d8261b249f0ea6aac`, the same as m1-foundation's (`.evani-tmp-join/m3-m1.txt`, built from an m1-foundation copy the same way); before this change the wasm was `5fb86831…`/`47fcd68e…` and 179 bytes bigger.

- **2026-10-20 — S6-5: the pseudo-3D road is evani-render feature `pseudo3d`, drawn as 2D quads in the sprites pass.** The OutRun-style view arcade racer (027, camera `pseudo-3d-road` in catalog/genres.json) needs is projected on the CPU (`crates/evani-render/src/pseudo3d.rs`) into quads in CSS pixels and drawn in one instanced call (`gpu/pseudo3d.rs`, new `shaders/pseudo3d.wgsl`) at the start of the `sprites` pass, before the frame's sprites, so a kit's car and HUD sprites sit on top. It binds the sprite pass's own group 0 (atlas, sampler, viewport uniform; layout A) and samples the built-in white square for solid fills, so it adds no target, no bind group layout and no pass, and needs neither `forward` nor any other feature (a 2D racer can use it alone). Painter's order instead of the classic clip-height: segments are emitted far to near, each followed by its sprites, so hill crests hide the road and the scenery behind them and nearer sprites cover farther ones without any clipping code; ground fully hidden by a nearer crest is skipped. The track (`RoadTrack`) is built with eased sections and polynomial eases (no trig) and seeded scatter from the kit's `Pcg32`, and `curve_at` / `height_at` give gameplay the road under a car in the fixed step, so the road is deterministic; only the backdrop silhouette uses `sin`, and it is render-only. Units: distance along the road in world units, lateral position and sprite sizes in road half-widths, heights in segment lengths, so the tunables rescale the whole view. Live parameters, registered by the render module only with the feature: `render.pseudo3d.curve`, `.hill`, `.draw_distance` (i32, segments), `.segment_length`, `.fog`, `.camera_height`, `.fov`, `.road_width`. Hooks are appended to the ends of existing lines only (frame.rs lines 208, 463, 1064, 1260, 1263 and 1272; gpu/mod.rs 11; lib.rs 37 and 261), so with the feature off every line number and column is unchanged. With the 3D path on, the empty-sprites skip also waits for the road (the cfg pair on lines 1260 and 1263). Byte check with `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=fc9d5ab`: the sandbox and 2d-platformer ship builds have the same wasm and js SHA-1 before and after (sandbox b3c681e / 26d4c58, 2d-platformer 5258c57 / 3e4e070). Cost with it on (sandbox ship module set, Trunk release, gzip -9, same flags for every variant): off 646,816 raw / 285,634 gzip wasm; engine feature on with no road shown 658,886 / 290,749 (+12,070 raw, +5,115 gzip, js +1 byte); with the sandbox proof (`pseudo3d-proof`: track, scenery, car) 662,212 / 292,344 (+15,396 raw, +6,710 gzip). Part of the engine cost is the float maths it links (`sin`, `tan`, `exp`); a kit that needs it smaller can ask for approximations.
- **2026-10-20 — The sandbox proof for S6-5 is a separate feature, `pseudo3d-proof`, never in evani.toml.** `apps/sandbox/src/proof_road.rs` builds a seeded closed track of about 500 segments (bends of curve 3 to 6 both ways, hills of 15 to 40 segment lengths, kerb posts every 8 segments, trees, bushes and flowers scattered with seed 0x56005), drives it at 60 units per tick from `Time::tick` and leans the camera out of each bend, with a two-sprite car on top. Like `relief` and `skin-proof`, it is built with `trunk build --features pseudo3d-proof`; default and ship builds are unchanged. The road covers the sandbox's 3D grid in that build.
- **2026-10-21 — Several live sessions in one folder (LIVE_API.md 2.6; asked for by jdholst on the ChimpVibe Discord).** `cargo xtask live` and `dev` without `--port` pass the bridge's new `serve --any-port`: 8096 if free, else the first free port from 8098 to 8139 (8097 is the Model tab's, 8180 up the review bridges'), else any. Without the flag a taken port stays an error, so the Evani.dev tab's launcher (`serve --port 8096`) behaves exactly as before. Each default-session bridge writes `.evani/live/sessions/<port>.json` and also `session.json`, which is therefore the most recent session; on exit a bridge hands `session.json` to the newest session still answering on its port (liveness is a 250 ms TCP connect, no process API and no new dependency). A second bridge on a game that a running bridge already holds (`target/evani-live/<game>/live-owner.json`) builds into `target/evani-live/<game>-port<P>/` with its own cargo and dx target dirs, starting from a copy of the shared build when that is current. MCP: every game tool takes an optional `session` (port or game name, never sent on to the bridge), `EVANI_LIVE_SESSION` sets the default per MCP server, and the new `live_sessions` tool lists them. Dev tools only; nothing reaches a game's download.
- **2026-10-21 — Lit surface pass (S5-R5b): the engine feature `look-surface` lights models with metal-rough and occlusion maps, with four rules.** (1) Relief wins on bindings: the gate is `all(look-surface, not(relief))`, so both never claim material slots 5, 6 and 8 together. (2) The glue avoids the patch-hook text: the lit shader is joined at build time from parts and never contains the text the patch hooks search for (tests/lit_wgsl.rs checks it). (3) Ambient takes the lower of the map occlusion and the screen-space occlusion, so the two never darken twice. (4) A look cannot push point lights: it supplies only `surface_params`; lights stay with the point-lights module. Same-line cfg hooks keep every shared file's line numbers. Proof: naga validates both shadow tails, with and without skinning, with a film-style `surface_params` body (cases 0-11) and a real specular lobe; 97 unit, 3 lit_wgsl and 9 model_wgsl tests pass with `look-surface`, 102/3/9/2 with the full mix. Two unit tests and model_wgsl already fail on main with `shadow-cascades,pcss` because the tests never declare `pcss_params` (reproduced without look-surface); not caused by this work. Byte check with `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=lit00000`: match-3 (wasm 1e951cdd, js 19b69c68) and base-building-rts (wasm 3b94d48c, js 8692a458) ship builds have the same SHA-1 as m1-foundation 4b61971. Cost with the sandbox `lit-proof` feature (ship module set, Trunk release, gzip -9): off 648,583 raw / 289,336 gzip wasm; on 701,799 / 310,739 (+53,216 raw, +21,403 gzip; js unchanged at 106,096 / 16,706). Clippy `-D warnings` needed `#[allow(clippy::large_const_arrays)]` on the three const-built shader byte arrays (placed on the same line).
- **2026-10-21 — Film preset (S5-R5b): the stylizedpbr look gets a `film` feature.** `FilmLook` with `presets/film.json` gives a warm key light, cool fill and a back-edge kick that follow the camera, deeper shadows, and skin and eye surface kinds on models tagged `evani_surface.kind`. It sits behind its own cfg gates so base-building-rts stays byte-identical. The sandbox `lit-proof` feature turns on `look-surface` plus `film` over the basic look and is kept out of evani.toml, like `relief`. Credit: the film look comes from ChimpDuty, Chris Pearson. 4 of 4 film tests pass.
- **2026-10-21 — Film preset retuned: lit-proof keeps the plain scene's brightness, film shadows are deeper.** With lit-proof on, the sandbox washed out (mean 140 to 197, clipping 1.9 to 6.4%, ground shadows 49 to 128, cream sky, milky cubes, glows yellow at 54 deg) while the marshal read as intended. Causes: the film's scene-wide values hit everything the basic look draws: the stylizedpbr filmic curve is about 1.6x brighter in the midtones than basic's, render.ambient 0.30 and wrap 0.35 light the shadowed ground, rim 0.9 milks the cubes, the clear colour was cream, and the per-channel curve (`mix(pc, bl, 0.3)`) slides bright orange to yellow. Models get the film hemisphere (w = 1), which ignores render.ambient, so the scene and the marshal can be tuned apart. Changes, all film-only (base-building-rts never builds `film`): `presets/film.json` now has ambient [0.08, 0.09, 0.12], a blue clear colour [0.35, 0.55, 0.85], render.exposure 0.55, fx.bloom.threshold 4.0 (the karts' haze), wrap 0.1, rim 0.3, contrast 1.0, kick 0.5, and more model-only light to keep the marshal's key/fill/rim (sky 0.65, ground 0.25, fill 0.5); a new film param `look.stylizedpbr.hue_keep` (0.9; 0.3 is the plain curve) sets the tonemap's hue-keeping share; `film::post` skips an `fx.*` value the game doesn't build. Live tuning of this look never reached the screen: its param system wrote slot bodies with `ShaderSlots::set_body`, which no pipeline rebuild follows; under `film` it now also queues `SlotRebuilds::request_detached` (as photoreal does). The plain look keeps the old line (byte rule), so its live slot tuning is still broken: a follow-up for the plain look's next byte-changing release. Measured (4 views x Medium and Ultra, `.evani-shot-litproof/tuned/`): mean 144.2 vs 140.3 off (+2.8%), clipping 2.85%, ground shadows 41 to 43 vs 49 off, sky 211 deg (off 212), cube saturation 0.34 to 0.38 (off 0.39), glows 40 deg (off 35, before 54); marshal box 113 (off 76, before 145) with the warm key, buckle sheen, rim and cool fill kept. Ship builds of match-3, sandbox and base-building-rts are byte-identical to the join (EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971).
- **2026-10-21 — Socket rotation (ChimpDuty gap 13, Evani.Model track) is done at build time, with no engine code.** A socket is an anchor with a rotation: `extras.evani.sockets.<name> = {p, q}` in the model frame (docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md "Sockets"). Evani.Model writes one for each template anchor with a `q` (glTF `rotation` on the `anchor:<name>` node, `q` in its extras, and the root entry); `cargo xtask convert` (`tools/evani-convert/src/sockets.rs`) turns every socket into an anchor node with that rotation, adding one at the scene root or turning the one there after checking it stands at `p` (0.1 mm) and agrees with any rotation it has. The runtime already composed an anchor's whole world matrix (`Models::anchor_frame` = base TRS x anchor matrix, decomposed by `Frame::from_matrix`), so no reader or render change is needed: 0 bytes for every game, used or not, and anchors without `q` are written byte for byte as before. Chosen over reading `sockets` in `evani-gltf` (runtime JSON parsing in every game with `models`) and over a new `socket_matrix` API (`anchor_frame` already is that helper). Known limit: the frame is the rest pose; a socket on a skinned joint does not follow the animated joint yet (same as anchor positions today).
- **2026-10-21 — Fur locks (ChimpDuty gap module, Evani.Model track) are a build-time tool, `tools/evani-model-fur-locks`, not an engine module.** Ported as is from ChimpDuty's `evani-model-fur-locks` (evani-model is unchanged since its pin): tapered ribbon locks grown over fur regions, clumped, drooping, twice as long where fur meets gear, capped at 1,750 locks and 14,000 triangles, byte-identical per seed (one `Pcg32`, `dmath`, no threads). The `fur-locks` command line and `bake::bake_tier` append them to a compiled tier as one more solid named `fur_locks` and write the `.glb`, so a game gets plain triangles in its model file and 0 runtime bytes of code; no game depends on the crate. ChimpDuty's demo and one test used `evani-model-code` and a colour preview that Evani.Dev does not have: the demo became `fur-locks --check` and the test compiles the frontier marshal (the bench model with colour regions) instead. No new dependencies. Chosen over a runtime fur pass (code in every download, and a per-frame cost) and over a `compile` hook inside evani-model (it would grow the core crate for one look); a modeler flag can call `bake_tier` later.
- **2026-10-21 — Finger curl is a closed-form helper in evani-model (`pose::curl_finger`), not an IK solver.** It wraps a finger chain round a cylinder (a grip, handle or bar) by hinging every bone about the cylinder's axis, knuckle first, through the exact smallest angle that puts its far joint on the surface pushed out by the finger's half thickness (A cos + B sin = K, solved with `dmath::atan2`), capped at a per-joint limit; out of reach it turns to the closest approach and reports no contact. Deterministic (no iteration, no platform maths), mirrored hands come out mirrored, and the local rotations rebuild the posed joints exactly (tests). It lives in the build-time model crate, so 0 runtime bytes. Chosen over a general CCD/FABRIK solver (iterative, tolerance-dependent bits, more code than a grip needs). Limits: one hinge axis per bone (the cylinder's), no finger-to-finger collision, and the result is a rest-pose offset, not a runtime pose.

## S5-H2: shared helpers replace private copies (2026-10-21)

- **Moved.** `ParamRegistry::f32_or(name, default)` and `bool_or(name)` (evani-core `params.rs`) replace the private `param_f32` / `param_bool` readers in the 2d-platformer, kart-racer and top-down kits. `num::approach`, `num::wrap_angle` and `num::smooth01` (evani-core `num.rs`) replace the copies in the platformer (`approach`), the kart racer (`approach`, `wrap_angle`, `finale::smooth01`, `roadmarks::smooth`). Same formulas and operation order; the kart's `kart::wrap_angle` path still works (re-export). Audit items 10 and part of 7 (STAGE5_DUPLICATION_AUDIT.md).
- **Left for later, on purpose.** Match-3's own copies (`draw::param_f32`, `ease_in_out`) stay: match-3 is the byte-identity probe, and a kit edit moves panic line numbers in its wasm. The other audit items (colour, hash, sounds, `tunables!`, shake, DOM shell, particles, spline) change more code or what a player sees and are separate reviewed changes.
- **Byte cost: none.** match-3 ship build with `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT` pinned, before (4b61971) and after: wasm SHA-1 01a83a67e7e4fc7958f084382e077bae6236595a and js 19b69c6854cd764c881d214d8261b249f0ea6aac both times. The first try put the new functions in the middle of `params.rs` and `num.rs`, which moved line numbers in core and changed the wasm; they now sit at the end of those files (the lesson is in KIT_PROCESS.md).

## Stage 7 research picks: shared files and the byte budget (S7-R1, S7-R2, 2026-10-21)

- **Shared library files.** A file two games share lives at `/lib/<sha256>.<ext>` on one origin, served with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`. Browsers partition the HTTP cache by top-level site and keep Cache Storage per origin, so sharing only works with one origin and one path. The `stream` shim looks the hash up in Cache Storage, checks the bytes against it, and fetches again on a miss or a mismatch (S7-8, S7-9).
- **No shared engine wasm.** Each game's wasm is built with its own features, so two games rarely share a code file; splitting the engine into shared parts would fight build-time on/off. Sharing comes from library assets only.
- **Importance and budget (S7-3, S7-4).** A build-time `importance.json` per game from the projected on-screen size of each model along a seeded camera path; `budget` picks each asset's LOD count and texture size by value per byte (importance / bytes) against the existing `<id>.<tier>.glb` layout. Both are tools: 0 B in the shipped game.
- **Later, not now.** KTX2 with the small mips first (a progressive texture), once the budget exists.
- **Mesh compression.** meshopt only (`EXT_meshopt_compression`, already decoded); no Draco (a second, larger decoder for a similar result).
- **Known limit.** A game embedded on another site and the same game opened directly are different top-level sites, so they don't share a cache.

## Per-tier download targets in the catalog (S7-1, 2026-10-21)

- **Where.** `catalog/tiers.json` gets `download_targets`: code ceilings (gzip, wasm + js + html) 350 KB for 2D games and 500 KB for 3D games (a `3d` look style or `models`; pseudo-3D draws without `forward`, so it counts as 2D), and per tier `boot_kb`, `total_kb` and `texture_memory_mb` (75 percent of the tier's `max_texture_memory`). The numbers are the table Chris approved as provisional on 2026-10-20 (S4-3); they become final after the kart racer is measured on Low and Ultra (waiting on Chris). `assets/tiers/*.json` stay runtime-only: the targets are tool data and are never compiled into a game, so no ship build changes.
- **Checks.** `cargo xtask check` errors when the targets are missing or unsound (boot under total, everything under the 10 MB package cap, room past the 3D code ceiling, texture memory under the tier's ceiling; test `targets_match_the_approved_table`) and warns when a game's last publish summary has a tier over its target.
- **Publish.** `cargo xtask publish` prints `PASS` or `FAIL` per tier with code, boot and total and the margin under the tightest, and writes them to `summary.json` `sizes.tier_targets` (test `publish_flags_a_tier_over_its_target`). Each tier is judged on its heaviest texture family (ASTC, BC7 or PNG/JPEG), worked out from `stream.json` and each model's texture sets by the engine's own fetch rules (`evani-guide` `tier_fetch`), so a pass holds on every device. It reports; it does not block a package, because the targets are still provisional.

## Per-level asset groups (S7-7, 2026-10-21)

- **What.** `stream.json` entries may be in a group `level:<name>`. `cargo xtask manifest` writes them from `[stream.levels]` in evani.toml (level name to model ids or file names; a name takes the file of that name and every `<id>.` file; a file in two levels or a name that matches nothing is an error with a `fix:` line). Level groups are left out of the background plan, so a level downloads only when the game asks: `Models::prefetch("level:<name>")` (settled tier's files, the one texture set the device samples, low priority, into Cache Storage) or by making the handles. `Models::release("level:<name>")` frees the group's models (GPU textures, buffers, bind groups; the texture bytes come back off `max_texture_memory`) and keeps their handles idle until the next `prefetch`. Docs: docs/CONTENT.md "Level groups".
- **Where.** evani-render feature `level-groups` (implies `models`), opt-in: new files `model/handle/level.rs` and `gpu/models/level.rs`; hooks in `handle.rs`, `model.rs` and `gpu/models.rs` are `#[cfg(feature = "level-groups")]` statements appended to the ends of existing lines, so line counts, panic locations and WGSL are untouched. Levels come from evani.toml (build-time data), not from the simulation: models stay render-only.
- **Byte-identical proof.** `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971`, `cargo xtask build <id> --ship --no-diff`: match-3, base-building-rts, kart-racer and sandbox (feature off) have the same sha1, size and gzip size of `.js` and `_bg.wasm` as the S7 base table (`sha-base.txt`). None of them turns the feature on.
- **Byte cost when on (measured).** Sandbox ship build with `level-groups` on and `prefetch`, `release` and `texture_bytes` called (an upper bound: unused methods drop out): wasm 648,143 to 655,269 B raw (+7,126 B), 286,497 to 289,626 B gzip -9 (+3,129 B, about +3.1 KB); the `.js` is unchanged. This is **over the plan's estimate of about +1 KB gz**; it goes to Chris for a decision, not accepted here. Games without `models`, or that do not list the feature, pay 0 B.
- **Tests.** `release_frees_texture_memory`, `released_groups_stay_released_until_prefetched`, `a_level_downloads_only_when_asked`, `prefetch_before_the_manifest_waits`, `a_prefetched_file_is_a_cache_hit_later` (evani-render); `levels_group_their_files` (evani-guide). The GPU drop itself (`gpu/models/level.rs`) is browser-only and only compile-checked on wasm32; the sandbox two-level netlog proof is not done because it needs a game that turns the feature on.

## Measured on-screen importance: `cargo xtask importance` (S7-3, 2026-10-21)

- **What it does.** `cargo xtask importance <id> [--viewport WxH] [--out file]` runs the game natively and headless (the fixed 60 Hz step, a 1280x720 view unless told otherwise) with evani-render's dev-only `importance` feature turned on for that one run, and writes `apps/<id>/assets/importance.json`. The game's own capture moments (live's `CaptureMoments`, at most 24, sorted by name) and then 90 poses along its `BenchPath` are sampled: after a 45-frame warm-up the driver releases the clock, calls a moment's `setup`, holds the clock there, waits 3 frames and takes one sample; the path poses are one per frame. Every model draw in a sampled frame is projected into the view. A game with no moments or no path is measured on what it has; a game that draws no models gets "the run wrote no importance file" and a fix line.
- **The measure is an upper bound.** A draw is its model's bounding sphere (the one frustum culling uses) projected as a disc and clipped to the screen; its share is the disc's visible area over the screen's, its size the disc's diameter in pixels. No occlusion and no depth test, so a model behind a wall counts as seen; that over-counts a hidden model and never under-counts a shown one, which is the safe side for a texture budget. A camera inside the sphere counts as the whole screen.
- **File shape (version 1, sorted keys, six decimals, so two runs are byte for byte the same).** `game`, `models[]`, `samples{moments, path}`, `tool`, `version`, `viewport`. Each model: `id`, `max_screen_share`, `mean_screen_share`, `frames_on_screen`, `frames_total`, `max_px`, `sources` (the largest share per moment and for the path), `importance_score` = 0.5 x max + 0.5 x mean, and `suggested_texture_px` (a power of two, 16 to 4096, from the largest pixel size). `cargo xtask bytes` already reads `models[].id` and `max_screen_share` (S7-2) and shows them next to the model's bytes; the value-per-byte budget (S7-4) will read the rest.
- **0 bytes in a shipped game.** The code is `crates/evani-render/src/importance.rs`, compiled only with the `importance` feature (it needs `models` and evani-core's `capture`). No app's `evani.toml`, `default` features or ship feature set names it; `cargo xtask importance` adds `evani-render/importance` on the command line. The string `evani-importance` is in the file's output and its stderr line, and is now in `DEV_ONLY_STRINGS`, so the ship guard (and `publish`, which calls `dev_only_in`) fails a build that carries it with an `ERROR ... fix:` message (tests `ship_guard_refuses_the_importance_tool`, `importance_never_reaches_a_ship_build`). The sandbox has a feature `importance` for its test only, not in `evani.toml` or `default`, like `pseudo3d-proof`.
- **Hooks in shared engine files, all appended to the end of an existing line so no line moves.** `model.rs` line 602 (the `importance: Log` field of `Models`) and line 813 (`self.importance.note(...)` in `select_lods`, after the draw's model index is read, so it runs for every draw, single-LOD models too); `lib.rs` line 384 (`importance::drive(world);` at the start of the renderer's frame) and line 614 (`pub mod importance;` after `pub mod model;`). Everything else is the new file. The driver only delivers model files the game has not already taken from `assets/models`, so a game that loads its own models measures the same.
- **Test.** `importance_is_deterministic` (`apps/sandbox/src/importance_tests.rs`, `cargo test -p evani-sandbox --features importance`) runs the sandbox twice in one process (the settings go in as an `importance::Settings` resource, not the environment, because other tests share the process and the driver would deliver models to them) and compares the two files byte for byte, and checks `frontier-marshal` and `proof-kart` have a share above zero; unit tests in `importance.rs` check the projection, the file and the log, and in xtask the table and the arguments. The sandbox's own `importance.json` is committed (`bytes` reads it; it is under `assets/`, not `assets/models/`, so the page's `copy-dir` does not ship it): frontier-marshal 6.4% of the screen at most, proof-kart 5.6%, proof-crate 0.9%, proof-barrel 0.2%. kart-racer measures proof-kart 7.1%, proof-barrel 1.8%, proof-crate 1.6% from its title and race moments and its bench path.
- **Byte check.** `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971`, `cargo xtask build <id> --ship --no-diff`, 2 jobs, built in the `Evani.Dev-s7-3` worktree and compared with the S7 baseline from the `Evani.Dev-s7` worktree (so the folder name is not in the bytes). match-3: wasm `a7078a25c5ebba433cfd29685b249c0ebebe6287` (682,984 raw / 300,891 gzip), js `19b69c6854cd764c881d214d8261b249f0ea6aac`. sandbox: wasm `4c1581e5022938300c039fbb5ac195fafa2df1d3` (648,143 / 286,497), js `0a855822e34eb4902e09c5c4c37723841c764576`. All four equal the baseline, file names (content hashes) included.
- **Overlap.** S7-5 (converter) and S7-7 (level groups) touch `convert`, `stream.rs` and `handle.rs`; S7-3 does not. The only shared edit that may conflict is S7-7's change near `model.rs` line 602 (the `Models` struct), and it is a one-line field.

## The quality gate scores what each device samples (S7-5, 2026-10-21)

- **What changed.** A textured candidate is no longer scored with resized PNG maps alone. For every setting the gate makes the geometry file once and then one render file per texture family a device can end up with (`gate::FAMILIES`, the same list `tier_fetch` and `cargo xtask netlog --device` use): `astc` (phones), `bc7` (desktops) and `rgba` (the PNG/JPEG core every other device gets). The ASTC and BC7 maps come from the tier file's own encoder (`encode_ktx`, the same `ktx create` arguments, BC7 through UASTC and `ktx transcode`) run on the same lossless resized PNG the tier's sets are made from, then level 0 is decoded back to a PNG the gate page draws. The finished tier file is checked the same way: its own `astc` and `bc7` sets and its core images, one render each, with `extras.evani.textures` taken out of the copy so the gate's Chrome draws exactly that family.
- **Every family, judged on the worst.** A tier is not tied to a device class (a phone can run High, a desktop Low), so a setting passes only when all three families pass. The floor (the best rung, which sets the bar) is the worst family's score; a search trial stops at its first failing family, and the family that failed last is tried first next time, so a failing setting usually costs one render. The finished file passes only if every family passes, its sheet shows the worst family, and drift is the largest drop, family by family, from the chosen trial to the file (`family_drift`). The textured exemption in `drifted` is gone: drift is now checked on textured tiers too.
- **Decoded on the CPU, from level 0.** Scores don't depend on which compressed formats the gate's own Chrome can sample. ASTC is decoded by `ktx extract` (the KTX tools' reference decoder). `ktx extract` refuses BC7, so the converter has its own BC7 decoder, `tools/evani-convert/src/bc7.rs`, a port of bcdec's `bcdec_bc7` (Sergii Kudlai, MIT, notice in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md); BC7 decoding is exact by the format's definition. Test `bc7_decode_matches_the_ktx_tools`: against `ktx extract --transcode rgba8` of the UASTC file the BC7 set comes from, largest difference 1 and mean 0.063 of 255 per channel. The engine makes the decoded image's mips itself where the sets carry encoded ones; the gate's views are close enough that level 0 is what they sample.
- **The look fixture (test `trial_scores_the_device_encoding`).** The S3-12 look fixture (toon, `--min-distance 1`, as the open S3-6 finding measured it), every family scored. Medium: floor 80.9, bar 75.9; the search chose vp 14, vn 8, vt 10 at 256 px maps (trial 76.6, worst on bc7); the finished file scores 76.6, worst on bc7, and passes, no drift. Low: floor 80.4, bar 75.4; the search chose vp 14, vn 16, vt 10 at 128 px maps (trial 78.7, worst on bc7); the finished file scores 78.7 and passes, no drift. Before S7-5 the same fixture went from trial to finished file 81.1 to 76.7 (Medium) and 80.7 to 77.0 (Low): the trial now sees the loss, so the search stops at a setting whose file really passes. On this fixture the three families sit within about 0.6 of each other (Low's best rung without a look: astc 76.8, bc7 76.3, rgba 76.9), so BC7 is the worst family by a small margin, not a desktop-only quality problem. The BC7 set's UASTC step is close to lossless here (UASTC quality 2 decoded to RGBA8 against the source PNG: PSNR 61.0 dB, mean squared error 0.05; quality 4 gives 64.7 dB), so a slower UASTC level would not change the gate's verdict; the encoder settings are unchanged. Without a look the fixture's Low tier has no passing rung (best rung 76.3 on every family, under FLOOR_MIN 78, so the bar stays 90), as before S7-5: the old resized-PNG trial path made the same images as today's rgba family. Thresholds unchanged (FLOOR_MIN and the others stay provisional until Chris's blind test).
- **Cost.** Three renders instead of one for the floor and the finished file, about one for each failing trial; encoded maps are cached per texture size. Converter only: no game links it and no ship build changes (match-3 and sandbox ship byte for byte as before, EVANI_GIT_COMMIT pinned).

## Film preset fix 2: stone, grid and kart keep their mid-tones (2026-10-21)

- **What changed (data only).** `looks/evani-look-stylizedpbr/presets/film.json`: light direction [-0.2865, 0.866, 0.4095] (60 degrees up, the plain look posts 53), ambient [0.18, 0.19, 0.22] (was 0.08), contrast 0.85, exposure 0.5, model sky 0.42, fill 0.28, kick 0.35. The film test now expects contrast 0.85000. No shader, guard or WGSL file touched.
- **Numbers (Medium, all nine views, lit-proof against off; before in brackets).** Scene mean 143.9 against 142.1 off (1.01x). Clipping 0% ([2.36%]; off 1.48%). Navy cubes in the back overview 15.3% ([49.3%]; off 12.9%). Pillars (back) 50/62/70 ([38/52/61]; off 77/91/95); pillars (far) 37/38/46 ([25/24/37]; off 55/66/71). Marshal box 93.5 ([113]; off 76). Sky hue 210 to 211, orange 40. Shadow to lit-ground ratio 0.37 to 0.40 (off 0.31 to 0.39; before 0.27 to 0.31). Low: mean 143.8 against 141.6 off, clipping 0% (before 2.4%). The plain kart no longer clips (before 17% on Medium). Captures and the full numbers file: `.evani-shot-litproof/tuned2/`.
- **Trade-off.** In `forward.wgsl` cast shadows and turned-away faces share one light term (ambient plus key times ramp of min(ndl, -0.1)), so no data lever lifts dark stone without lifting ground shadows too. Film shadows are now about as deep as the plain look's, not deeper than it. The pillars stay darker than in the plain look because their slate colour is very dark and the camera sees their backlit side. The old film had cut ambient to 0.08, which is what crushed them.
- **Kart camera cut.** The proof karts moved to VARIANT_AT [8, 0, 19] and PLAIN_AT [19, 0, 8] (`apps/sandbox/src/proof_model.rs`), inside the orbit's frame; test `karts_stay_whole_in_the_orbit_frame`. This changes the sandbox default bytes (new SHAs in the hand-over), a real fix as allowed.
- **Build warning.** `deliver` is read only on native builds, so it has `#[cfg_attr(target_arch = "wasm32", allow(dead_code))]`; the lit-proof and default wasm builds print no "never read" warning.
- **Low draws more triangles: no tier bug.** Low has no shadow map, so it draws 400 shadow-disc decals at 24 triangles each (9,600). The Low file is picked correctly, but `frontier-marshal.low.glb` has the same 19,545 triangles as the base file; it is lighter in textures only. Open: a real mesh LOD through `cargo xtask convert`.
- **Byte check.** `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971`, `cargo xtask build <id> --ship --no-diff`, 2 jobs. match-3 (wasm `a7078a25...`, js `19b69c68...`) and base-building-rts (wasm `73336a11...`, js `fae52869...`) are identical to the join3 baseline. sandbox: js is unchanged (`0a855822...`, same size) and the wasm changed from `4c1581e5...` to `079a8d41...`, same 648,143 bytes raw and 286,497 gzip: only the proof karts' two spawn positions moved.

## ChimpDuty intake: collision as evani-physics `collide` (2026-10-21)

- **Everything now.** Chris chose "Everything now": ChimpDuty's collision gap comes into the engine now, even though STAGES.md would place it later.
- **Where.** Opt-in feature `collide` of `crates/evani-physics` (new files in `src/collide/`, three hooks at the ends of existing lines in `lib.rs`). It follows how `simple` and `rigid` are gated; no new crate. Off by default.
- **What.** Static tri-mesh world with a BVH, raycasts that return the surface tag, line of sight (with a skip-tags variant), sphere and capsule casts, and a character mover (slide, step-up, slopes, ceilings, ground snap). The chimp-sized preset is gone; `CharacterConfig::default()` is a generic adult. Nothing chimp-specific remains.
- **No new dependencies**, so THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md is unchanged.
- **Deterministic maths.** `collide::trig` gives sin, cos, atan2 and radians as plain polynomial code (same bits native and wasm). `min`, `max`, `clamp` and `hypot` are plain comparisons and `sqrt`. No wall-clock time; nothing random.
- **Tunables.** `physics.collide.radius`, `.height`, `.step_height`, `.max_slope`, `.skin`, `.snap_distance`, `.max_slides`, `.depenetrate_passes`, `.speed`, registered when `PhysicsModule` is in the app and changeable live.
- **Proof.** Sandbox feature `collide-proof` (`apps/sandbox/src/proof_collide.rs`): a capsule walks stairs, a platform, a ramp and a wall corner. Hooks are three line-end cfg edits in `apps/sandbox/src/main.rs`. Headless captures: `.evani-shot-intake/collide/before.png` and `after.png` (main checkout).
- **Bytes.** `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971`, `cargo xtask build <id> --ship --no-diff`: match-3, sandbox, base-building-rts and kart-racer have the same sha1, size and gzip size as `sha-join.txt`. When on (sandbox, ship feature list, release trunk builds): base 648,583 B (286,596 gzip); plus `evani-physics/collide` 651,065 B (287,581), that is +2,482 B (+985); plus the full proof scene 679,232 B (301,134), +30,649 B (+14,538).
