- **Tests.** 40 unit tests in the crate (rays with tags, line of sight, sphere and capsule casts, mover walls, steps, slopes, ground snap, determinism) plus the doc example, with the feature on and off; sandbox `capsule_walks_the_level` and `lap_restarts`.

## ChimpDuty intake: combat as `evani-combat` (2026-10-22)

- **Everything now.** Chris chose "Everything now": ChimpDuty's combat and bots gap comes into the engine now, as the collision gap did.
- **Where.** A new opt-in crate, `crates/evani-combat/` (hitscan, gun, health, grenade, rocket, blast, path, bot, tune, params, dmath), built on `evani-physics` feature `collide` (which the crate turns on itself). It is gameplay only shooters want, so it is a crate of its own, not a feature of an existing one; a kit that does not depend on it downloads none of it. No edits to evani-physics, evani-core or any look.
- **What.** Hitscan against the level's triangles and body hitboxes (zones; a wall in front stops the bullet); guns with spread, recoil, reload and heat; range falloff (`damage_at`); health with armour and helmet; bouncing grenades; rockets; blasts with falloff and line of sight; Dijkstra routes with a string pull; a bot brain (sight, sound, reaction delay, aim, orders) with the game supplying the route finder. The gap's chimp and CS names are gone: `Tuning::default()`, `Tuning.grenade`, weapons `rifle`, `pistol`, `launcher`; the JSON tuning loader is gone.
- **No new dependencies**, so THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md is unchanged (and `serde_json` is no longer pulled in by it).
- **Deterministic maths.** The gap's `Rng` is replaced by the engine's seeded `Pcg32`. Sine, cosine and atan2 come from `collide::trig`; the range falloff power is `dmath::powf` (plain code); min, max and clamp are plain comparisons. No wall-clock time and no platform maths library: a fight replays bit for bit on native and wasm.
- **Tunables.** `CombatModule` registers 114 live `combat.*` parameters (per weapon, grenade, rocket, bot and sound, plus health, armour, helmet, run speed and respawn), owner `combat`; `CombatParams::apply` refreshes a `Tuning` every fixed step.
- **Proof.** Sandbox feature `combat-proof` (`apps/sandbox/src/proof_combat.rs`): a bot routes round a wall along an `evani_combat::path` route with the collide mover, turns, waits out its reaction delay and shoots a target (tracers, hit marks, health and armour bars) at the live `combat.*` values. Hooks are line-end cfg edits in `apps/sandbox/src/main.rs`. Headless muted captures: `.evani-shot-intake/combat/before.png` and `after.png` plus 67 frames (main checkout); no page errors, about 59 fps.
- **Bytes.** `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971`, `cargo xtask build <id> --ship --no-diff`: match-3, sandbox, base-building-rts, kart-racer and tactical-fps have the same sha1, size and gzip size as `sha-join.txt`. When on (sandbox, ship feature list, release trunk builds): base 648,583 B (286,596 gzip); plus collide alone 651,065 B (287,581), +2,482 B (+985); plus the whole combat-proof scene 710,715 B (316,007), +62,132 B (+29,411) over base.
- **Tests.** 34 unit tests in the crate (hitscan zones and walls, gun spread and recoil, health and armour, grenade bounce and fuse, rocket impact, blast falloff and cover, routes, bot brain, tuning and parameters, determinism) plus the doc example; sandbox `bot_rounds_the_wall_and_kills_the_target`, `lap_restarts` and `live_params_reach_the_fight`.

## ChimpDuty intake: render limits (2026-10-22)

Chris chose "Everything now" for the five limits in ChimpDuty's handover, so all five are in the engine, each behind its own opt-in feature. A game that turns none on builds byte for byte what it built before: the eight ship builds match the join-3 baseline file by file (SHA-1 of every `.js` and `_bg.wasm`). Notes, proofs and numbers: `docs/CHIMPDUTY_INTAKE.md`.

- **1a. Camera range (`camera-range`).** `render.camera.near` (0.005 to 10 m, default 0.1) and `render.camera.far` (10 to 100000 m, default 1000), read once a frame into a per-thread cell so `shaders::view_proj` keeps its signature and culling, GTAO, decals, outline and fog agree on the planes. Chosen over a new argument on `view_proj` (every caller changes) and over a world resource (not reachable from the free function). Reversed 32-bit float depth keeps the precision at a 10x closer near plane.
- **1b. First-person models (`view-model-models`).** `Models::draw_view_model(_with)` takes an instance in camera space and draws it in the existing `view_model` pass with the model pipeline. Finest level, no shadow, no skinning. Chosen over a second model API with its own pass setup.
- **2. Look effects (`look-effects` in evani-fx).** A look may request an effect on (`request_effect`, `request_effect_from`). **The rule:** a request only counts for an effect compiled into the game and on tiers with at least 9 points (VR, Medium, High, Ultra; Low and Lite refuse unless the request asks lower); a veto from the same look always wins; the tier budget, VR comfort rule and adaptive quality still decide last, so a look can want an effect on but never force it. The requests live in a world resource made by the first request, not in `LookData` or a tier file, so a game whose look never asks carries none of it. Chosen over letting a look edit the tier's `effects_on` (it would override budgets).
- **3. HDR colour and fog colour (`hdr-color`, `fog-color`).** `render.clear_color` goes up to 64 (linear) and `render.fog.own_color` / `render.fog.color` give the fog a colour of its own; with it off the fog fades to the background as before. The shaders already took the fog colour as a uniform, so only the value changed.
- **4. Pre-warm (`prewarm`).** The loading state is held until the kit material code is built, no pipeline build has been in flight for 3 frames (at least 8 frames) and the GPU queue has confirmed "submitted work done", capped at 6 s. Measured on the sandbox warm scene: held 123 to 139 ms, longest frame gap in the first 4 s 250 ms before and 115 ms after (noisy PC). Chosen over compiling every pipeline at startup in a list (a list per game) and over a fixed delay.
- **5. PCSS speckle (`pcss-stable`).** New `shadows_pcss_stable.wgsl` (existing WGSL untouched): taps compared with the receiver plane at their own centre, golden-angle tap pattern instead of a per-texel hash. The first version took one texel of slope as its margin, which is over 30 cm at a 14 degree sun and removed real shadows; the shipped one blends each tap's four texels by hand with a 0.2 texel margin and a cap of 16 (test `the_margin_stays_small_at_a_low_sun`).
- **Known failures that were there before this work** (confirmed on the untouched main code): the shader tests `engine_shaders_validate_with_every_body_set` and `passes_use_the_required_slots` fail when `evani-render` is tested alone with `--features pcss` (the unknown-slot error names `pcss_params`).
- **Cost of all five together:** +12,099 bytes raw, +4,922 gzip in the sandbox wasm; 0 for a game that uses none.

## ChimpDuty materials, sky and daylight intake (2026-10-21)

- **What came in.** Chris chose "Everything now" (2026-10-21) for ChimpDuty's HANDOVER sections 10 and 12: the opt-in crate `evani-material-proc` (procedural box surfaces painted through evani-render's kit-material hook, ambient occlusion baked on the CPU at load, 61 live `material.*` params; the start of the engine's shared materials library), its `sky` feature (a Preetham dome with clouds ported from three.js `Sky.js`, MIT, credited in the WGSL and THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md; the sun follows `render.light.direction` by default; 14 `sky.*` params), photoreal's `daylight` preset (`evani_look_photoreal::daylight::DaylightLook`) and a test of the ACES fit evani-core's `tonemap` slot already uses. Full list, API and what ChimpDuty changes: docs/CHIMPDUTY_INTAKE.md.
- **Opt-in crate, not a module flag.** The crate is listed in `tools/evani-guide` `OPTIONAL_CRATES`, so `cargo xtask check` refuses a game that carries it as a plain dependency; a game lists it as an optional dependency behind one of its own features. Only then do evani-render's `forward` and `kit-material` turn on.
- **The sky is a feature of that crate.** Without `sky` the crate and its composed shader carry no sky code (a test reads the composed WGSL).
- **The daylight preset has no feature, on purpose.** Under cargo 1.98, declaring any `[features]` table on a crate changes its `-C metadata`, and so the bytes of every game that links it, with the feature off (proven with a throwaway probe project; the first try put the preset behind a photoreal feature and tactical-fps's ship wasm changed with the same size, photoreal rlib metadata f0fc6522 to fe207504). So photoreal's Cargo.toml is unchanged, `pub mod daylight` is always compiled, and a game opts in by naming `DaylightLook`; a game that doesn't links none of it. Rule for later intakes: adding a feature to any crate a shipped game links is a byte change for that game, even unused.
- **The look never depends on the materials crate.** Its sky-matched colours (clear colour at the dome's horizon, ambient tints) are constants; `crates/evani-material-proc/tests/daylight_match.rs` checks them against the sky.
- **Catalog fit.** The catalog describes photoreal as desaturated, so the preset grades through photoreal's own filmic stage and grade with saturation 0.88 rather than ChimpDuty's fuller colour. The catalog has no sky or daylight entries and no `daylight` variant of photoreal: a gap for Chris, nothing contradicted.
- **No roughness param.** The forward mesh pass takes a colour from the kit-material hook and the roughness from the look slot, so the materials register no `roughness` (engine gap, recorded in the intake doc).
- **ACES.** Already the default `tonemap` body (Narkowicz 2015, CC0); only a test was added (`aces_tests` in `crates/evani-core/src/slots.rs`), credit in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md. ChimpDuty's own tonemap body was not brought in.
- **No new dependency.** The crate's WGSL tests use naga through the existing `=30.0.1` pin (dev-dependency only, as in evani-render, evani-fx and the looks).
- **Bytes.** Ship builds with EVANI_GIT_COMMIT pinned: match-3, sandbox, base-building-rts, kart-racer and tactical-fps ship byte for byte as before (SHA-1 of every wasm and js file, against the join build). Cost when used (sandbox trunk release builds, wasm, raw / gzip -9): sandbox default 1,292,472 / 552,968 bytes; `material-proof` without `sky` (the materials crate, the kit-material hook, photoreal with its daylight preset and the proof yard together) 1,372,162 / 582,416 (+29,448 gzip); with `sky` 1,389,008 / 589,553 (+7,137 for the sky, +36,585 in all). JS glue unchanged. Table: docs/CHIMPDUTY_INTAKE.md "Cost".

## `evani-destruct`: Hexagon's destruction as an opt-in module (Chris's request, 2026-10-21)

- **Why now.** Chris asked for it himself ("we have a pretty good destructive engine in Hexagon, follow the same mechanics and rebuild it for Evani.dev"), which overrides the stage limits for this work. It would normally belong to **Stage 5 (Prove the range)**, where Hexagon is kit 006's demo (docs/HEXAGON_PLAN.md); the shader cut-outs, cracks and metal dents stay there (docs/DESTRUCTION.md section 2). Nothing in the Stage 7 gate depends on it, and no kit game uses it.
- **What.** A new crate `crates/evani-destruct`, written fresh in Evani's style from Hexagon's rules (hole spheres, 27-sample cell mass, footprint support and tipping, floor islands and slivers, landing craters, pooled debris and dust). No third-party dependencies. Spec and port table: docs/DESTRUCTION.md. Live params `destruct.*` (25, Hexagon's values as defaults).
- **Opt-in.** A game turns on its own `destruct` feature (optional dependency, same name). The sandbox has `destruct` and `destruct-proof` (the proof scene), hooked at the ends of existing lines behind `cfg`. With `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971` the ship builds of match-3, sandbox, base-building-rts and kart-racer are byte for byte the same as before (wasm and js sha1 equal to the join3 reference, sandbox wasm `4c1581e5…`, match-3 wasm `a7078a25…`).
- **Cost when on.** Sandbox with its ship features plus `destruct-proof` (release, `--no-default-features`, without the ship path-stripping flags): wasm 712,388 bytes, 313,472 gzip, against 648,143 / 286,497 without it, so about +26.3 KB gzip for the module and the proof scene; js 16,761 gzip (+2 bytes). Simulation cost of the proof's 16 s loop, native release: 16 µs per 60 Hz tick on average, worst tick 135 µs (test `cost_per_tick`). Debris and dust live in fixed pools made once (`Debris::new(max, dust_max)`); stepping allocates nothing for them.
- **Determinism.** Fixed 60 Hz tick, caller's `Pcg32`, no clocks; the proof replays its loop from the start when time goes back or any `destruct.*` param changes (it compares the whole `Tuning`), so a live change shows at once and the same moment always looks the same (test `replaying_gives_the_same_moment`).

## Layered materials: cloth sheen and weave, clear coat, brushed metal, iridescence (`material-layers`, 2026-10-22)

- **What.** A new crate `crates/evani-material-layers` (glTF `KHR_materials_sheen`, `_clearcoat`, `_anisotropy` and `_iridescence` factors into a 64-byte layer record, an in-code 64x64 weave and knit map, live strengths, the tier plan and the layer lobes as WGSL) and the evani-render feature `material-layers = ["look-surface", "dep:evani-material-layers"]` with its own fragment entry `model_layered_fs` (`shaders/model_lit_layers.wgsl`). Layer order follows three.js: base, then sheen, then clear coat. A material with no layer flag draws exactly as `model_lit_fs`. Research: `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-22-skin-eye-hair-cloth-and-playground-material-shader.md` and `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-22-close-up-character-and-playground-material-tools-h.md`; credits (Filament, three.js, glTF-Sample-Viewer, Bevy) in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md.
- **Opt-in.** Off unless a game turns on `evani-render/material-layers`; the crate is in `tools/evani-guide` `OPTIONAL_CRATES`, so the check refuses it as a plain dependency. The render hooks are `cfg` additions at the ends of existing lines; the old 5-part lit shader list excludes the feature and a 7-part list at the end of `shaders.rs` takes over with it on.
- **Binding 11 is a per-frame uniform, not a look slot.** Bindings 9 (weave map) and 10 (layer record, per material) sit beside the lit pass's own; binding 11 is one shared 32-byte uniform written in `prepare` with the live strengths, weave strength and scale and the tier path. Live sliders and the quality option change a buffer, never a pipeline, so dragging them costs no shader rebuild; a slot would have rebuilt the pass on every change.
- **Highlights at the look's brightness.** The layers must not out-shine the look they sit in. `ly_look_spec()` is the look's own `slot_specular` at a fixed reference point (N.H = N.L = N.V = 1, roughness 0.5, dielectric) over the plain GGX value there (0.0509296); the anisotropic lobe is scaled by it. The clear coat uses the same scale, or pi when the look has no specular (a coat is a mirror-like layer even on a matte look); the sheen lobe uses a factor of pi (Charlie D with Neubelt V is defined without the look's lobe).
- **Factors only.** The converter step (`tools/evani-convert/src/layers.rs`, after sockets) writes `extras.evani_layers`, removes the four extensions and names each dropped extension texture in its report, so no game downloads texture bytes nothing draws. Cloth weave and subsurface tint come from `extras.evani_cloth`.
- **Tiers.** `material.layers.quality` (auto, cheap, full; auto follows the tier): Lite and Low cheap (a Kajiya-Kay band mixed into the look's lobe, no thin film, a one-tap weave); Medium and up full (stretched GGX anisotropy with a bent reflection normal, thin-film iridescence, a three-tap weave with relief). Live strengths, each 0 to 2: `material.layers.sheen_strength`, `clearcoat_strength`, `anisotropy_strength`, `iridescence_strength`, `weave_strength`, plus `material.layers.weave_scale` (0.1 to 8).
- **Proof scene.** Sandbox features `layers-before = ["lit-proof"]` and `layers-proof` (adds the render feature and the crate), shown with `?ly`: a round velvet pouf, a red clear-coat ball, a turned-metal disc (tilted 20 degrees past the camera-sun half vector: facing it exactly puts the whole flat disc at the lobe's peak and hides any brushing) and a soap-film ball. The four original models (`apps/sandbox/assets/models/layer-*.glb`, high and low) are streamed only when the scene asks for them; the sandbox's ship wasm and js do not change. The pass is opaque, so the soap-film ball is a dark film-coloured ball, not a see-through bubble.
- **Why a pouf and not a curtain (an engine limit found on the way).** The first velvet model was a pleated curtain. `cargo xtask check --full` (look-parity, `baked_sun_draws_no_smear_on_any_model`) failed it: on Lite and Low the baked sun darkened 18.9% of the curtain that the shadow map lights (Medium and up), and still 10.5% at half the fold depth and a 1.5% smear at 30%. Cause: the bake keeps one cone of open sky per vertex, and a fold's open sky is a tall slot (open up and down along the fold), which no single cone holds, so a high sun reads as blocked in the valleys. The test was not loosened; the proof uses a convex pouf instead (0.0% disagreement on every level and sun). Deep cloth folds, grooves and slots on the cheap tiers need a better bake (two cones, or a slot axis): a gap for a later stage, not built here.
- **Test-only clippy fix.** `crates/evani-render/src/model/variant.rs` (a test line, clippy 1.98 `manual_is_multiple_of`); it is in no download.
- **Bytes.** Ship builds with `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971`: match-3, 2d-platformer, model-view, sandbox, base-building-rts, kart-racer, tactical-fps and top-down-action-adventure ship byte for byte as before (SHA-1 of all 16 wasm and js files equal to the join-5 reference, same base f4751068). Cost when on (sandbox trunk release, ship feature list, `layers-proof` against `layers-before`, so the proof scene is in both): wasm 707,830 to 731,456 bytes raw (+23,626), 310,670 to 317,874 gzip -9 (+7,204); js 106,687 both (gzip +1). The proof models are 11 to 60 KB each per tier and download only with `?ly`.
- **Known failure that was there before this work.** `cargo test -p evani-sandbox --features lit-proof` fails `proof_model_is_drawn_with_its_normal_map` (expects 2 textures, finds 3, because look-surface now lists the metallic-roughness map); it fails the same way without this work and `proof_model.rs` has other uncommitted work in the main checkout, so it was left alone.

## Character materials: wet eyes, soft skin, fur and hair shine (Chris's request, 2026-10-22)

- **Why now.** Chris asked for "the tools that you need for hair, skin, clothing, playgrounds, brick. All of the delicate materials". This is the character part (eyes, skin, hair and fur); clothing, brick and the playground materials are other workers' branches. Research: `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-22-skin-eye-hair-cloth-and-playground-material-shader.md` and `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-22-close-up-character-and-playground-material-tools-h.md` (in the main project folder).
- **What.** A new crate `crates/evani-material-character` (tags, the skin lookup, 37 live params and `shaders/character.wgsl`), switched on only by evani-render's new `char-materials` feature (`look-surface` plus the optional dependency). A glTF material picks its shading with `extras.evani_kind` (`eye`, `skin`, `hair`, `fur`; docs/MODEL_FORMAT.md "Character materials"); untagged materials draw as before. The WGSL is spliced into the lit model fragment stage by `patch_lit` (8 exact-text edits after `fn model_lit_fs(`); if any line it expects is missing it returns `None` and the engine keeps the plain pass rather than draw something half-patched.
- **Skin lookup as a fit, not a texture.** The pre-integrated skin table (Penner and Borshukov 2011, d'Eon and Luebke's six-Gaussian profile) is made in code at load, but the model pipeline has no free texture binding. Adding one would change the bind group layout of every model pipeline in every game. So every 8th row is fitted per channel as a blurred ramp (two numbers) and the 48 numbers ride in the `char_params` slot; the shader blends rows by curvature. Nothing is downloaded.
- **Sample mask, not alpha-to-coverage.** Hair and fur edges are soft through `@builtin(sample_mask)` (a dithered 4-bit mask at MSAA 4, a hashed 1-bit test at 1x). Alpha-to-coverage would need the fragment alpha below 1, and the tonemap reads alpha < 0.999 as its screen-door mark, so soft hair would have been dithered a second time. The MSAA count comes from the pipeline key, not the tier.
- **Quality table in code.** `material.character.quality` -1 follows the tier (Lite 0 flat iris and wrap-lit skin; Low 1; Medium 2 adds the lookup and pores; High and Ultra 3 add wrinkles); 0 to 3 force it. The table is in `params.rs`, not a new tier-file field, because the tier files are compiled into every game and a new field would change every game's bytes.
- **Before and after from one build.** `material.character.enabled` (1 on, 0 off) drops a tagged material back to the plain lit pass live, so the proof's before and after shots come from the same build and the same frame setup.
- **Compiled-in fixture.** The proof head `tests/fixtures/models/char-head.glb` (1,125,896 bytes, our own, written by `char-head.mjs`, byte-identical on re-run) is `include_bytes!`'d by `apps/sandbox/src/proof_character.rs` behind the sandbox's `char-proof` feature, as `proof_skin.rs` does, so the sandbox's assets, its stream.json and its default and ship builds stay as they were. The head has lid shells with an exact almond opening over the eyeballs; the first version, a plain bowl socket, left the eyes looking like balls stuck on. The lids then drew a goggle outline where their shell met the head, so each lid now bends from the eyeball onto the head's own surface across a 7 mm band and lies on it from there out, with normals taken from the lid grid itself; only the natural lid-margin and lower-lid creases remain.
- **Byte proof.** With `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=4b61971` the ship builds of all eight games (match-3, 2d-platformer, model-view, sandbox, base-building-rts, kart-racer, tactical-fps, top-down-action-adventure) are byte for byte the same as the join-5 reference: every wasm and js SHA-1 equal (sandbox wasm 648,143 bytes, match-3 wasm `a7078a25…`).
- **Cost when on.** Sandbox trunk release builds (default features), wasm raw / gzip -9: `lit-proof` 1,328,889 / 566,549 bytes; `lit-proof` plus `evani-render/char-materials` 1,364,858 / 580,179, so +35,969 raw and +13,630 gzip for the crate, its WGSL and the hooks; the JS glue is unchanged (131,062 / 19,379). The proof head (`char-proof`) adds its 1.1 MB fixture on top, in that feature only.
- **Wrinkle spacing.** `material.skin.wrinkle_scale` defaults to 400 lines per UV unit (about 1.6 mm apart on the proof head). The first default, 45, drew 14 mm stripes across one cheek in the face shot; the same shot at quality 2 (no wrinkles) had none, which is how they were traced.
- **Hair layers 10 mm apart.** The baked sun of Lite and Low starts a vertex's rays two grid cells off its surface (64 cells over the model: about 9 mm on the proof head), so it cannot see a layer closer than that. With the outer hair layer 4 mm over the inner one, the inner layer and the base cap under both read sunlit where the shadow map shades them: lit 52.5% baked against 33.1% with a shadow map under the sandbox sun, failing `baked_sun_draws_no_smear_on_any_model` (6 points allowed). The fixture now has hair volume instead (layers 4 and 14 mm off the skin, the outer flaring 2 mm to its tips): 34.8% against 32.8% (sandbox sun) and 38.5% against 37.0% (toon look sun). The check is unchanged. A model with hair cards packed tighter than the bake's cells will read lighter under the hair on Lite and Low; the hair's own outer surface, the part that shows, agrees.

## `evani-material-nature`: water, glass, foliage, grass and thin back-light (2026-10-22)

- **What.** A new opt-in crate `crates/evani-material-nature`: back-lit foliage clusters, shell grass, thin translucency (leaves, ears, paper, lampshades, wax), lamps, water with code-made waves, Fresnel, depth fade and shoreline foam, and thin glass (panes and bottles). Research: `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-22-skin-eye-hair-cloth-and-playground-material-shader.md` and `2026-10-22-close-up-character-and-playground-material-tools-h.md`.
- **One post pass over analytic surfaces, no engine change.** The module pushes a `NatureEffect` into evani-render's `PostEffects` and adds a `nature` pass (order 4, reads `hdr` and depth, writes `hdr`) through the public post-effect interface and a `nature_mat` shader slot. Each surface is an analytic shape (water plane, box or capsule of glass, ellipsoid blob, ground region) met by the pixel's view ray and the depth buffer, five per draw. Chosen over new forward-pass materials, which would have meant editing evani-render's model and kit-material code (other workers' files) and changing every game's bytes. The pass is off until a surface exists.
- **Tier modes.** Lite and Low: `Flat`, a multiply and an additive draw per batch (calm water with its tint, body colour and Fresnel sky; whole glass; no waves, foam or glow). Medium and VR: `Split`, a multiply and an additive draw (everything, no screen copy). High and Ultra with `material.nature.refraction` on: `Refract`, copy `hdr` into the transient `nature.scene`, then one replace draw that bends the copy by the waves and a bottle's curve. Surface caps per tier: 10, 20, 40, 80, 120 (and `material.nature.max_surfaces`).
- **Picking surfaces from glTF**, first match wins: the material's own `extras.evani_kind`; the root `extras.evani.nature` map (material name to kind); then the KHR extensions (`KHR_materials_transmission` with `KHR_materials_ior` near 1.33 is water, other transmission is glass, `KHR_materials_diffuse_transmission` is a thin surface). Boxes come from the `POSITION` accessor bounds through the node's world matrix. The game hides water and glass nodes from the solid draw with `node_bit`. `cargo xtask convert` copies each material's `evani_kind` into the root map, because root extras survive into every tier file and material extras do not.
- **The Leaf kind.** A single leaf (`leaf`, `frond`, `petal`) is its own kind, next to the plain thin patch (paper, a sign) and the thin blob (an ear): it is lit through with the foliage colour instead of the thin-surface colour, so a leaf and the bush it came from glow alike. Ten kinds in all, each with an `evani_kind` name.
- **`sky.match_background`** (default 1): the reflected sky takes the game's background (`render.clear_color`) per channel, packed exactly into one uniform word. The first shots showed grey water under a blue sky because the reflection used the grey `sky.horizon`.
- **`camera-range` kept.** Mirrors evani-fx: with evani-render's per-frame near and far planes on, the pass reads depth with the same planes.
- **Determinism.** The waves run on the fixed-step simulation time; no clocks, no `rand`. `water.rs` mirrors the shader's wave hash on the CPU, and a test pins that the waves depend only on position and sim time.
- **Live params.** 47 under `material.nature.*` (44 table values, `enabled`, `refraction`, `max_surfaces`).
- **Proof.** Sandbox feature `nature-proof` (`apps/sandbox/src/proof_nature.rs`, line-end hooks in `main.rs`): a garden under a low sun with a pool, a window pane, a bottle, two bushes, a grass patch, a leaf, a paper sign, an ear blob and a lampshade. Headless muted captures at all five tiers in `.evani-shot-naturemat/`.
- **Bytes.** All 8 ship games (match-3, 2d-platformer, model-view, sandbox, base-building-rts, kart-racer, tactical-fps, top-down-action-adventure) build to the same wasm and js raw and gzip sizes and SHA-1s as join-5: +0 bytes. The crate is absent unless a game lists it.
- **Cost when on.** Sandbox release build with `nature-proof`: wasm 648583 -> 749617 raw (+101034), 286595 -> 315864 gzip -9 (+29269); js unchanged (106096, gzip 16759 -> 16757).
- **GPU time** of the nature pass at 1600x900 on an RTX 4060 Ti (proof garden, 11 surfaces): lite 0.32 ms (1040x585), low 0.32 ms (1200x675), medium 1.38 ms, high 1.41 ms, ultra 0.79 ms (the last four at 1600x900; the lite and low figures are with only water and glass planned). The grass shell loop is most of it.
- **Known cost, follow-up.** `nature.scene` is a full-size HDR transient allocated on every tier, though only High and Ultra read it. Allocating it lazily needs an engine change in evani-render's `gpu/frame.rs` (targets are allocated from the graph at resize, around lines 595-610); left for a later engine pass so this work changes no evani-render byte.
- **Credits.** Filament's subsurface term and Babylon.js's thin-translucency thickness falloff (both Apache-2.0, maths written fresh) in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md; glTF-Sample-Viewer's Fresnel and GGX and the PCG hash are credited in the WGSL. No new dependencies (`serde_json` and the naga dev-dependency are existing pins).

## Playground surfaces: relief brick, cobble, tiles and planks (2026-10-22)

- **What.** evani-material-proc gains a `relief` feature (`src/relief/`, `shaders/relief*.wgsl`): four extra surfaces (ids 32 to 35: brick, cobble, glazed tiles, wood planks) baked once at load into a height, slope, colour and roughness tile, and lit through a new opt-in evani-render feature `kit-surface` that lets a kit's code hand back a bent normal and a roughness (GGX sun highlight and a faint sky reflection on smooth parts). Layers on top: wetness (darker, glossier), puddles (water filling the low spots of upward faces) and moss (in the joints and on top faces, in patches). 28 live params, `material.relief.*`. Research behind it: `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-22-skin-eye-hair-cloth-and-playground-material-shader.md` and `.vibedeck/research/2026-10-22-close-up-character-and-playground-material-tools-h.md` (in the main work copy). Cloth weave moved to the layered family and is not here.
- **Baked, not computed per pixel.** The patterns (brick courses, a jittered Voronoi for cobbles, tiles, planks) run once on the CPU at load, deterministic and bit-exact on every platform (integer hashes, `+ - * /`, `floor`, `sqrt` only; bakes pinned by hash in the tests). The shader only reads texels from the kit-data buffer. Each tile wraps exactly, so no seams (test).
- **Block layout.** 4 header records, then 4 records per surface, then the texels (two floats each, offsets counted in floats from the block start), appended after the world's own records in `kit_data`. Mip levels down to 4 x 4 stop shimmer at distance; at the default 256 the block is about 175k records of the 262k the buffer allows, leaving room for a large world.
- **Tiers.** `lite` paints each surface's flat mean colour from a 16 x 16 bake; `low` paints the baked colour with the bake capped at 128 and no bent normal; `medium` and up get the full relief. `kit_material` (shadow map, cut-outs) paints relief surfaces flat; only `kit_surface` (forward pass) does the full relief.
- **Free surfaces.** A mesh in the world material that is no box face (the proof's stone ball) takes one relief surface triplanar (`ReliefConfig::free_surface`).
- **Wiring.** The relief system runs after the material draw system and re-sets its kit code whenever the kit code's generation moves (a look change reinstalls the plain code first), so the existing draw system is untouched.
- **Known cost.** With `sky` on, the whole kit_data buffer (about 2.8 MB with the relief) is re-sent at 4 Hz when the sun moves. A partial upload in evani-render would fix it; suggested as a follow-up, not built. GPU: the forward pass costs about 1 to 2 ms more at 1600 x 900 on an RTX 4060 Ti when relief fills the screen (8 texel reads a pixel, 24 on a triplanar mesh; e.g. the wall view 1.8 -> 3.9 ms, `low` still about 2.9 ms; the readings are noisy, other builds ran on the PC at the time). Reading one mip on `low` would cut it; not done.
- **Credits.** GGX, Smith visibility and Schlick follow Filament's `brdf.fs` (Apache-2.0); ideas only (per-brick variation, the joint profile) from arlez80's Godot brick shader (MIT). Both in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md and in the code.
- **Proof.** Sandbox feature `playmat-proof` (`apps/sandbox/src/proof_playmat.rs`, hooked at line ends behind `cfg`, never in `evani.toml` or `default`): a yard with a brick wall and pillar, cobble and tile floors, a plank deck and bench, a tiled plinth and a free stone ball under photoreal's low daylight sun. The address picks the view: `?pm=before|dry|wet|moss|flat&cam=wide|wall|floor|deck`.
- **Bytes.** The relief code costs nothing for a game that does not list it: with the feature off, all eight shipped games (match-3, 2d-platformer, model-view, sandbox, base-building-rts, kart-racer, tactical-fps, top-down-action-adventure) build to the same wasm and js, byte for byte, as before (`build --ship`, SHA-1, raw and gzip size; rechecked after the look fixes below). The relief's own cost, measured on the proof page as two trunk release builds of the sandbox that differ only in whether `relief::install` is called: wasm +34,790 bytes raw (1,394,744 -> 1,429,534) and +14,032 bytes gzipped (591,484 -> 605,516), kit-surface hook included. The proof scene and the photoreal look it pulls in are separate: the whole `playmat-proof` page is 135,394 bytes raw, about 51.9 KB gzipped, over the plain sandbox page (a trunk release build, not a ship build). The proof is dev-only and never in `evani.toml` or default features. The debug views (`playmat-debug`, `relief-debug`) are compiled out of `playmat-proof`.
- **Look fixes after the first proof (round 2).** Three defects, each traced with the new debug views (`?dbg=1` puddle mask, `2` moss mask, `3` roughness; feature `relief-debug`, proof only).
  - *Puddles invisible.* The puddle mask was fine; the water level never rose above the stone tops over most of the ground (it needed noise near the top of its range), so puddles were mostly joint water, and where water did cover the ground it was lit as a pale flat patch: the sky reflection was a flat 4 per cent of the fog colour. Fix: the puddle field is now a coverage (`puddles` is the share of ground that holds water), the level starts above the joints and climbs past the stone tops, the water body is dark with a faint blue-green, and `kit_spec` reflects a horizon-to-zenith sky along the reflected view ray with a higher reflectance for rough < 0.35 (water and glaze). A soaked rim darkens the stone just above the water line.
  - *Tiles flat.* The lobe was sized for a point sun, so a glaze at roughness 0.2 almost never caught it, and the sky term was the same 4 per cent. Fix: the sun lobe is widened for the sun's disc, the glaze reflects the sky, each tile's glaze roughness differs a little (sheen varies tile to tile), and the grout is 9 mm with a 9 mm bevel (was 5 mm and 2.5 mm), so joints and edges read. Proof camera `cam=glint` looks low over the tiles towards the sun.
  - *Moss blotchy on tile faces.* Moss followed noise plus a small cavity term, so noise alone could moss a face. Fix: moss follows the baked cavity (joints, cracks) first and creeps onto faces only when overgrown; the noise only breaks it into patches.
  - The tile bake changed, so its pinned hash was updated (the other three bakes are unchanged).

## Join 6: the four material families together (2026-10-22)

- **What.** Branch `join-6-materials` from join-5 (f4751068): layered-materials, char-materials, nature-materials and playground-materials, merged in that order with `--no-ff`. Every conflict was two families adding beside each other (workspace members, evani-render features, sandbox proof features and line-end hooks, `OPTIONAL_CRATES`, the converter steps, these notes); each kept both sides. The playground's "Look fixes" bullet, which its branch had put inside the collision section, now sits in its own section above.
- **Characters and layers on the same lit pass: the rule.** With both `char-materials` and `material-layers` on, the model pipelines draw `model_layered_fs`, so the character patch goes into that stage (`patch_layered`, the same eight edits plus one); `model_lit_fs` stays in the module unpatched and unused. Each material gets exactly one path: a material with a character kind (`extras.evani_kind` skin, eye, hair or fur: `surface.kind.z` above 0.5) takes the character shading and its layer record is ignored (its flags read as 0), so it draws exactly as with `char-materials` alone; every other material keeps its layers (sheen, clear coat, anisotropy, iridescence, weave) and draws exactly as with `material-layers` alone. Layering a coat or sheen over a character kind is not supported; a character model puts cloth, buttons or metal on their own untagged materials. Test: `crates/evani-render/tests/char_layers_wgsl.rs` (naga, both shadow tails, skinned or not, 1x and 4x, every character quality, and the shipped forward source) and `the_layered_stage_patches_when_both_are_on` in the character crate.
- **The other pairs do not overlap.** The nature family is a post pass with its own shader slot, no model or kit pipeline change. The playground relief lights through `kit-surface`, which patches only `fs_main` (the kit mesh pass) after the kit-material splice; the model families patch only the model stages. evani-render's own `relief` feature (the S3-10 model relief) still switches off the lit pass and with it both model families.
- **Relief with a model family on.** `material-layers` and `char-materials` turn on `look-surface`, which swaps the forward source for the lit one: the plain forward source (its `fs_main` untouched) with the model stages appended. The three kit-surface hooks are in `fs_main` only, once each, so the relief splice patches the lit source the same way; `kit_surface.rs`'s tests now declare the lit (and relief) look slots, so they run under every combination.
- **Sandbox proofs together.** With several proof features on (the all-four size build) the proof system registered last holds the frame: playmat, then nature, then char, then layers (playmat sets the camera, nature the sun). A proof's test checks that it holds the camera or sun only when no later proof is on; everything else it checks (loads, draws, no errors, params) runs in every build. Each proof alone is checked in full, as before.
- **Join 6: material sets joined to m1-foundation (approved by Chris 2026-10-23).** m1-foundation had no commits beyond join-5 (f4751068), so it was fast-forwarded to `join-6-materials` (28f06d97). The main folder's uncommitted sandbox and model-view work (other work, not part of the join) was stashed for the four overlapping sandbox files, then re-applied cleanly. On the joined main line, `cargo xtask check` is clean, and `cargo test -p evani-sandbox --features layers-proof,char-proof,nature-proof,playmat-proof` passes (24 tests). The material viewer branches were not merged.
- **Soft-shadow test fix (2026-10-24, branch `pcss-test-fix`).** With the `pcss` feature on, two evani-render shader tests failed on m1-foundation (`passes_use_the_required_slots`, `engine_shaders_validate_with_every_body_set`): the forward shader gains a `pcss_params` slot with `pcss`, but the tests neither declared it nor expected it in the slot list. Test-only fix (expectation updated, nothing loosened). Guard: `cargo xtask check --full` now also runs the evani-render lib tests with `pcss` and with `pcss-stable` (two new `LOOK_PARITY_TESTS` rows). Sandbox ship output unchanged (same SHA-1 for `.js` and `_bg.wasm` with and without the fix).
- **Join 7: ChimpDuty render limits and the soft-shadow test fix (approved by Chris 2026-10-24).** Branch `join-7` from m1-foundation (65e84978): `pcss-test-fix` (741625e3) merged with `--no-ff`, then `intake-render-limits` (1f9552dc). The five render limits themselves (acfa2efb) were already on m1-foundation through the earlier join merge 73526651, so the only new commit on `intake-render-limits` was 1f9552dc, a cherry-pick of the same soft-shadow test fix. Code files merged without a clash (the same edit on both sides). One clash, here in DECISIONS.md: both branches added the same "Soft-shadow test fix" line at different places; kept one copy, after the Join 6 section. Found in the join checks: the integration test `model_wgsl` (`wgsl_validates_with_and_without_models`) failed with `pcss` or `pcss-stable` on, already on m1-foundation, for the same reason as the two lib tests (the shipped forward source calls `pcss_params`, which the test did not declare). Test-only fix (the test declares the slot under `pcss`, a copy of the private `shadow_ext::PCSS_PARAMS` signature); guard: two more `LOOK_PARITY_TESTS` rows run `model_wgsl` with `models,pcss` and `models,pcss-stable` in `check --full`. Also an unused import (`ParamValue`) in `kits/evani-kit-2d-platformer/src/draw.rs`, already on m1-foundation, failed `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`; removed. Checks on join-7: evani-render tests default, `pcss`, `pcss-stable`, each ChimpDuty feature alone and all together; evani-fx with and without `look-effects`; the character, layers, nature and proc material crates (all features); sandbox default, `render-limits-proof` and the four material proofs; clippy `-D warnings` native (workspace, and the features on) and wasm (sandbox default and `render-limits-proof`, evani-render with the features on); `cargo xtask check` and `--full` clean. Byte proof: the 16 shipped files of the eight games (rustc 1.99.1, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=65e8497`) have the same SHA-1 as the 65e84978 baseline, 16 of 16.


## Multiplayer: lockstep with a referee, Sugarpeak online, ChimpVibe bridge (2026-10-24)

- **Model: deterministic input-only lockstep with a referee, and prediction (Chris's pick, relayed by the coordinator 2026-10-24).** It replaces the plan's "keep the snapshot core" recommendation (docs/NET_BUILD_PLAN.md 2.1) for Sugarpeak. Players send only inputs (3 bytes a tick: steer, throttle, buttons). The referee (the host's server, running the same rules headless) puts every seat's input for a tick into one frame (a late input repeats the seat's last one, with one-shot presses dropped; empty seats get the referee's own AI), steps, and relays the confirmed frames; every peer replays them bit for bit and the referee's run decides the results. Each player predicts ahead from its confirmed copy with its own inputs (others repeat their last), re-running the prediction whenever new frames confirm (a rollback, GGRS-style), so the local kart reacts at once. Every 30 ticks the referee sends a 32-bit state hash; a mismatch, a gap or a fresh join gets the full state in the next view. Input delay is a setting (`ClientConfig::input_delay`, default 0: prediction makes it unneeded for feel). Code: `crates/evani-net/src/lockstep.rs` (feature `lockstep`).
  - Why it fits: down-bandwidth is inputs, not states (measured in HANDOFF.md); results come from the referee; AI racers run only on the referee, their inputs in the same stream (ChimpVibe points 7 and 8).
  - The existing snapshot core (`Server`, `Client`) is untouched and still used by Hexagon; the full state is sent only for joins, repair and spectators (below).
  - **Spectators**: each spectator view is a state from a few ticks back plus every frame since, so it is complete on its own (ChimpVibe's rule: a spectator who skips views or switches who it follows is one view from a right picture). Spectators play the frames from a small buffer and send nothing.
  - Ideas, not code: Gaffer on Games, "Deterministic Lockstep" (https://gafferongames.com/post/deterministic_lockstep/; an article: redundant inputs with acks, a playout buffer, periodic hashes); GGRS (https://github.com/gschup/ggrs, MIT OR Apache-2.0: the save/load/advance shape, the sync test `lockstep::sync_test`, spectators, input prediction). No code was copied and no crate is a dependency; were GGRS code ever copied, its copyright notice and source must go with it.
- **`evani-referee/1`**: the neutral headless referee ABI (`crates/evani-net/src/referee.rs`, feature `referee`, the `export_referee!` macro). A game's referee crate allows `unsafe_code` for the `#[unsafe(no_mangle)]` export attributes only; host bytes are read from the module's own scratch buffer by index, never through a raw pointer. Sugarpeak's: `apps/kart-racer/referee` (README there).
- **`--cfg evani_headless`** (evani-core): leaves the browser glue (wasm-bindgen, web-sys, the page clock and storage) out of a wasm build, so a referee wasm imports nothing. Set only by referee builds (`bridges/chimpvibe/build.mjs`); every normal build is unchanged (the byte proof below). Declared through evani-core's build script (`cargo::rustc-check-cfg`).
- **Kit split**: `evani-kit-kart-racer` has a default `render` feature (drawing, sound, HUD, the plugin); off, it is the race rules alone (race, karts, AI, items, track, `trackgeo` wall geometry, `finish` timings). The shipped game turns it on, so it is unchanged; `tests/replay_proof.rs` replays three scripted single-player races and matches the hashes recorded on the code before the split. Online hooks in `race.rs` (`Race::online`, `Race::seed`, `Race::view_karts`) are `None`/the old seed offline.
- **`evani-net/1` port** (`crates/evani-net/src/port.rs`, `web/port.js`, feature `port`, wasm): bytes over one MessagePort. A parent frame's `hello` is accepted only when `event.source` is the real parent (never the page itself); an in-page adapter registers explicitly with `globalThis.evaniNet.attachPort(port, {role, follow})` (or queues `[port, opts]` on `globalThis.evaniNetPorts` before the game loads). `HostPort` is a plain byte pipe with `send` (datagram) and `send_reliable`, so a game without the netcode (Broadside's JSON rules) can use it; it also implements `Transport`.
- **New features are opt-in and off for everyone else**: evani-net `lockstep`, `referee`, `port`; the kart racer app's `net` turns on the first and the last. Sandbox and Hexagon (net = true) take none of them.
- **ChimpVibe bridge outside the engine** (Chris: "We write the adapter"; "ChimpVibe-specific code inside our engine is off-limits"): `bridges/chimpvibe/` is its own Cargo workspace (the root workspace excludes `bridges/`); no engine crate depends on it and `grep -ri chimpvibe crates/ tools/` stays empty. `rules-shim/` wraps the referee in `chimpvibe-rules/1`; `play-adapter.js` joins their renderer contract to `evani-net/1`; `test/` holds a Node harness and a fake shell page (test only).
- **Sugarpeak `net = true` on branch `sugarpeak-mp`** for the hand-over build; with no host the game is the single-player game. Whether the Evani.app build turns it on is Chris's call when this branch joins.
- No new dependencies (base64 is written out in the bridge).
- **Byte proof (2026-10-25)**: ship builds of the seven other games against the 65e84978 baseline (`.evani-tmp-matcat/sha-base.txt`, same rustc 1.99.1, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=65e8497`; script `.evani/tmp/sugarpeak-mp/ship.sh`). The first run found match-3's wasm one byte different: the `Pcg32` checkpoint methods had been added above `below()`, which moved a panic location's line number by 11 (match-3 is the only game that keeps that location). They now sit in their own `impl` after every other method, and the rebuilt match-3 is identical. Lesson for every engine change that must be byte-neutral: add code below existing code in a file (or in a new file), never above a line that can panic.

## Netcode tests and the sync-test guard (N1 of docs/NET_BUILD_PLAN.md, 2026-10-24)

- **Model and prior art (Chris, 2026-10-24, relayed by the coordinator; recorded here as asked).** Two picks from the multiplayer research report (`.vibedeck/research/2026-10-24-multiplayer-net-module-for-evani-dev.md`): (1) deterministic, input-only play with a thin relay that also referees by running the same simulation headless, ideas from Gaffer on Games, "Deterministic Lockstep" (https://gafferongames.com/post/deterministic_lockstep/; an article: ideas only, our own code); (2) GGRS (https://github.com/gschup/ggrs, MIT OR Apache-2.0) as the design for a later rollback layer: approach only; any code taken from it must keep its copyright notice and licence text with attribution. Nothing is copied and no crate is added. The built core already is that referee (clients send inputs only, the server steps the real simulation; docs/NET_BUILD_PLAN.md 2.1 keeps its snapshots); these tests hold it to the lockstep standard of bit-for-bit agreement.
- **`evani_net::synctest` (errors become tools).** GGRS's sync-test idea, our own code: `SyncTest::new(&game, seed, ticks).run(&initial, inputs)` steps a reference copy, a second independent copy (`Check::Repeat`: clocks, globals, counters in the game value, map order), a copy encoded and decoded before every step as a snapshot is (`Check::WireRoundTrip`: a field the `Wire` impl forgets or reads back differently), and every tick replays the last `check_distance` ticks (8 by default) from a saved state with `Role::Resim` (`Check::Rollback`: state changed only on a first run, hidden state a rollback cannot rewind). The first difference returns a `Divergence` with the tick, check, both hashes, the first differing byte of the encodings and, with `.parts(...)`, the named part of the state, plus a `fix:` line. `first_divergence` compares two per-tick hash lists (replay proofs, K1). Any game's tests can call it; Sugarpeak's step (K2) must pass it. Lag compensation is not exercised there (empty pose history).
- **Tests (`crates/evani-net/tests/net/`, one binary).** A toy arena `NetGame` (movement with drag and wall bounces, pushes, seeded pickups, fire presses with lag-compensated rewind, joins and leaves) and a harness: one referee, any number of clients and bots on one `Loopback`, a spy transport that decodes every snapshot a client receives, and per-tick hashes of the referee and of every client's prediction. 49 tests plus 2 unit tests: wire round trips at the limits, exact float bits, range and length refusals, 20,000 garbage and mutated packets through every decoder, pinned FNV-1a vectors, the pinned arena state hash and protocol hash; referee determinism (same seed, same run, stats included), bots, joins and leaves seen once, no id reuse within a tick, full and wrong-protocol refusals, timeouts and closed links; hostile input: garbage and wrong kinds counted and dropped, out-of-range values refused or clamped by the step, inputs too far ahead or in the past ignored, first copy of a tick wins, a 500x flood changes nothing, strangers ignored, a lying view time clamped to the lag window; loopback: 2 clients on a perfect link, **8 clients plus 4 bots at 100 ms latency, 30 ms jitter, 5% loss each way with a late joiner**: every one of 2,314 snapshots equals the referee's state and 1,820 settled predicted ticks equal the referee bit for bit (1,168 rollbacks on the way), a whole lossy session is repeatable, a lone player is never rolled back after pacing settles, pacing settles the slack at the target with no late input afterwards, redundant inputs at 20% loss leave 7.6% of input ticks repeated, recovery from short and long outages, `LocalSession` with bots; sync tests on the arena (2,000 ticks with joins and leaves) and on five games broken on purpose, each caught by the right check at the right tick.
- **Float determinism (native x86_64 vs wasm32).** `tests/net/float_probe.rs` compiles a dependency-free float kernel (`float_kernel.rs`) to wasm32 with plain `rustc` (no imports) and runs it in Node, 20,000 inputs per family. Same bits: + - * /, sqrt, mul_add, min/max/abs/copysign, floor/ceil/trunc/round, int-float casts, and a kart-like integration step built from them. Different bits: sin/cos/tan, asin/acos/atan/atan2, exp/ln/log2/exp2, powf/powi/cbrt/hypot, and NaN payloads. So the shipped referee must be the wasm build (plan 2.8: browsers and the referee then run the same compiled-in maths library); a native referee or native tool reproduces a browser's run only if the game's step uses IEEE-exact operations; and a step must never let a NaN into its state. Wasm-to-wasm across browsers and devices is K3's job (needs browsers and the leased devices).
- **Bug found and fixed.** `wire::quantize` at 24 bits returned 2^24 for the top of the range (`t * steps + 0.5` rounds up in f32), one past the 24-bit code; now clamped to the top code. No game calls it yet.
- **Wired into the gate.** `cargo xtask check --full` runs `cargo test -p evani-net` once per project (`NET_TESTS` in tools/evani-guide/src/check.rs), whether or not a game lists `net`.
- **Not testable yet (later packages).** Input delay (`net.input_delay_ticks`), the catch-up cap and per-link rate caps (N2a/N2b): the core has none, so a flood is harmless but not counted; no `sanitize` hook (the step clamps); a partly corrupt input packet keeps the frames decoded before the bad byte (they are well-formed inputs of that client's own player; N2a may make a packet all-or-nothing).
- **Byte proof.** All 8 games built `--ship` on this branch (rustc 1.99.1, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=65e8497`, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1`) against the m1-foundation 65e84978 baseline (net-engine branches from it, so the baseline stands): all 16 files (js and wasm of 2d-platformer, base-building-rts, kart-racer, match-3, model-view, sandbox, tactical-fps, top-down-action-adventure) have the same SHA-1, size and gzip size. The two games that ship `net` (sandbox, tactical-fps) are unchanged too: `synctest` is generic and never reaches a download, and no game calls `quantize`. The `net` module's own cost in the sandbox (`cargo xtask build sandbox --ship` with and without `net`) was not measured here: the PC had no spare cores when this package ended. It moves to the next package that builds the sandbox (N2a, or P1 at the latest).

## Multiplayer hosts: we write the ChimpVibe bridge, outside the engine, and ship it with Sugarpeak Racer (Chris, 2026-10-24)

- **Decision.** Chris chose "We write the adapter", then: "Let's just write the bridge and then we'll hand them over the full game with the bridge in it and tell them to make it work." We build and maintain a small ChimpVibe-only bridge in `bridges/chimpvibe/` (package C1 of docs/NET_BUILD_PLAN.md). It translates between the engine's neutral contracts (`evani-net/1` in the browser, `evani-referee/1` on the server) and ChimpVibe's `chimpvibe-rules/1` rules ABI and their shell messaging.
- **Where it ships.** Inside the Sugarpeak Racer hand-off package for ChimpVibe (`game/rules/rules.wasm` with the bridge linked in, `bridge/play-adapter.js`, rules source, `HANDOFF.md`). Never in the game folder shipped to Evani.app or any other portal, never in `crates/`; no engine crate or other game depends on it; `grep -ri chimpvibe crates/ tools/xtask/` stays empty.
- **Their side.** ChimpVibe makes it work in their play frame and server glue, following docs/NET_BRIDGE_GUIDE.md Part C, and changes nothing inside the engine or the game; change requests come back to us and go into the engine for every host.
- **Why.** Chris's rule that the engine stays platform-neutral (partner code lives outside it) and that compatibility with every other host is never broken; writing the bridge ourselves lets us hand over a complete, tested game.
- **Guide.** docs/NET_BRIDGE_GUIDE.md: plain summary, Part B for any host, Part C for ChimpVibe. First draft B0 2026-10-24; finalised in B1 against the built code.

## Frame order: the picture is drawn after every module's frame step (2026-10-25)

- **The bug (reported by Broadside3D, measured).** `App::frame` ran module frame steps in the order the game added them, and the render module draws the whole picture inside its own `frame`. It has to be added first (fx, nature and the rest add passes to its graph when they are built), so everything a later module set in its frame step reached the GPU one frame late: the nature pass's copy of `RenderWorld::camera` and the time (`evani-material-nature` lib.rs `frame`), fx replans and effect switches, fog's slot body, a module's own draws. Kit `Stage::Frame` systems were never affected (they run before every module). Broadside saw it as sea, waterline and foam flicker in a zoomed-out free-look drag (1,206,892 pixels over 8 stepped frames) and worked round it game-side by splitting the render module (`draw_last.rs`, commit 74245d8 there). Confirmed here by a failing test first: a cube pushed by a module added after render on frame 3 was drawn on frame 4 (`[0, 0, 0, 1, 0]`).
- **The fix: `EngineModule::draws`** (default `false`; `RenderModule` returns `true`). `App::frame` runs every other module's `frame` in add order, then the drawing modules' in theirs. Chosen over a new `draw`/`record` hook: no module or game changes, the render module's own update work (importance driver, particle staging) still sees every other module's output, and a game-side split like Broadside's keeps working unchanged (its wrappers don't forward `draws`, so both stay `false` and its own order holds). Build, start, `visibility_changed`, the overlay and the version line keep the add order. The fixed 60 Hz step, the systems and the sim are untouched; only when the draw happens within the frame moves. Cost: 114 to 137 bytes of wasm per game (below).
- **Live tools.** A capture live queues in its frame step is now taken by the renderer the same frame, drawn with every value of that frame (the overrides live set `CAPTURE_DELAY_FRAMES` earlier are already in force). `param.set`, `sweep.run` and `compare.ab` values reach the picture the frame they are applied; `HoldCanvas` moves with them, so a trial is never shown. Telemetry samples (taken in live's frame step) carry the previous draw's stats; applying and drawing moved together, so the first sample showing a new value is the same as before. docs/LIVE_API.md has the note.
- **Guards (errors become tools).** `drawing_modules_run_after_every_other_frame_step` and `update_modules_keep_their_order` (evani-core `app.rs`: a drawing probe added first must see the value a later module wrote that frame, and run last); `a_later_modules_draws_land_in_the_same_frame` (evani-render `frame_order_tests.rs`, the test that failed before the fix); `the_ocean_is_drawn_with_this_frames_camera` (evani-material-nature: camera dragged 40 m a frame, the nature pass's copy must equal the game camera at draw time). All run in `cargo test --workspace`, so in `cargo xtask check --full`.
- **Visual proof** (`.evani/shots/frameorder/`, scripts in `.evani/tmp/frameorder/`): sandbox feature `nature-orbit` (new, proof only, never in evani.toml: the water garden's camera circles the pool at 3 rad/s of sim time), release web builds of the base (b56f3fae plus only that proof switch) and of this branch, headless muted Chrome, every frame driven by the script with fixed timestamps from the first, text overlays hidden. 8 pairs of a frame stepped forward (camera moves) and the same timestamp again (the same view at rest): before 2,247,540 pixels differ (1,026,157 by more than 30), on the nature surfaces (`base-lag-heat.png`); after 0. The at-rest frames of the two builds are pixel-identical in 8 of 8 pairs (a still camera looks exactly as before).
- **Sizes** (`cargo xtask build <game> --ship`, rustc 1.99.1, `EVANI_GIT_COMMIT=65e8497`, against Join 8's `sha-after.txt` at b56f3fae): wasm raw +124 (2d-platformer), +128 (base-building-rts), +132 (kart-racer), +114 (match-3), +114 (model-view), +132 (sandbox), +137 (tactical-fps), +121 (top-down-action-adventure) bytes; gzip -31 to +152 bytes; every js the same raw size (gzip within 2 bytes). Not byte-identical, as expected for a core loop change.
- **For Broadside3D:** drop `app/src/draw_last.rs` and add `RenderModule::new()` first as every other game does; nothing else changes. Keeping the split also still works.

## Contributions from Broadside 3D (2026-10-25, joined in Join 10)

- **Shadow fit (`shadow-fit`, branch `bs/shadow-fit`).** For cameras 100 m or more from what they look at, the plain 26 m shadow box lets casters pop at its edge, and raising `fx.shadows.distance` by hand needs the bias and normal offset retuned with it (one out-of-tree game hand-tuned 140 m, offset 0.2 m, bias 0.0002). An opt-in renderer feature, off by default, so games without it are byte for byte the same (16 of 16 shipped files of the eight games, same SHA-1 as b56f3fae). `fx.shadows.fit = "view"` sizes the box from the camera's reach plus `fx.shadows.view_depth`, in whole `fit_step`s so the texel grid only changes once per step; the size is held with hysteresis (grow at once, shrink only when the wanted size is over half a step below the next step down; the first version had none and a camera wobbling 1 cm at a step edge flipped the box between 136 m and 144 m each frame: test `a_camera_wobbling_at_a_step_edge_keeps_one_box`). In split-screen the reach is measured from the box centre (between the players). `fx.shadows.auto_bias` keeps the offset at 1.5 texels and the bias at 0.5 texel of the far box, using the half-map texels when two cascades share the atlas. Hooks sit on existing lines behind `cfg` (line numbers, hence panic locations, unchanged). Guard: `cargo xtask check --full` runs the evani-render lib tests with `shadow-fit` (a `LOOK_PARITY_TESTS` row).
